# Aakash Eye Hospital Project Guide

This project is a production-ready, mobile-first website for Aakash Eye Hospital. The goal is to present a clear, premium, trustworthy, and easy-to-use digital experience for a multi-branch eye hospital. Most visitors are expected to use mobile devices, so every implementation decision must protect small-screen usability first.

## Product Direction

- Build a direct, simple, patient-friendly hospital website, not a complex web app.
- Prioritize fast navigation to services, doctors, hospitals, contact numbers, WhatsApp, maps, and appointment booking.
- Keep pages focused and easy to scan. Avoid long marketing sections, unnecessary animations, and complicated interactions.
- Design for a world-class multi-branch eye hospital: professional, clinical, calm, trustworthy, and polished.
- Mobile UI/UX is the primary experience. Desktop must still look premium, but mobile should never feel like an afterthought.
- Content should be clear for patients and families, including older users who may need larger touch targets, readable text, and obvious actions.

## Tech Stack

Runtime:

- React 19
- React DOM 19
- React Router DOM 7
- Vite 8
- Framer Motion for controlled animation
- Lucide React for icons
- React Hook Form for forms
- Zod and `@hookform/resolvers` for validation

Development:

- ESLint 9 with React, hooks, and refresh plugins
- Prettier 3
- A static site for any host: every page is prerendered at build time (see Search and prerendering)

Important commands:

```bash
npm install
npm run dev
npm run lint
npm run build
npm run preview
```

Deployment target:

- Build command: `npm run build` - three steps: `vite build`, `vite build --ssr src/entry-server.jsx`, `node scripts/prerender.mjs`
- Output directory: `dist` - every page as HTML, `404.html`, `sitemap.xml`, `robots.txt`, `_redirects` / `_headers` (Cloudflare, Netlify) and `.htaccess` (Apache, LiteSpeed). The hosting platform is not chosen; `docs/hosting.md` has each platform's steps and the launch checklist
- Public static assets served from `public`
- No environment variables: the appointment page composes a WhatsApp message client-side and posts nothing to a server.

Every file above is reachable from `src/main.jsx` through the import graph (except `src/styles/preloader.css` and `src/styles/fonts.css`, which `vite.config.js` inlines into `index.html`; `src/entry-server.jsx`, the prerender's second entry; and `src/data/redirects.json`, which only `scripts/prerender.mjs` reads), and every file under `public/assets/media` is referenced from a data file, a component or `index.html`. That is checked, not assumed: an import-graph walk found `src/i18n/strings.js`, `src/lib/data.js` (a barrel nobody imported), `src/lib/schemas.js` (zod schemas describing data shapes the site no longer has - `home.hero.slides`, `site.pageSeo`, `vision.values[].icon`) and `src/types/types.js` with no importer, and all four were deleted with their directories. Validation now lives where it is used - `appointmentSchema` in `src/lib/appointmentData.js` is the one zod schema left, and it validates a form, not a data file. **When a file is removed, re-run that walk** rather than trusting the tree above; the tree is a description, not a manifest.

## Architecture Rules

- Pages live in `src/pages` and are loaded through `src/router.jsx` using lazy route modules.
- Reusable page sections live in `src/sections`.
- Shared UI primitives and layout components live in `src/components`.
- Editable content belongs in `src/data` JSON files whenever possible.
- Data access and validation helpers belong in `src/lib`.
- Global styling lives in `src/styles/global.css`; keep changes scoped and avoid unrelated rewrites.
- **Namespaced stylesheets own their own components, and each is imported by what it styles, so a page loads only the CSS it uses** (see Stylesheets per page). `src/main.jsx` imports what every route needs: `global.css`, `system.css`, and the three parts of the layout - the site header's `src/styles/header.css` (`.hd` / `.hd__*`), the footer's `src/styles/footer.css` (`.ft-`) and the cookie consent's `src/styles/cookie.css` (`.ck` / `.ck__*`). Each page imports its own: the landing page `src/styles/landing.css` (`.e-hero`, `.e-impact`, `.e-cond`, `.e-why`, `.e-docs`, `.e-voices`), the two About pages `src/styles/about.css` (`.ab-`), the services index `src/styles/services.css` (`.sv-`), the service detail page `src/styles/service-detail.css` (`.sd-`) **and `services.css`** (its overview and signs tabs are the index's eye model and chart), the doctors page `src/styles/doctors.css` (`.dr` / `.dr-`), one doctor's page `src/styles/doctor.css` (`.dp-`), a hospital's own page `src/styles/branch.css` (`.br-`), the hospitals index `src/styles/hospitals.css` (`.hs-`), the contact page `src/styles/contact.css` (`.ct-`), the appointment page `src/styles/appointment.css` (`.ap-`) and the not-found page `src/styles/not-found.css` (`.nf-`). **A component that more than one page renders imports its own sheet**: `CountUp` imports `count-up.css` (`.e-count`) and `BranchStatus` imports `branch-status.css` (`.br-status`) - their rules lived in `landing.css` and `branch.css`, which the other pages using them no longer load. The opening curtain and the reload veil own `src/styles/preloader.css` (`.pl` / `.pl__*` / `.pl--wait`) and the fonts own `src/styles/fonts.css`; neither is imported - `vite.config.js` inlines both into `index.html`, because they have to paint before any script (see Opening curtain). Never restyle these components from `global.css` or `system.css` - the header in particular used to be styled from three places at once, and one source of truth is what keeps it predictable.
- **Every `.e-link` carries an invisible `::after` halo (`inset: -10px 0`) in `system.css`**, so a 25px text link is a 45px target without the underline moving. It started as a patch on the landing page's `Meet the full team` and moved into the primitive when the service detail page turned up three more (`Visnagar`, `Ask us directly`, `See all 11`) - every `.e-link` on the site is a route, and the fix belongs where the link is defined. Measure with `elementFromPoint`; the element rect cannot see the halo.
- `.e-label` in `system.css` is `--e-ink-2`, not `--e-ink-3`: 0.72rem semibold uppercase needs 4.5:1 and `--e-ink-3` measures 2.9:1 on paper. Every section eyebrow on the site reads that one rule, so the services page no longer carries its own override. Do not lighten it back for looks.
- `src/lib/contact.js` holds the shared phone/WhatsApp/map link helpers. Use it instead of re-declaring `cleanTel`, `getPrimaryPhone`, `buildWhatsApp` or `buildMapLink` in a component. It also owns `BRANCH_STORAGE_KEY`, `storeBranch()`, `readStoredBranchSlug()`, `applyBranchConsent()`, `BRANCH_CHANGE_EVENT` and `getStoredBranch()` - the hospital the header stored for this reader, and the event that tells the rest of the page it changed. Where it is stored follows the cookie choice (`localStorage` only once `experience` is allowed, `sessionStorage` until then - see Cookie consent), so nothing reads the key straight from a storage. `Header`, the service detail page and the contact page's switchboard all read that one key, and `Header` also listens for `BRANCH_CHANGE_EVENT`, so a hospital chosen on the contact page moves the number at the top of the screen at once - a phone number offered in the page can never be a different hospital's from the one at the top of the screen. It also owns `hasBranchPage()` and `buildBranchHref()` - the one place that decides whether a hospital link opens that hospital's own page or the index with it selected - so the header, the drawer, the footer, the impact chips and the branches index can never disagree. They live here rather than in `branchData.js` so the header does not pull the doctors and services data into the root bundle for one link.
- **`src/lib/hours.js` is the one reader of a hospital's OPD hours.** Every hospital carries its own `hours` block in `branches.json` - a `schedule` of slots (a set of `days` with one `opens` and one `closes`), a `closedNote` for the days in no slot, and the `note` printed under the hours on its page - and `site.openingHours` is the network default a hospital without a block inherits, plus the one `timeZone` every hospital's clock runs on. `getBranchHours(branch)` merges the two; `getHoursRows()` derives the printed `Monday to Saturday, 9:00 AM - 6:00 PM` lines from the schedule (two slots on the same days share a row); `getOpenState()` answers the live pill; `toOpeningHoursSpecification()` feeds the JSON-LD; and the appointment flow's open days and its date check read the same block. There are no hand-written display strings any more - `site.businessHours` was the second copy that let the printed `6:00 PM` and the structured `20:00` disagree for a release, and deriving the words from the numbers is what makes that impossible. Anything that shows hours takes the hospital it is describing (`BranchStatus` takes a `branch` prop; the footer and the contact page's foot follow the reader's stored hospital and name it), because a figure for "the OPD" with no hospital attached is somebody else's on five pages out of six.
- Public media should live under `public/assets/media` and be referenced with `/assets/media/...` paths. The hero film lives in `heroes/` alongside the stills.
- Prefer existing helpers such as `SEO`, `JsonLd`, `SmartImage`, `Reveal`, and `LazyMapFrame` before creating new primitives.
- Remove dead components, imports, CSS selectors, and unused assets when a section is removed. This site must stay clean and deployment-ready.
- **Auditing a namespaced stylesheet for dead selectors: match class names as whole tokens, never as substrings.** `jsx.includes("sv-part")` is satisfied by `sv-parts__list` and will report a genuinely dead class as live. Tokenise both sides (`/[A-Za-z0-9_-]+/g`) and compare sets.
- **A script that strips rules must not split a prelude on commas without stripping comments first.** A comment sitting above a rule is part of that rule's prelude, and comment prose contains commas: splitting `/* ... two services, so the answer ... */ .sv-match` on commas keeps the fragment before the comma, drops the half carrying the selector, and leaves an unterminated comment welded to the rule body. It fails as a CSS syntax error several hundred lines later, nowhere near the cause. Strip comments, then split.

## Search and prerendering

Every indexable page is rendered to static HTML at build time, so a search engine, a social preview or a reader without JavaScript gets the whole page and its head in the first response. **`docs/SEO.md` is the handbook** (what is set up, why, the keyword ownership, the local SEO and the open items) and `docs/hosting.md` the per-host setup. The rules a change must keep:

- **Every component must render in Node.** `src/entry-server.jsx` renders each page with `renderToString` through React Router's static handler, from the same `routes` array the browser uses (`src/router.jsx`). Nothing may touch `window`, `document`, `localStorage`, `sessionStorage` or `matchMedia` during render or in a `useState` initialiser without a `typeof window` guard, and every `useSyncExternalStore` needs a `getServerSnapshot` (the desktop shape is the server's answer). The build fails on the first render that throws.
- **React adopts the prerendered page; it does not rebuild it.** `main.jsx` calls `hydrateRoot`, so the elements already on screen become the app's and nothing is painted twice - the first paint stays the page's largest, which is the LCP the old rebuild used to lose (React's copy, a few pixels wider in the web fonts, became a new LCP at about 2s on nearly every page). It adopts only where React's first pass is certain to match the build (`canAdopt()`): the file is this path's (`data-path` on `#root`, written by the prerender - a host answering an unknown path with another page's file gets a fresh build), the browser is the one the build assumes (scroll-driven animation understood - `SCROLL_DRIVEN`, which the prerender assumes true - and motion allowed), and the query string holds nothing a page reads (`utm_*` and click ids are ignored). Everywhere else `createRoot` builds the page fresh over the copy, exactly as before. Either way it waits for the route's own module first (`routeReady()`), under the curtain on a first visit and the reload veil (`#app-wait`) later in the visit.
- **React's first pass must produce exactly the build's markup.** It renders the server's view of the world, and the reader's own values arrive in the pass straight after, before the curtain or the veil lifts. So never read storage, cookies, the clock, `matchMedia`, whether `IntersectionObserver` exists, or the URL's query in render or a `useState` initialiser. Use `src/lib/hydration.js`: `useHydrated()` (false on the server and while adopting, true from the pass after), `useClientState(readClient, serverValue)` (a `useState` that starts at the build's value and reads the reader's once the page is adopted - a component mounted later reads it from the start) and `useCurrentYear()`. `useSyncExternalStore` with a `getServerSnapshot` already behaves this way. The curtain is `useIntroDone()` / `useAfterIntro()` in `lib/intro.js`, and **the build is made as if the curtain were up**: the first visit is the arrival most worth getting exactly right, and a reload flips to "done" under the veil. A text mismatch makes React discard the copy and build afresh - safe, but the gain is lost; **an attribute mismatch is never patched** and leaves the build's value in the page. Floating-point output must be rounded (`IrisMark` fixes its rays to three places: Node and Chrome disagree in a sine's last digit).
- **What the pass after adoption changes**, all of it in place: the stored hospital (header, footer, contact switchboard and directory, a service page's actions, the hospitals map, and the booking form, which holds a hospital only once one is named or chosen and shows `openingBranch` until then, so it is never rebuilt - rebuilding it painted the step's lede a second time and Chrome counted it as a new LCP); the OPD status (`BranchStatus` states the hospital's hours in the build - "OPD hours" over the first row - and the live answer after); the cookie sheets (`CookieConsent` is not in the build and mounts as React adopts the page); WebGL (`EyeModel` is its drawing in the build); whether an observer exists (the service visit's route and the explorer's console and grid are on in the build, off until seen after); and the home film (the build carries its poster `<picture>`, see Hero).
- **Adoption is invisible, and must stay so.** It happens under the curtain or the veil, and `html.app-adopting` (added by `main.jsx`, removed by `AppPreloader` once that pass is laid out) turns transitions off on `#root` meanwhile, so whatever the reader's values change lands in its final state as a fresh build would. A shared-layout element whose place depends on the reader carries a different `layoutId` until then (`ct-jack-bg-built`), or framer would slide it from the head office to their hospital. `AppPreloader` does everything - lifting the veil, driving the curtain - from the pass after adoption, the moment a fresh build used to mount, and the header writes `storeBranch()` only from then, or it would overwrite the reader's hospital with the build's.
- **A shape decided in JS must not move what follows it.** The build is the laptop shape; where a phone shows a different one, the content after it moves as React adopts the page, and Chrome counts that as a layout shift even under the veil. CSS keeps the build's laptop-only parts out of a phone's layout - `.ap-aside { display: none }` below 1024px, where the booking slip lives in the last step. Measure CLS on a phone after touching such a section.
- **Check every change with React's development build.** `NODE_ENV=development npx vite build --mode development`, the same for `--ssr src/entry-server.jsx --outDir dist-server --emptyOutDir`, then `node scripts/prerender.mjs`: served, each page logs every mismatch React finds while adopting it ("A tree hydrated but some attributes..." / "Hydration failed..."). Load each page as a first visit and as a returning reader with a hospital stored and a cookie choice made, on a phone and a laptop; zero messages is the bar. Rebuild for production afterwards.
- **The static copy is the finished page.** `scripts/prerender.mjs` strips framer-motion's starting styles (`opacity:0`, transforms, clip-paths) from it and keeps each beside it in `data-ssr-style`, which `main.jsx` puts back just before adopting (`restoreStartingPoses()`) - so the copy reads without JavaScript and paints whole, and React's first pass still matches it. Readers never see it settle: the curtain or the veil covers it.
- **Every page carries a Content-Security-Policy `<meta>`, written by the prerender from the page as built** (`securityPolicy()` in `scripts/prerender.mjs`): scripts only from the site and the page's own inline scripts, allowed by the sha256 of their exact text, so any inline script added to `index.html` or the prerender is allowed automatically. Frames only from the `mapEmbed` origins in `branches.json`. No `eval` (zod runs `jitless`, set in `appointmentData.js`, because its `new Function` probe reports as a console error under the policy), nothing fetched from another origin, styles `'unsafe-inline'` (the static copy's style attributes, framer's popLayout `<style>`). `frame-ancestors`, `nosniff`, `Cross-Origin-Opener-Policy`, `Permissions-Policy` and HSTS are headers every host sends (`SECURITY_HEADERS` in the same script). The build also deletes the `.DS_Store` files Vite copies from `public/`. **A third-party script, font, image or API means adding its origin to `securityPolicy()`** - otherwise it is blocked, and that only shows as a console error. Verified by loading all 45 pages under the policy at 390 and 1440px, the production and development builds both: 0 violations, 0 hydration messages; and 13 representative pages in Safari 27 (0 violations, 0 errors, the Google map framed and any other origin refused). `docs/hosting.md` has the Security section.
- **One head per page.** `SEO` takes the page's `seo` block; `lib/seo.js` resolves it (`buildHead`) into the title, description, robots, canonical (`https://www.aakasheyehospital.com` + the slash-less path, never a query string; none on a `noindex` page), Open Graph and Twitter tags, and the prerender writes the same tags into the HTML with the social image's real pixel size. In the browser `applyHead()` replaces every `data-seo` tag on each navigation, so a page without an image cannot inherit the last page's.
- **One JSON-LD `@graph` per page**, from `components/JsonLd.jsx` over `lib/schema.js`, with stable `@id`s (`/#organization`, `/#website`, `/branches/<slug>#hospital`, `/services/<slug>#service`, `/doctors#<name-slug>`). Only what the page shows: phone groups and social profiles flagged `"unconfirmed": true` are left out, and so is anything the hospital has not confirmed.
- **The build is the check.** `prerender.mjs` fails on a page without a title, without exactly one `<h1>`, with the wrong canonical, invalid or missing JSON-LD, a duplicate title or description, a page rendering as not-found, or a redirect pointing at a page that does not exist; it warns on a title over 65 characters or a description outside 70-165. Fix the cause - never relax the check.
- **A heading must read as words to a crawler.** Adjacent elements that the layout separates still need a real space between them in the markup (`{" "}`): textContent is what a search engine reads, and it once read every service H1 as `CataractSurgery` and every doctor as `Dr. Vishnu S. PatelMS (Ophth)`.
- **Pages, sitemap and robots come from the data.** `getPages()` in `src/entry-server.jsx` lists every page from `services.json` and `branches.json`; the sitemap's `lastmod` is each page's sources' last git commit (today for uncommitted edits, left out in a shallow clone), and its `image:image` entries are the photographs each page shows with alt text.
- **Fonts are self-hosted** (`src/styles/fonts.css`, inlined into `index.html`; files in `public/assets/fonts`), with the two first-screen faces preloaded and **metric-matched fallback faces** (`size-adjust` and the overrides, measured against Georgia/Arial and Android's Noto Serif/Roboto) first in every stack, so a font swap moves nothing - home CLS went from 0.112 to 0.001. Re-measure the fallbacks if a family changes.
- **Below-the-fold images are `loading="lazy"`.** `SmartImage` turns `eager` into `fetchpriority="high"`, so an eager image anywhere but a page's hero competes with the LCP image - the hospital gallery's first photograph did, and cost Visnagar 0.6s of LCP.

## Stylesheets per page

**A page loads the shared stylesheet and its own, and nothing else.** The site used to ship one 285KB stylesheet (51KB gzipped) to every page; now `main.jsx` imports only what every route needs (see the namespaced-stylesheets rule under Architecture Rules) - one `index-*.css` of 59KB (12.2KB gzipped) - and each page imports its own sheet, which Vite writes beside the page's chunk: home 43KB, the services index and service pages share `services-*.css` (53KB), a hospital page 33KB, contact 19KB, booking 20KB, the About pages 18KB, the doctors page, a doctor's page and the hospitals index about 8KB each. Every page links exactly two stylesheets.

- **The first page a visitor opens links its own sheet in its head**, render-blocking right after the shared one, so neither the static copy nor React's first render is ever unstyled. `scripts/prerender.mjs` reads Vite's build manifest (`build.manifest` in `vite.config.js`; the prerender deletes `dist/.vite` once it has read it) and walks the page module's imports (`pageAssets()`), so a sheet a page reaches through a shared chunk is linked too.
- **Moving between pages in the app**, Vite's preload helper fetches the next page's sheet before the route's module resolves, so the incoming page is styled the frame it is inserted - checked on two ten-page sequences at 390 and 1440px, and the geometry of every element after a move matches a direct load (0 differences).
- **The page's own chunks are fetched once the first screen has painted**, by an inline script the prerender writes (`warmScript()`): it adds their `modulepreload` links when the image marked `fetchpriority="high"` has loaded, or after 3s. As plain `modulepreload` links in the head they took the bandwidth the hero picture needed (home LCP 1.27s against 0.98s); left to the router, React waited a round trip longer for them (3.77s against 4.10s). Now home LCP is 0.98s and React renders at 3.70s (4x CPU, 1.6Mbps).
- **A component more than one page renders cannot rely on one page's sheet.** `CountUp` (`.e-count`) and `BranchStatus` (`.br-status`) kept their rules in `landing.css` and `branch.css`, and every other page using them rendered them unstyled - found by diffing computed styles, not by looking. Their rules are their own sheets now (`count-up.css`, `branch-status.css`), imported by `main.jsx` because at 1.1KB together they cost every page less than the extra render-blocking request each was. Before moving a rule out of the shared sheets, list every page that renders the component.
- **Verified by computed style, not by eye**: every element and pseudo-element on all 45 pages, at 390 and 1440px under reduced motion, matches the single-sheet build exactly (0 differences) in Chrome. Not yet re-checked in Safari: a self-reporting probe page stalled each time about a minute in, because Safari pauses a page while another app is in front - run it with Safari frontmost.
- **Measured**: first paint about halves on every page (1.6-1.8s to 0.8-1.0s at 4x CPU and 1.6Mbps) and React renders 0.2-0.3s sooner. LCP falls by half on the home, doctors, doctor, contact, booking and hospitals pages and is unchanged where a photograph sets it (emergency, Visnagar). Lighthouse's default mobile run (what PageSpeed Insights shows) puts LCP lower on ten of twelve pages, level on contact and 0.3s higher on the journey page, and the performance score up on eight (home 68 to 71, Visnagar 72 to 75, booking and the hospitals index 77 to 80), level on three and a point down on journey.
- **The services index, the service pages and the two About pages used to report LCP as React's copy rising after the curtain** (4.0-5.1s at 4x CPU and 1.6Mbps), and it was not the stylesheets. The static copy paints before the web fonts arrive, in the metric-matched fallbacks; React's copy - in the web fonts - came out 1-3% wider wherever a line broke differently, and Chrome counts any larger paint as the new LCP. Two fixes were measured and rejected (block-mode twins of the web fonts for the static copy, which broke four other pages; normal wrapping in the static copy, which was not the cause). **The real fix landed: React adopts the static copy instead of rebuilding it** (see Search and prerendering), so there is no second copy to count - Chrome's observed LCP is the first paint on every page (see Known Launch Notes for the numbers).
- **Two traps for anyone measuring this.** A test server that sends images without `Content-Length` (chunked, or gzipped) makes Chrome judge an image's detail from the bytes it has so far, discard the hero as a low-content placeholder for good, and report LCP from whatever React paints later - the split first measured two to four times slower than it is. Real hosts send `Content-Length` for images; a local server must too. And DevTools network throttling shares bandwidth equally between requests, so preloaded fonts arrive far later than on a host that honours priorities - do not tune font loading on it.

## Landing Page Structure

The home page (`src/pages/HomePage.jsx`) renders, in order:

1. `Hero` - full-bleed hospital film under the header's glass capsule: a glass chip, a one-line serif headline, a two-line sentence, one cream booking pill and a three-stat trust row.
2. `ImpactStats`
3. `ConditionsCarousel`
4. `WhyChooseUs`
5. `DoctorHighlights`
6. `PatientStories`

The page ends there, on the patient wall, and the footer carries the closing appointment CTA. Ten sections used to follow it - `StorySpread`, `TreatmentIndex`, `FeatureSpread`, `TechnologyBand`, `FacilityStrip`, `Timeline`, `SpecialistsRail`, `BranchLocator`, `FAQ` and `AppointmentBand` - and all ten were the pre-rebuild landing page. They were removed rather than reordered: each was built to a different reference from the six above it, and keeping them would have meant the page introduced the doctors twice (`SpecialistsRail` repeated `DoctorHighlights`) and the branches three times. Their section files, their `.e-index` / `.e-feature` / `.e-people` / `.e-loc` / `.e-close` / `.e-story` / `.e-tech` / `.e-facility` / `.e-time` rules in `landing.css`, and their `home.json` keys (`featuredServiceIds`, `quickTreatments`, `story`, `technology`, `facility`, `closing`) are gone with them. `FAQ.jsx` and its `.e-faq` rules are gone too: the service detail page was its last consumer and now carries its own `ServiceFaq`. The home page's `FAQJsonLd` went with the visible FAQ, since structured data has to describe content the reader can actually see. Anything that replaces them is built one component at a time.

### Hero

`src/sections/Hero.jsx` owns the `.e-hero` rules at the top of `src/styles/landing.css`.

- The hero is a muted, looping film of the hospital - the building, the atrium reception, an examination room - following the Function Health reference. It replaced a still macro photograph of an eye; `eye-macro-wide.jpg` went with it.
- The source is a 6.9s 4K60 clip that is 100MB and unusable as shipped. Everything under `public/assets/media/heroes/hero-*` is encoded from it and must be re-encoded, never hand-edited. The recipe: crop `3688:2074:0:40` first, because the scenes after the first are composited inside a 3688x2154 box and carry black padding on the right and bottom edges that `cropdetect` will not find on its own (the opening exterior shot fills the full 3840x2160, so there is no bar spanning the whole frame). Then 1920x1080 for the landscape cut and a centred `1556:2074:1066:40` crop scaled to 900x1200 for the phone. Audio is stripped - autoplay requires it and the file does not need it. **The 4K source is not on the build machine any more**, so the current landscape cut was re-encoded from the shipped 1920x1080 mp4 (see the watermark line below); if the source turns up, redo both cuts from it.
- Two encodes rather than one CSS crop. `cover` in a portrait box would throw away two thirds of the landscape frame and still push the full 1920px width over mobile data; the 3:4 cut is framed for the phone and is a third of the bytes (webm 570KB against 1.25MB). `usePhoneVariant()` picks between them with `matchMedia` at the same 760px the layout breaks at, and the `key` on the element forces the reload when it changes.
- webm (VP9) first, mp4 (H.264) second, so the smaller file wins wherever it is understood.
- **The landscape cut is cropped clear of the generator watermark** - a semi-transparent four-pointed sparkle in every frame at about x 1695-1770, y 875-950 of the 1920x1080 cut, which sat beside `09 Consulting specialists` on a laptop. `crop=1680:944:0:54` on the 1920x1080 mp4, kept at 1680x944 rather than scaled back up (an upscale adds bytes, not detail); H.264 `-preset veryslow -crf 21`, VP9 two-pass `-crf 33`: 1.85MB and 1.02MB. The poster is frame 0 of the same crop, so the swap from poster to film does not jump. The phone crop never contained the mark. A sideways phone plays the landscape cut, so check it there too.
- The film is not load-bearing. The poster is its first frame, preloaded per breakpoint from `index.html` because it is the LCP element. **The prerendered hero carries the poster, not the film**: while React adopts the page `Hero` renders `HeroPoster` - the two posters in a `<picture>`, `fetchpriority="high"` - and a held `Play` control, and `HeroFilm` takes their place in the pass after, under the curtain or the veil, on the same frame. `usePhoneVariant()` reads `matchMedia` as the film mounts (it only ever mounts in the browser), so a phone no longer fetches the desktop poster first, and the one-frame flash of bare navy where the phone film used to replace the desktop one is gone. **That frame is a blur** - the film opens on a focus pull onto the building, which is right as the curtain lifts - so a film that will not play gets a sharp still instead: `still` in `home.hero.video` (`hero-desktop-still.jpg` / `hero-mobile-still.jpg`, the atrium at frame 78, 2.6s, cut from the same encodes) replaces the poster under `prefers-reduced-motion` and when `play()` is refused with `NotAllowedError` (iOS Low Power Mode, a data saver). The exterior is never fully sharp - the fade into the atrium starts as its focus lands - which is why the still is the atrium.
- **`.e-hero__media` carries no z-index at all, and is never animated**, so it makes no stacking context and the pause control's `z-index: 2` competes in the section's own stack, above the copy (`z-index: 1`). It was `z-index: 0`, which put the whole media layer, control included, under the copy's box - the copy's bottom padding on phones, the shell on wider screens - and **no tap or click ever reached the control**: tap-tested dead at 320, 390, 768, 844 landscape and 1366px. The film and scrim still paint under the copy in source order. Hit-test the control with `elementFromPoint` after touching any z-index here. **An animation on the frame does the same thing** - an animated transform or opacity makes a stacking context - and the first build of the scroll choreography sank the whole frame and killed the control again at every width; the choreography moves `.e-hero__video` and `.e-hero__scrim` individually and the control rides its own layer (see Scroll choreography).
- The control's label and `aria-pressed` follow the element's own `play`/`pause` events rather than the click, because autoplay can be refused (iOS Low Power Mode, a data saver, a browser policy) and the button has to show what actually happened. A refused `play()` fires no `pause` event, so `attempt()` also sets the control to `Play` on `NotAllowedError` - before, a refused film sat still under a control offering to pause it. The glyphs are filled (`fill="currentColor"`): outlined, the pause sign read as two hollow boxes at 16px.
- The element sets `muted` as an attribute as well as a property (React only does the latter, and Safari wants the attribute on the element it is asked to autoplay) and carries `disablePictureInPicture`, `disableRemotePlayback` and `controlsList`. A browser that refuses the film tends to paint its own play affordance over it, and the hero already has one control; there should never be two.
- The film pauses when it leaves the screen and resumes when it comes back, but a deliberate pause is remembered in `pausedByUserRef` and survives the round trip. Do not let the observer override it.
- **On a first visit the film is held at its first frame until the opening curtain starts to lift** (`heldByIntroRef`, released by `INTRO_DONE_EVENT` from `src/lib/intro.js`), and the copy rises from 0.35s after that, so it is arriving as the eye opens onto it. **It rises in reading order** - chip, headline, sentence, button, 80ms apart, then the three figures from 0.3s later (`RISE` / `cascade()` in `Hero.jsx`, the service hero's device) - rather than as one block. Measured at 6x CPU throttle on a 390x844: median frame 17ms, none over 50ms. Before this the film autoplayed under the curtain and the reader joined it 2.6s into a 6.9s loop. `heldAtMount` is read into state once as `HeroFilm` mounts and `afterIntro` comes from `useAfterIntro()`, not from the ref, because `react-hooks/refs` rejects a ref read in render; a page reached in-session has no curtain, no hold and no delay.
- Art direction still changes at 760px, but no longer by demoting the media to a band - the portrait encode is cut for that box and renders about 1:1 on a 3x screen. The section becomes a two-row grid: the film and the copy share the first row, and the stat row gets a solid navy band underneath, which is the reference layout. `.e-hero__inner` takes `display: contents` so its two children can hold their own rows; both then carry the shell width themselves. It is a plain div, so dissolving its box costs nothing semantically - do not do this to an element with an implicit ARIA role.
- **A phone held sideways drops the chip and the sentence** (`(max-width: 1179px) and (max-height: 520px)`) and, below 761px, reserves the capsule's height at the top of the copy (`padding-top: --hd-height + 14px`, rows `auto auto`), so the copy grows the hero downward rather than sliding up under the glass. Before it, at 740x360 and 667x375 the chip was hidden under the capsule and the headline cut by it. Measured at 844x390, 740x360 and 667x375: headline and button on the first screen, nothing under the capsule, the figures band on screen and the numbers sheet starting on `innerHeight`.
- The phone film row now runs to the top of the screen, under the 72px header; the headline needs about 240px of it with the CTA and its padding, so the smallest phone still shows well over a third of the frame above the copy.
- **The hero says one thing, in the reference's shape, at every width: chip, one headline line, a sentence of at most two lines, one button.** It used to carry a two-line headline, a 115-character sentence and a second action (`Call Visnagar OPD`), and read as busier than the reference it follows. The headline is `Advanced` + the italic `eye care.` on one line (`.e-hero__accent` is inline now), the sentence is 68 characters, and the call link is gone - the OPD and emergency numbers are in the header's strip, the drawer and the footer. **The phone shows the sentence again**, since it is two lines there and the headline one; it was dropped when the headline took three lines and the sentence four. Measured: the headline is one line from 360px up (two on the 320px SE), the sentence two lines at every width from 320 to 1920px, and `Book appointment` above the fold everywhere (its foot at 416px on a 320x568).
- **The hero owns the first screen at every size**: `min-height: calc(100svh - var(--hd-strip-h) + var(--sheet-r))`, with a `vh` fallback line above it, and `padding-bottom: var(--sheet-r)` - the numbers sheet lies over that foot (see Scroll choreography), so the sheet's top edge, not the hero's bottom, is where the first screen ends. The header's glass capsule floats on the film (`.hd[data-over]` pulls the hero up under it), so only the desktop strip above the hero is subtracted - `--hd-strip-h` from `header.css`, 0 on phones. `.e-hero__inner`'s top padding is at least `--hd-height + 32px` so copy can never sit under the capsule, the pause control's top position between 761 and 1040px is `--hd-height + 14px`, and `.e-hero__shade` is a shade across the top of the film for the capsule's white links (see the Header section) - its own element rather than the scrim's first layer, because the film sinks on scroll and the shade has to stay under the header while it does. Sized by a share of the viewport it left a strip of the next section's empty top padding showing under the stat band - 43px on a 390x844 phone, 79px on a 1080p monitor - and a blank strip of paper under a navy band read as a gap, not a peek. Measured: the numbers sheet's top edge equals `innerHeight` exactly (the hero's bottom is `--sheet-r` below it) at 320x568, 360x640, 375x667, 390x844, 412x915, 430x932, 360x780, 768x1024, 1024x768, 1440x900, 1920x1080 and 2560x1440. A 1280x600 laptop window is the one that overflows, because the copy needs more than 467px; content is the floor. Verify by measuring, not by eye.
- **On phones the video is `position: absolute` inside its frame.** The section only has a `min-height`, which leaves the grid's block size indefinite and its `1fr` row content-sized - and a 360px-wide portrait video is 480px of content, which held the film row open and pushed the stat band 42px past the fold on a 360x640 phone. Out of flow, the row is exactly what the screen has left and `cover` crops the film to it. The row is `minmax(0, 1fr)` and the frame carries `min-height: 0` for the same reason.
- `Book appointment` is the one thing the phone layout protects and it clears the fold on every size (its foot at 416px on a 320x568). On phones from 360x640 up the stat band clears it too; `ImpactStats` owns the numbers two sections later regardless.
- `.e-hero__copy` carries 62px of bottom padding on phones purely to leave the corner under the CTA free for the pause control. At 22px the control sat on top of the right end of the action row and stole part of its tap target.
- **The control's right edge is the shell's right edge at every width** (`right: calc((100% - var(--e-shell)) / 2)`, resolved against the film's own width) - the line the figures end on and the mirror of the copy's left edge; at 1440 it also lands under the right edge of the header's booking pill. **From 1180px it sits on the booking button's row**, centred on the button (`bottom: --hero-foot + 7px`), and the figures leave it room (`margin-right: 44px + --hero-stat-gap`). Pinned to the window's corner as it used to be, it landed on `09 Consulting specialists` from 1041 to 1411px. Paying for that room is why the row's gap is `clamp(28px, 4vw, 48px)` and the stat spacing `--hero-stat-gap` (`clamp(18px, 2vw, 30px)`): at the old sizes the headline broke onto two lines from 1440px up. Measured one line at 1100-2560px. **From 761 to 1179px it sits at the film's top right**, under the capsule - beside the figures there it would push the headline onto two lines at 1041px. It is 44px at every width; phones had it at 40.
- Contrast is a requirement, not a preference, and it has to hold across every scene rather than on the poster frame - the atrium skylight and the exam-room floor are near-white. Measure by seeking the film to several timestamps, making the glyphs `color: transparent` while leaving every background behind them painted rather than hiding the copy block (hiding it measures bare film and ignores any scrim or pill the text actually sits on, which once under-reported a measurement by a full 7 points), then sampling each text box and checking against WCAG (3:1 for the headline, 4.5:1 for body-size text).
- Sample each box as a **grid and keep the worst cell**, never as one average. Text sits on moving photography, so a bright patch under a single letter is exactly the failure a box average hides: the phone headline averaged a comfortable 5.40:1 over the same frame where its worst cell was 2.82:1, below the minimum. Worst local cell across six timestamps is now phone headline 4.72:1; desktop headline 4.69:1, lede 11.1:1, call link 15.6:1.
- The phone scrim is deliberately light. The heavy version it replaced was sized for a lede that needed 4.5:1, and once the lede was dropped it was holding nothing but a display line that needs 3:1 - all it did was flatten the lower half of every scene into solid navy, so the hero read as a dark panel with a strip of ceiling rather than as film. The gradient now grounds the last third and leaves the rest of the frame open. If copy is ever added back to the phone hero, the scrim has to be re-measured, not restored from memory. **Its lower layer is the figures band's own navy (`12, 44, 73`), opaque at the foot**, so the film dissolves into the band; in the near-black it used to be, the film ended darker than the band under it and the join read as a seam.
- **The phone figures band reads 2 / 2 / 2 lines at every width from 320px**: each label is capped at `8.4em` with `text-wrap: balance` (0.76rem), the middle column is `1.15fr`, and the outer columns sit on the shell's edges. Left to fill its column, `Founded in Visnagar` fitted one line from 412px while the other two broke, and at 320px `Hospitals across Gujarat` took three. A phone held sideways lifts the cap (the columns are wide enough for one line each) and runs its rows `1fr auto`, so spare height goes to the film instead of an empty strip under the labels.
- The phone CTA is a pill that hugs its label, not a full-bleed bar. The bar was the heaviest object on the screen and read as a slab of white across the frame; the reference lets the film carry the width and gives the action a shape. It stays a generous target at 56px tall with its label padded 26px from the edge and a 44px arrow disc, which is what matters for the older patients this site is built for - do not shrink it to make it prettier.
- The mobile crop was tested tighter (dropping the empty ceiling) and the tighter version is worse: it truncates the skylight arch mid-curve and crowds the exam room with a foreground stool. Keep the full-height 3:4 cut. The film reads as 70.5% of the phone viewport against the reference's 70%.

- **The copy is set the way the Function Health hero sets it, and that is what makes it read crisp on the film.** Measured on functionhealth.com: a display serif at weight 300, normal spacing, a warm off-white and a two-layer text-shadow (`0 2px 6px`, `0 8px 16px`). The build before this was Inter Tight 600 at 82px squeezed to `-0.035em` in pure white with no shadow - on moving footage the letters ran into each other and read as a soft white mass, which is what "not crisp" was. Now:
  - **A glass chip over the headline** (`.e-hero__eyebrow`, `hero.eyebrow` - `Appointments on WhatsApp`), in the reference's slot for its "HSA/FSA Eligible": a true, patient-facing fact about booking here. **Its glass is dark** (`rgba(7,20,33,0.5)` with blur): a white tint lightens the ground under white type, and at 320px, where the chip sits high in the bright part of the film, it measured 1.99:1.
  - **The headline is Source Serif 4 at weight 350** (the font request is now a `300..600` weight range), `-0.012em`, `#fbf7ef`, with the reference's two-layer shadow. 350 rather than the reference's 300 because this face's hairlines at 300 are finer than theirs. `clamp(3rem, 5.6vw, 5.2rem)` - the reference's 80px at 1440 - which is also what keeps one line inside the copy column (at 5.6rem a line of this length needed 670px of a 664px column). On phones `max-width` is lifted and the size is `clamp(2.5rem, 11.3vw, 3.5rem)`, because the serif's optical-size axis draws it wider at phone sizes.
  - **The supporting line is 1.08-1.3rem at 0.95 white** (1.02rem on phones) with a hairline shadow and `text-wrap: balance`, so its two lines are even rather than leaving two words on the second (it was 16px at 0.88).
  - **The CTA is made of the hero's own two colours** (`.e-hero__cta`, 58px, 56px on phones): the headline's cream (`#fbf7ef`) with navy type, a faint white sheen across its top and a navy hairline at its foot, and the arrow in a 44px navy disc (`.e-hero__cta-disc`) - the navy of the figures band under the copy. On hover the pill turns white, lifts 2px, the disc lightens to `--e-navy-2` and the arrow steps 3px forward. It replaced a flat cream pill the hospital found plain; **a magenta build came between the two and was sent back** - a saturated fill jumped out of the film and read as an advert rather than part of the hero. Keep the booking action in the hero's cream and navy. Pixel-sampled worst cell, glyphs transparent: label and arrow 13.35:1 at 320, 390 and 1440px. `ctaLabel` lives in `home.hero`.
  - Contrast, pixel-sampled worst cell across seven film timestamps with every text-shadow removed so none can flatter the number - re-measured after the watermark crop and the band-navy phone scrim: at 1920 and 1440 the chip 15.7 / 16.4:1, headline 8.7 / 8.4:1, sentence 12.1 / 12.5:1, figures 9.3 / 6.5:1 and their labels 6.7 / 5.9:1; at 1024 headline 7.7:1 and sentence 9.8:1; at 412, 390, 360 and 320 the chip 11.9 / 11.1 / 8.9 / 7.4:1, headline 6.9 / 6.4 / 4.6 / 3.7:1 and sentence 8.5 / 7.9 / 6.4 / 7.2:1; the band's figures 14.3:1 and labels 8.4:1. The bluer scrim cost about a point on the phone headline; every value still clears 3:1 for display type and 4.5:1 for the rest. Hero controls take a cream focus ring (`#fbf7ef`) rather than the site's magenta, which is barely 3:1 on the film's navy. **Below 360px the phone scrim is deeper**: the SE is the one phone where the headline wraps, which lifts the copy into a brighter band of the film, and there it measured 2.79:1 against the 3:1 a display line needs. Every wider phone keeps the lighter film.

`AppointmentPage` reads `branch`, `service` and `name` from the query string so a hospital page, a treatment page or the header can prefill the booking flow.

### ImpactStats

`src/sections/ImpactStats.jsx` is the numbers band directly under the hero and owns the `.e-impact` rules at the end of `src/styles/landing.css`.

- Four cards - surgeries, patients, specialists and hospitals - each with a kicker numeral, a counted figure, a label, a line of copy and a small illustrative panel. The panel is what makes the section feel alive; the layout follows the Function Health "Testing is easy" reference.
- Card copy and the procedure mix / patient trend figures live in `home.impact`. **The specialists and hospitals figures are counted, not typed**: a card (or a hero stat) carries `"count": "specialists"` or `"count": "hospitals"` in place of a `value`, and `HomePage` resolves both from the data (`specialists` in `doctorsData.js` - every record that is not an optometrist - and `branches.items`). The hero's `19 Consulting specialists`, this card and the journey page's closing `Eye specialists` figure read the same `specialists` list, so they can never disagree; they used to be three hand-typed 9s. The people visual lists the first three consultants; the phone's proof card shows no charts.
- The heading is the Gujarati line `impact.titleGujarati` over the English tagline (`impact.tagline` plus the accented `impact.taglineAccent`). Gujarati needs its own family - `--e-gujarati` (Noto Serif Gujarati, self-hosted in `src/styles/fonts.css`) - because Inter Tight has no Gujarati coverage and the system fallback differs on every platform. The serif face is deliberate: it pairs with the Source Serif italic tagline and the hero's serif accent, where a grotesque read flat.
- Source Serif 4 ships with its italic (self-hosted, `src/styles/fonts.css`), so the tagline and the hero accent get true italics instead of browser-slanted romans.
- `.e-head__body` caps its measure at `46ch`, which is computed on 16px body text and is far too narrow for a 3.3rem display line; `.e-impact` clears that cap on the head and puts the measure on the tagline instead.
- **The band is a sheet laid over the hero's foot**: `margin-top: calc(var(--sheet-r) * -1)`, rounded top corners and a shadow cast back onto the film (the SHEETS block at the top of `landing.css`). Where the browser drives animation from scroll, the cards' arrival is the scroll choreography's and framer's entrance stands down (`SCROLL_DRIVEN`); elsewhere the cards keep framer's entrance.
- Each card owns its own in-view trigger (`useInViewReplay` on the card, `amount: 0.45`) and passes it down to `CountUp` and to its panel; on a phone the proof card's band owns it (`amount: 0.6`). **The count and the panel play again every time the reader comes back to the card** - see "Card animations replay" under Scroll choreography. Do not move the trigger back onto the number itself: in a tall card the figure sits near the top, so watching the figure started the count while only the card's top edge had crossed the fold, and the count finished before the card was readable.
- The figures animate with `src/components/CountUp.jsx`, which counts when its `start` prop turns true and renders the final value immediately under `prefers-reduced-motion`. The counting digits are `aria-hidden` and the whole figure sits in a `.sr-only` span beside them, because a card off screen shows `0` - a screen reader always hears `1,00,000+`, never the picture of the count. Numbers are grouped with `en-IN` (1,00,000), so keep values as plain numbers in JSON.
- The hospitals card cycles a highlighted branch chip every 1.9s; the cycle is disabled under reduced motion, and each chip links to `/branches?branch=<slug>`.
- Grid is 4 columns, and 2 columns at <= 1080px. The illustrative panels stretch (`flex: 1`) so their tops line up inside a row.
- **Each card's five parts sit on rows shared across the grid** (`grid-template-rows: subgrid`, `grid-row: span 5`, inside `@supports`), so a label that wraps or a sentence one line longer moves its neighbour's parts with it - two cards side by side on a tablet otherwise start their sentences and panels at different heights. The label sits on the foot of its row (`align-self: end`), so a one-line label stays with its sentence. The card has exactly five children - the visual's wrapper renders even when empty - and `span 5` depends on it.
- The trend plot's SVG is `preserveAspectRatio="none"` with a `non-scaling-stroke` line inside a `240 / 74` box, the end dot is an HTML span placed in percentages so it stays round, and the reveal is a `clip-path` wipe on the SVG - a `pathLength` draw does not survive a non-scaling stroke.
- The branch chips are links, so each is a 40px chip with a 3px invisible `::after` halo - a 46px target that still leaves 2px between rows at the 8px gap. `.e-impact__caption`, `.e-impact__axis` and `.e-impact__more` are `--e-ink-2`, not `--e-ink-3`: 0.68-0.74rem semibold needs 4.5:1 and `--e-ink-3` measures 2.9:1 on the panel. Hover on the chips and the cards' lift is gated on `(hover: hover) and (pointer: fine)`: a tablet's finger left both stuck.

**On a phone the four cards are one proof card** (`ProofCard`, `.e-impact__proof` / `__photo` / `__tag` / `__band` / `__fact`, and `.e-impact__nearest` under it): the head office's own photograph dissolving into a navy band, and the four figures on that band in the hero's figure style - cream serif, labels at 0.78 white, ruled two by two with the hero band's hairlines. It is the hero film's phone composition at card size (photograph, scrim into navy, figures on the navy), so the page arrives at the numbers without changing language. Two phone builds came before it and both read as a dashboard and were sent back: a console of four figure tiles over a tabbed chart panel, then a ledger of 58px figures over gradient bar charts ("not going with the website UI", "the numbers are too eye catching"). The charts stay on the laptop's cards. The card section is 753px at 390x664 (the ledger was 1060, the console 978).

- **Shape decided in JS** (`PHONE_QUERY` in `ImpactStats.jsx`, `useSyncExternalStore`): `(max-width: 620px), (max-width: 1023px) and (max-height: 520px)` - a phone, or a phone held sideways - because the card is different markup. Every card rule sits under the same query in `landing.css`. Tablets and wider keep the card grid and the two actions; the build is the grid, and the card takes its place as React adopts the page, below the fold (CLS 0 at 320x568 and 390x664).
- **The photograph is data**: the head office's (`getPrimaryBranch`) hospital-page `imageSmall`, its `imageAlt`, and a dark-glass tag (the hero chip's glass, with the one pin) filled from `impact.photoTag` - `Visnagar head office, since 1993` - or `photoTagNoYear` for a head office with no confirmed year. A new head-office photograph changes the card with no edit here. It is `loading="lazy"`, 4:3 on a phone, and drifts inside its frame on the scroll (`e-drift` at 1.12, the services stage's move; the frame is `overflow: clip`).
- **The figures are the hero band's, a step smaller**: `clamp(1.4rem, 6.9vw, 1.85rem)` in the cream `#fbf7ef`, the `+` in the soft accent `#f4a6c4` (the magenta is 2.6:1 on navy), labels 0.8rem. `1,00,000+` holds its line in a 108px column at 320px (`.e-impact__band` gives up 4px a side below 360px). They count up once 60% of the band is on screen (`useInViewReplay(band, 0.6)` - the band sits under the photograph, so the card's own trigger started the count unseen) and replay off screen like the cards.
- **One way on**: `impact.nearestLabel` (`Find the hospital nearest you`) as an `.e-link` to `/branches`, which replaced six hospital chips. No booking or services button: the hero's is a screen above, the header carries its own, and the services index follows.
- **The motto plays itself on a phone** (`e-impact-dawn`, in the scroll choreography): અંધકારથી અજવાળા તરફ - "from darkness towards light" - brightens from its first word to its last as the sheet rises off the film, a text-clipped gradient sliding across it on the scroll (`entry 25%` to `cover 45%`, finished before the line is halfway up the screen). Ink at rest, solid ink under reduced motion and in browsers without scroll timelines; the glyphs keep 0.12em of ground above and below so no matra is cut from the light.
- **A phone held sideways** sets the photograph beside the band, dissolving into it from the left, with the figure following the width (`clamp(1.2rem, 3.5vw, 1.85rem)`) and the tag at 0.74rem on one line: the card is 227-332px tall from 568x320 to 932x430.
- Pixel-sampled worst cell, glyphs transparent: the tag 7.0:1 on a phone and 7.3:1 sideways (its glass is 0.6, and the sideways scrim keeps a top shade - at 0.52 with none it measured 4.4:1 at 844x390), figures 13.4:1, labels 9.2:1, the `+` 7.5:1.
- Measured on the dev server and the production build at 320x568, 360x640, 375x553, 390x664, 393x659, 412x915, 430x746, 600x960, 620x900, 568x320, 667x375, 740x360, 844x390 and 932x430: no overflow, every figure on one line inside its column, the tag on one line inside the photograph, the link a 45px target through its halo. Real touch at 320x568, 390x664 and 844x390: the link opens `/branches` and a tap on the card does nothing. Under reduced motion every figure is final, the photograph still, the motto solid ink and 0 animations run. A real touch scroll through the section on the production build runs at a 16.7ms median with no frame over 34ms at 4x CPU throttle (one 66ms frame at 6x). 0 hydration messages in the development build at 320x568, 390x664 (first visit and returning), 844x390, 768x1024 and 1440x900. Not yet checked in Safari.

- Surgery, patient and procedure-mix numbers are placeholders until the hospital confirms them.

### ConditionsCarousel

`src/sections/ConditionsCarousel.jsx` is the service picker under the impact band and owns the `.e-cond` rules in `src/styles/landing.css`. It follows the Manipal "Centres of Excellence" reference: **one wheel of names beside one photograph** on a wide screen, the same wheel turned on its side on a phone held upright (eleven rows under one pinned photograph), and a rail of photograph cards on a tablet or a phone held sideways. It replaced a six-slide rail of condition cards with doctor quotes, treatment pills and a `quoteFull` / `quoteShort` pair; all of that copy is gone from `home.json` and the file keeps its name because the tablet shape is still a carousel.

**Eleven, not six, and the list is the services list.** `home.conditions.items` names each service by slug and carries only the one line written for this section (`headline`); the title, photograph, icon and sentence are the service's own, joined through `getTreatedConditions()` in `src/lib/homeData.js`, so nothing about a service is written twice and a slug that matches nothing is dropped rather than rendered empty. The order is the services index's order (surgical, specialty, children, everyday, urgent), so `Emergency Eye Care` is 11 and the wheel counts the same way `/services` does. The section labels (`eyebrow`, `title`, `tagline`, `ctaLabel`, `findPrompt`, `findLabel`, `previousLabel`, `nextLabel`) live beside the items. The eyebrow is `Our services`, not `What we treat` - over `Conditions we treat` it said the same words twice - and the lede is `text-wrap: balance`, which it needed on every phone from 360px: it ended on `hospitals` alone.

**Three shapes, decided in JS** (`useSyncExternalStore` over `WIDE_QUERY` and `INDEX_QUERY`, the locate card's device; the build is the rail): the wheel from 1024px, the index on a phone held upright (`(max-width: 760px) and (min-height: 521px)`), and the rail on a tablet or a phone held sideways. They carry different roles - tabs beside a tab panel, a list of links, a rail of cards - and a `role="tab"` that is `display: none` is a lie to a screen reader. `data-shape` on the section names the one showing, and every index rule keys on it. The chosen index lives in the parent, so a resize mid-way flips the shape and keeps the service: verified live, picker 05 -> rail at 05 -> next -> picker at 06, nothing thrown.

**The wheel (>= 1024px).**

- A rounded paper panel holding a counter (`05 / 11`), an up chevron, a five-slot drum and a down chevron; the stage beside it stretches to the drum's height, so the photograph's frame is always exactly as tall as the wheel (520px). `--cond-item` (76px) in the CSS **must equal `ITEM_HEIGHT` in the component**: the drum's height and its padding are built from it there, and the roll offset here.
- **The names are translated by a spring, never scrolled.** The list is a `motion.div` with `y` bound to a motion value; a click, a chevron, `ArrowUp`/`ArrowDown`/`Home`/`End` animate it to `-index * ITEM_HEIGHT`. It was deliberately not built as a scroll-snap container or a wheel-event handler: a native vertical scroller inside the page captures the reader's wheel on the way past and is a scroll trap by another name, and a `drag="y"` sets `touch-action` so a tablet could not scroll the page over a 500px panel. Nothing here jacks the page's scroll.
- **Each name's distance from the centre slot is written straight to the DOM every frame** (`--d`, in slots, from `useMotionValueEvent`), and the CSS turns it into scale (1.14 at the centre) and fade (floor 0.62), so the drum moves as one and never re-renders to do it. **The first paint is explicit** - a motion value that does not change fires no change event, and without `paint(offset.get())` in the effect every name sat at `--d` 0, the chosen size, until the first roll; that is exactly how the first build shipped.
- **The drum rolls into place each time the wheel comes into view** (`ARRIVE_SLOTS`, 1.5 slots down, released by `useInViewReplay` on the panel): the one unasked-for move here, and what tells the reader this is a wheel and not a list. Above the fold it holds, so the roll is not spent unseen; once the wheel is fully off screen the drum is set back 1.5 slots below the chosen service, so a reader who comes back watches it roll onto the service they chose (verified: 05 away at -190px, -263 at 180ms back, settled at -304 with `--d` 0).
- **The drum is `overflow: clip`, never `hidden`, and focus is `preventScroll`.** `hidden` made it a scroll container, and focusing a name after `End` scrolled the drum under the translated list - the chosen name was out of sight and the drum read as empty. Both fixes are needed: `clip` cannot be scrolled programmatically at all.
- **The names are cut to `100% / 1.14` of the drum**, because the chosen one is scaled 1.14: at full width its scaled box grew past the drum and its focus ring lost both sides. The ring is an inset `box-shadow`, not an outline, for the same reason. Measured with `Range.getClientRects()`: no name wraps at 1024-2560px.
- The chosen name's neighbours measure 4.89:1 on the paper tint (ink at the 0.62 floor), the counter 16.6:1 and 7.1:1, pixel-sampled; the slot beyond them sits inside the drum's own mask fade, which is the reference's drum edge, and is not a text-contrast claim. Chevrons are 44px and read as pressed-flat when disabled, as the reference greys them.
- **The stage never goes blank.** Every photograph asked for stays mounted, stacked; the component names one `on` (fading in over whatever was there, 620ms, and settling from 1.06 to 1 over 1.4s - one slow move, once), one `under` (the previous one, held at full opacity with no transition) and the rest `off`. The fade only starts once the file has decoded (`data-loaded` from `onLoad`, with a `complete` check in the ref callback for a cached file), so a slow image never shows half-painted: verified on a throttled network, `under` at 1 while `on` arrives. The requested set grows in the choose handler (the chosen index and its neighbours), never in an effect, and is seeded from the index the picker mounts on. Photographs are `loading="lazy"`, so nothing loads until the section approaches.
- **The caption runs along the photograph's floor, not in a card floating over it.** The first build put the reference's white card at the bottom right, and every one of these photographs has its subject at the centre, so the card sat on the patient's face in most of them. Now a local gradient (`.e-cond__floor`, navy at the edge and clear by 72%) grounds the foot and the caption sits on it in white: the kind of care as an eyebrow, the headline, the sentence and a filled `Know more` (`.e-btn--light`, 52px) at the right; below 1180px the button drops under the words. The floor is one static element under every caption, so only the words swap (`AnimatePresence mode="wait"`, 340ms) - a gradient that left with the caption blinked the photograph bright between services. The tab panel is `aria-labelledby` the chosen tab, and the button carries a visually hidden `: <service>` so eleven `Know more` buttons are not eleven identical names to a screen reader.
- Contrast on the floor is pixel-sampled worst cell across **all eleven photographs**, with the glyphs made transparent and the text composited over each cell: at 1440px the eyebrow 5.4:1, headline 9.6:1, sentence 9.6:1; on a 390px phone the name 5.6:1 and headline 8.5:1. Re-measure if the gradient, the padding or a photograph changes.

**The index (a phone held upright).** The wheel turned on its side: the photograph is pinned under the header in a sticky dock and the eleven services run under it as rows, so the page's own scroll turns the wheel. It replaced the rail on phones, which showed one service to a screen - eleven swipes to learn what the hospital treats, and nothing to say which swipe a reader's own trouble was behind. Measured at 390x664: all eleven names in about two screens of scroll, every one a link.

- **The photograph shows the first row fully clear of the dock's foot** (`read()` in `Index`, a rAF-throttled passive scroll listener gated by an `IntersectionObserver`; the photographs it reaches are requested in that handler, never in an effect). A white plate travels to that row (`layoutId="e-cond-plate"`, the site's pill device), its numeral turns accent, its name navy, and a navy arrow disc appears at its end - the one arrow in the list. The other names fade with their distance below the foot (`--d`, floor 0.66: ink composites to 5.7:1 there, where the 0.95rem numeral would drop under 3:1, so only the name fades), and a row sliding under the dock fades out (`--up`) so it never shows as a slice of letters. Both are written straight to the DOM.
- **Rows are placed by their layout offsets, never their boxes**: they rise into place on the scroll (`e-rise`, 36px), and a box read mid-rise put the keyboard's first row 36px past the photograph.
- **The fade is inside the dock**, its bottom `--cond-fade` (18px) going from paper to clear, so the dock's box ends exactly where a row is fully clear - the edge the walker measures. Hung below the dock as a `::after`, the active row's plate slid under it whenever the header came back.
- **The dock follows the header** on `--hd-slide` (`body:has(.hd--hidden)`), and the foot is re-read every frame, so the plate stays on the first clear row whether the header is up or down.
- **The rows are `shortTitle`s in the serif** (`Children's eye care`, `Eye examination`) - one line at every width from 320px; the full title and the section's `headline` are in each link's accessible name (`.e-cond__sr`). Emergency care keeps the danger colour on its numeral, its plate (`#fbefed`) and its arrow, never on the faded name.
- **The caption on the photograph is the kind of care and the section's `headline`**, on a floor deeper than the stage's (the frame is 184-280px, `32svh`, so the caption takes half of it) with the label at 0.88 white. Pixel-sampled worst cell over all eleven photographs, glyphs transparent: label 9.7:1 and headline 13.5:1 at 320x568, 10.4 and 13.9 at 390x664. On the stage's own floor the label measured 4.1:1 on four of them at 320px.
- **The photograph is a second link to the row's service for a thumb** (`tabIndex=-1`, `aria-hidden`); the row is the link a keyboard and a screen reader use. **A keyboard Tab brings the row it reaches to the dock's foot** (`bringToFoot`, allowing for the header the scroll is about to show or hide), so the photograph always answers the focused row: Tab and Shift+Tab walk all eleven with the photograph following and every focused row clear of the dock.
- **The section takes the paper of its neighbours and their head on a phone**: the rule, the label and the headline set left (the rule draws across like theirs), the lede hidden (it named 1993 and six hospitals a third time in two screens), `padding-block: 24px`, and `z-index: 1` - the numbers sheet's upward shadow leaked a grey band across the top of the section below it. The way on is a quiet line after the list, `Not sure which one?` / `Find it by what you notice` to `/services#find-a-service`, outside the dock's box so the photograph never covers it.
- Measured on the production build: real touch scrolling through the list at 4x CPU throttle runs at a 16.7ms median with no frame over 34ms (50ms at 6x); real touch taps on all eleven rows, the photograph and the link land on their own href at 320x568 and 390x664, and nothing is left tinted after a tap and scroll; no overflow at 320, 360, 375, 390 and 430; 0 running animations under reduced motion; 0 hydration messages in the development build at 390x664 (first visit and returning), 844x390 and 1440x900. Not yet checked in Safari.

**The rail (a tablet, or a phone held sideways).**

- The same native `scroll-snap` rail as the doctor rail: swipe on touch, arrows on a pointer, keyboard scrollable, a plain horizontal scroller without JS. The track sits **outside `.e-shell`** with `padding-inline: max(24px, calc((100% - 1240px) / 2))` and a matching `scroll-padding-inline`, and 16px for both at <= 720px. It was first placed inside the shell with a `calc(50vw - var(--e-shell) / 2)` inset, and `--e-shell`'s `100%` then resolved against the track rather than the viewport - the inset came out at 32px and the rail opened 16px scrolled in.
- **A slide is the stage's frame at rail size**: the photograph fills a rounded frame (4:3 on a tablet, 4:5 on a phone, where the caption needs the height) and the same floor and caption sit on it, with the service's name set above the headline because there is no wheel to name it. The reference's white card overlapping the photograph was built first and removed for the reason the stage's was: it covered the patient's chin on every slide. Every frame in a row is the same height by construction, so the rail never shows a ragged line of card bottoms mid-swipe. **On a phone the sentence is dropped, not shrunk** (`display: none` at <= 760px): it is the first line of the page the button opens - the services index makes the same call for its rows - and with it the caption covered two thirds of the photograph. `object-position` moves to `50% 30%` there so the 4:5 crop keeps the head.
- Slides are `min(62vw, 520px)` wide, `min(100% - 36px, 400px)` below 761px; the gap equals the inset on phones (both 16px) for the reason the doctor rail gives. The arrows go below 761px - the next card is already peeking - and the counter and a scaleX progress bar (`--cond-progress`, written every scroll frame) stay, because eleven dots do not fit a 288px shell and a counter alone does not say how far along the reader is.
- **A phone held sideways** (`(max-width: 1023px) and (max-height: 520px)`) narrows the slide to `min(58vw, 400px)` and cuts the frame to 16:10 with the sentence dropped, so the name and the line under it reach the same screen as the picture.
- Verified with real touch events at 320x568 and 390x844: a horizontal drag moves one card and leaves `scrollY` where it was; a vertical drag over the rail scrolls the page and leaves the rail where it was.

**Measured:** no page overflow, no console errors and no `.e-cond` target under 44px at 320x568, 360x640, 375x667, 390x844, 412x915, 430x932, 844x390, 768x1024, 820x1180, 1024x768, 1180x800, 1280x720, 1440x900, 1920x1080 and 2560x1440. The head and a whole slide fit the first screen of a 390x844 (foot at 657px of the 779 budget). Under `prefers-reduced-motion`: 0 animations, the drum jumps (`offset.jump`), the photograph and the card are simply there. Checked in Safari as well as Chrome: the mask fade, the clip and the card render the same.

- The eleven `headline` lines are drafted patient-language summaries of each service and need the hospital's sign-off with the rest of the services copy.

### WhyChooseUs

`src/sections/WhyChooseUs.jsx` is the bento mosaic under the conditions picker and owns the `.e-why` rules in `src/styles/landing.css`. It follows the Narayana Health "Why Choose Narayana Health?" reference.

- The title is sentence case (`Why choose Aakash Eye Hospital?`) like every other heading on the site; it was the one Title Case heading on the page.
- Six tiles - expert care, advanced technology, care across locations, patient first, old case file promise, quality promise - live in `home.trust.items`. Each carries an `icon` (resolved through `src/lib/icons.js`, never imported ad hoc), a `tone`, a one-line `description` and a small uppercase `meta` proof line.
- Colour comes from `data-tone` in the JSON, never from `nth-child`, so reordering the items cannot scramble the palette. Tones are `navy`, `teal`, `accent` (dark, white text) and `clinical`, `paper` (light, ink text); three dark and three light keeps the rhythm of the reference without turning into a rainbow.
- The mosaic is a plain grid with per-column offsets: three columns with `nth-child(3n+2)` and `nth-child(3n+3)` pushed down, two columns with `nth-child(2n)` pushed down at <= 900px, and the phone shapes below 621px and on a phone held sideways. `align-items: start` is what lets the tiles keep their natural heights.
- **On a phone held upright the six reasons are a deck, not a grid** (`DECK_QUERY` in `WhyChooseUs.jsx`, `(max-width: 620px) and (min-height: 521px)`, which the deck block in `landing.css` must match). It replaced a two-column grid of the same tiles that the hospital sent back from a real phone: six half-width boxes (174px at 390, 139px at 320) each led by a glyph, sentences broken over four to six lines, uppercase proof lines over two and three, and visuals shrunk until they said nothing. Now every reason is a whole card across the width, and each card sticks one title row below the card before it (`position: sticky`, `top: --hd-height + 12px + --i * --why-strip`, following the header up through `body:has(.hd--hidden)` on `--hd-slide`), so the reader reads one whole card at a time, nothing they have read leaves the screen, and the finished stack is an index of all six titles with the last card open under it. As the section leaves, the deck squares up behind the last card.
  - **The card's first row is the strip a covered card keeps on show**: the index numeral (`.e-why__num`, `01`-`06` in the serif, inside the `h3` and `aria-hidden`, hidden from 621px where the icon leads) beside the title, one line at a fixed height (`--why-strip`, `clamp(46px, 8svh, 54px)`), so the stack of strips fits every phone with the last card whole under it. **No icon on a phone** - six glyphs stacked down an index was the per-item icon the hospital keeps sending back. Measured: no title wraps and every proof line holds one line at 320-620px, and the finished stack's foot is 504-552px down on every phone from 320x568 to 430x932 (the SE's 375x553 included) with the header hidden.
  - **A covered card recedes** by how far the cards over it have travelled (`--why-depth`, 0 to 5, `scale: 1 - depth * 0.02` from the card's top edge), so the strips step in like a deck seen from above. `useDeck` writes it from a rAF-throttled passive scroll listener that only runs while the list is on screen, reading every rect before writing - a measurement, not render state. It also holds every card to the tallest one's height (`--why-card-h`, re-measured when the list's width changes and when the fonts land), which is what lets the deck square up with no card peeking above the last. The sentence starts 8px under the strip so that even the deepest card, drawn at 0.9, never shows a line of it under its title.
  - **The column is flex, not grid** - a sticky grid item is confined to its own row and never sticks - and the finished index stands for a moment before squaring up because of a spacer (`::after`, `--why-hold`), not padding: a sticky card is held inside its container's content box. The section keeps its own 64px under the squared deck; with that cut to 16px the deck's shadow ended on a hard line where the doctors section begins.
  - **In the deck neither entrance runs**: the scroll choreography's `e-rise` and framer's `cardEntrance` both fade, and a card fading in over the one it covers shows that card through it. The card's own travel is its arrival; the visuals still play on arrival and replay on return. Under `prefers-reduced-motion` the same cards are a plain column - nothing slides over anything, the journey stage's rule - with 0 running animations.
  - Measured on a 390x664 with real touch drags at 4x CPU throttle: median frame 16.7ms, none over 34ms; the header hides on the way down and the stack re-forms under it (first strip at 84px) on the way up, and the header's logo, `Book` and menu hit-test to themselves over the stack. Safari 27 matches Chrome step for step (sticky, the depth, the header follow, the squaring). The deck costs height - about 2000px against the grid's 1230 - and that is the trade for whole cards at full width with nothing hidden.
- **A phone held sideways** (`(max-width: 1023px) and (max-height: 520px)`) has no height for a deck: the same card anatomy - numeral, no icon, proof line under a hairline - two to a row, level, no mosaic offsets (237-244px cards at 844x390). The proof line is `text-wrap: balance`, because at 568x320 `CLEAR GUIDANCE, NO` left `PRESSURE` alone on its line.
- Tablets and wider (621px up, any height over 520px) keep the mosaic unchanged - verified pixel-identical at 768, 1024, 1440 and 1920px after the phone pass. The tile's hover lift is gated on `(hover: hover) and (pointer: fine)`: a tap left it lifted.
- Tile text contrast is measured, not assumed. White at 0.82 over each dark tone gives 10.0:1 navy, 6.3:1 teal, 4.7:1 accent for the body copy. The `meta` line at 0.72 measured 3.89:1 on the magenta tile, below the 4.5:1 that 0.72rem uppercase text needs, so dark-tile meta sits at 0.88 (accent 5.2:1). Re-measure if a tone colour changes.
- This section deliberately carries no figures. `ImpactStats` two sections earlier already owns the numbers, and repeating 6 hospitals or 9 specialists here read as padding.
- Descriptions are one sentence each on purpose: two lines on a phone card at 360px and up, three on the SE. Tighten the copy before adding phone-only overrides - a longer sentence makes every card in the deck taller, since all six are held to the tallest.
- Each tile carries a small visual keyed by `visual` in the JSON, and every one of them illustrates its own point rather than decorating: `team` stacks the real consultant photos from `doctors.json` (only records with a `photo`; the rest are counted in its `+N`), `scan` is a diagnostic trace that draws bar by bar under a single sweeping beam, `map` connects one dot per branch along a dashed route, `explain` shows the three steps of a consultation, `files` fans out case-file years, and `checklist` ticks the protocol steps. Supporting strings live in `home.trust` (`explainSteps`, `filesYears`, `checklist`).
- Visuals never hard-code colour. The tile exposes `--why-ink`, `--why-line`, `--why-soft` and `--why-chip`, and the dark tones override all four, so a visual works on navy, teal, magenta or paper without knowing which it is on.
- Like an ImpactStats card, the tile owns the in-view trigger (`useInViewReplay`, `amount: 0.4`) and passes `active` down; the visuals play when the tile arrives, hold while it is on screen, and play again each time the reader comes back. The tile's own arrival is the scroll choreography's where the browser supports it (`SCROLL_DRIVEN`), framer's elsewhere. Under `prefers-reduced-motion` every visual renders its finished state immediately.
- The icon is rendered with `createElement(getIcon(...))` rather than `const Icon = getIcon(...)`. Inside a named component the assignment trips `react-hooks/static-components`; a `.map()` callback gets away with the older pattern, but do not copy it into a component here.
- On a phone the visuals are drawn at their desktop sizes (the card is about as wide as a desktop tile); only the route map is left-aligned at 138px like everything else in the card. The visual takes the card's slack and sits centred in it (`flex: 1 1 auto`, at least 56px), so cards held to one height keep their proof lines on one foot.

### DoctorHighlights
