`src/sections/DoctorHighlights.jsx` is the portrait rail under the trust mosaic and owns the `.e-docs` rules at the end of `src/styles/landing.css`. It follows the Function Health "Built with the world's top doctors" reference closely, and the things that make it read as that reference are the ones most easily lost - a first build put the head in this page's usual left-aligned label column with the arrows beside it and it stopped looking like the reference at all. Keep all five:

1. The head is **centred**, not the `.e-head` label/body grid the rest of the page uses.
2. The headline is **serif** (`--e-serif`), not Inter Tight - this is the one section on the page that leads with the serif - with the closing phrase in italic accent (`.e-docs__title em`, fed by `titleAccent`).
3. A centred, muted subtitle sits under it, in the register of the reference's "Function's Medical & Scientific Board".
4. The arrows **float over the photographs** at the left and right edges of the rail, as accent-outlined circles.
5. There are **no dots and no counter**.

- Section copy lives in `home.doctorHighlights` (`title`, `titleAccent`, `lede`, `ctaLabel`) and the card order in its `featured` array, which lists doctors by exact `name`. Names are resolved against `doctors.json` and anything that does not match is dropped, so a rename in one file cannot render an empty card.
- Card content comes from `doctors.json` and nothing is duplicated into `home.json`: the name carries its post-nominals inline (`Dr. Vishnu S. Patel, MS (Ophth)`) exactly as the reference does, over one line built from the record by `cardText` (`{specialty} at {hospitals}.` - `Senior Vitreoretina Consultant at Visnagar, Ahmedabad and Gota.`; `describeHospitals()` names every hospital the doctor sees patients at, visits included, in the site's order). It replaced a hand-written `highlight` per doctor, which was drafted copy and went with the hospital's own team list. **The featured list puts the four photographed consultants first** (Vishnu S. Patel, Sanjay B. Patel, Saurabh Kapoor, Dhaivat Vasavada), so the rail's first screen is portraits; the four after them (Aakash V. Patel, Nirav Zala, Pooja Yadav Patel, Toral A. Patel) show their monogram (`DoctorPortrait`) until a photo is supplied. Every portrait in the rail is lazy: `SmartImage` marks an eager image high priority, and this rail is four sections below the hero film.
- The portraits are **square, not 4:5**. The reference crops square, and the hospital's own files are 270x260, so a 1:1 box is both the faithful crop and very nearly a 1:1 render of the source rather than an upscale. Do not "improve" this to a tall portrait box - it upscales harder and crops the shoulders.
- Each arrow is **unmounted, not disabled**, at the end it cannot move toward: the reference shows no left arrow on the first screen. `sync()` only tracks `atStart` / `atEnd`; there is no page index to keep, because there are no dots to feed.
- The arrows are centred on the **photograph**, not the card - the card is taller than its image by the name and description. Since the media is square, half the card width is exactly half the image height, which is why `top` repeats the card's own `clamp()`. If the card width changes, that clamp has to change in both places.
- The card at each edge is ghosted by `.e-docs__fade` rather than cut, which is what tells the reader the rail continues; the strip is `pointer-events: none` so it cannot swallow a swipe. The fades are hidden at <= 760px - the reference cuts the peeking card cleanly on a phone, and at that width the fade only washed out the one portrait the reader is about to swipe to.
- The arrows are hidden at <= 760px: the next portrait is already peeking, and a swipe is the gesture people reach for. Nothing else is dropped on a phone - every card keeps its portrait, full name and sentence.
- The rail is a native `scroll-snap` container - the mechanism the patient wall's phone rail, the service hero's chips and the visit stepper all share. Do not add a carousel library, and keep `scroll-padding-inline` equal to the track's `padding-inline` (both drop to 16px at <= 720px so the first card lines up with the shell the heading uses).
- Measured with no page overflow and no sub-24px target from 320px to 1920px; the arrows appear and disappear at the right ends and behave identically under `prefers-reduced-motion`.
- **On a phone the section's top padding is 24px when it follows `WhyChooseUs`** (`.e-why + .e-docs`): both sit on `--e-paper-2`, so their two 64px paddings met as 128px of empty ground - a sixth of a phone screen that read as a gap in the page. The subtitle is `text-wrap: balance`; it ended on `team` alone from 360 to 412px.
- `Meet the full team` is the page's one route to `/doctors` and is a 25px text link; it is a 45px target through the halo every `.e-link` carries (see the architecture rules). Measured with `elementFromPoint`, not the element rect, because the rect cannot see the halo.
- **The portraits are the weak part of this section and the fix is new photography, not CSS.** Every file in `public/assets/media/doctors` is 270x260, letterboxed onto white, with backgrounds from white to grey to pink and wildly inconsistent framing - one doctor is a tight face crop, the next a full-body shot. The square box, `object-position: 50% 16%` and the tint behind the image are damage control. Replace with ~1200x1500 studio portraits shot at a consistent distance on a consistent background; nothing in the CSS needs to change when they land.
- This rail is now the only place the landing page introduces the doctors. `SpecialistsRail` used to repeat the first four of the same people in a grid further down; it was removed with the rest of the old tail, and nothing should reintroduce a second doctor block here.

### PatientStories

`src/sections/PatientStories.jsx` is the moving wall of patient quotes directly under the doctor rail and owns the `.e-voices` rules in `src/styles/landing.css`. It replaced a static two-column grid of the same quotes that sat near the bottom of the page.

- It sits immediately after `DoctorHighlights` on purpose: the reader has just been shown who the doctors are, and the next question is what it was like to be treated by them. That is also why it is the one dark band in the upper half of the page - it separates two paper sections and gives the quotes their own room.
- Section copy lives in `home.voices` (`eyebrow`, `title`, `lede`, `note`, `hospitalLabel`, `pauseLabel` / `playLabel`); the quotes come from `testimonials.json` through `getPatientVoices()` in `src/lib/testimonialsData.js`, and nothing is duplicated between the two.
- **Every hospital is on the wall: twelve themes, two for each of the six.** Each item names its hospital by slug (`branch`), and the name on the card (`hospitalLabel`, `Visnagar hospital`) comes from `branches.json`, so the two files can never disagree; an item whose slug matches nothing is dropped. The list is written in reading order - one theme per hospital in the first half, the other in the second, in the site's hospital order - because the phone plays it top to bottom and wider screens split it into two rows at the halfway point, so both rows visit all six and no hospital's two themes sit side by side. The wall used to carry eight themes from three hospitals.
- The head is the page's ordinary `.e-head` label/body grid rather than the centred serif head of `DoctorHighlights` above it. Two centred serif heads back to back read as one long title block. The `note` sits under the eyebrow in the label column, which is otherwise empty on desktop, and **names the hospitals the wall actually quotes** (`{hospitals}`, an `Intl.ListFormat` of the cards' own hospitals, `text-wrap: balance`). Where the head stacks (860px, and a phone held sideways) the note is hidden: it was three lines of small print between the label and the title, and the lede says the same thing.
- The eyebrow is text, not a number. Renumbering `01`-`09` across the page to slot a tenth section in was churn with nothing to show for it, and the three sections above this one already carry text eyebrows.

**Three behaviours, one per device and preference.** `mode` is chosen in JS - `static` under `prefers-reduced-motion`, `step` on a phone, `drift` everywhere else - and is set on both `.e-voices__rows` and each row so the CSS can follow it. "Phone" is `PHONE_QUERY`: 760px, **or a phone held sideways** (`(max-width: 1023px) and (max-height: 520px)`) - at 844x390 the two drifting rows made the section 1025px tall on a 390px screen, under a finger that gets no hover to stop them; the rail is 636px there. The media queries are read with `useSyncExternalStore`, so a phone never paints the two-row wall for a frame first.

- `drift` is the continuous marquee: two rows, opposite directions, a duplicated card set inside a track that translates by exactly one set. No JS ticker and no carousel library.
- `step` is a phone-only self-advancing snap rail: it holds a card still for `PHONE_HOLD_MS` (5s) from the moment it arrives, advances exactly one card, and stays swipeable throughout; the story bar under it shows the hold running. **A phone cannot use the drift.** One card fills the width there, so a whole card was readable for about half a second in every ten - the rest of the time the reader was looking at a quote clipped mid-sentence at both ends. This is the section's central compromise; do not "simplify" the phone back onto the marquee.
- `static` drops the movement and hides the control. **With a mouse it is a grid** (`.e-voices__grid` in the shell, `auto-fill, minmax(260px, 1fr)`: four columns at 1440, three at 1024): a rail that does not move has to be scrolled sideways, which a mouse cannot easily do. Touch screens (`(hover: hover) and (pointer: fine)` false) keep swipeable rails - two columns of six rows was 1875px on a tablet.
- The rail wrap is silent: `step` keeps the duplicate set, so reaching the end of the first set is a `scrollLeft -= setWidth` correction onto identical content rather than a smooth whip back across eight cards.

**The rest of the mechanics.**

- **Each row carries its own half of the list.** Both rows used to carry every quote, and drifting past each other they put the same card on screen twice at once - at 2560px, every card. The second row runs `SECOND_ROW_SCALE` slower and the other way, which is what stops the two rows drifting as a fixed pair.
- **A drifting track holds three copies of its set** (`LOOPS` in the component, `--voices-loops` on the track), and the keyframe ends at `calc((-100% - var(--voices-gap)) / var(--voices-loops))` - one set and its gap. Three copies keep the far end of the track off screen for the whole loop on a 2560px monitor with six cards to a row, with room for the arrival slide (see Scroll choreography); the phone rail keeps two for its wrap. Measured: no track edge on screen at any drift phase or scroll position at 761, 820, 1024, 1440, 1920 and 2560px.
- **Safari sized the track three times too wide, and that is what emptied the rows.** The track is `width: max-content`, and Safari takes a flex container's max-content from each item's own content - the quote on one line, about 1000px - where Chrome uses the flex-basis. The drift moves by a share of the track's width, so in Safari it slid past the last card and left a row (at times both) empty for much of every loop: 18,034px of track for 6,340px of cards, read in Safari 27. **The card carries `width` as well as its flex-basis** (both `--voices-card`), which makes the two browsers agree (6,338px, one set 2,119.6px); `.e-voices__grid .e-voices__card` resets it to `auto`. Chrome never showed the fault, so check a change here in Safari.
- The card grows with the screen again from about 2000px (`clamp(268px, 26vw, max(348px, 17.5vw))`, 448px at 2560; the quote `max(1.1rem, 0.72vw)`): at a fixed 348px a 2560px monitor showed seven cards to a row of six.
- One row on a phone so the section stays a single band of the screen, two from 761px up where a lone row leaves the width looking empty.
- Every copy after the first carries `aria-hidden`, so each quote reaches a screen reader once.
- Motion is stoppable three ways, because moving text that runs for more than five seconds has to be: the `Pause quotes` control, `:hover` / `:focus-within` on the wall, and a `pointerdown` hold. The hold is what covers touch - a finger gets no hover - and on the `step` rail it also keeps the rail from advancing out from under a swipe.
- Cards drift at about 42px a second (`SECONDS_PER_CARD` is seconds per card width). Do not speed either mode up to make the section feel livelier; the phone hold in particular is sized for a 17-word quote read by an older patient.

**Card anatomy and contrast.**

- The theme chip leads the card and the branch closes it. On a wall that moves the reader picks what to read from the tag, then commits to the quote; the branch answers a different question and gets a sentence-case 0.85rem line with a pin, not a second tracked uppercase label competing with the chip at the opposite corner - which is what the first build had.
- The rule above the branch line lands at the same height across a row, which keeps the wall level while the quotes run to different lengths. Keep quote copy between about 16 and 21 words so the cards stay close in height. The quote carries an 18px bottom margin so the longest one never has the rule touching its last line (it did, on a phone), and the quotes and the lede are `text-wrap: pretty` - five-line quotes at 320-360px ended on one word.
- **On a phone the foot is a story bar and a round pause control** (`.e-voices__story`, `.e-voices__toggle`): twelve 3px segments, one per quote, running from the shell's left edge (the card's and the heading's line) to a 44px circle ending on the shell's right edge - the hero film's control, solid glyph, with `Pause quotes` kept as its visually hidden name. Read segments are full, the one on screen fills over the hold, the rest wait. It replaced a `03 / 08` counter and hairline set loose beside the worded pill, which a real iPhone showed jammed against the pill's edge, top-aligned and unstyled, and which did not look like anything else on the site. **The bar is the rail's clock, not a picture of it** (`useStoryRail`): the hold is counted by a rAF clock from the moment the rail arrives on a quote - so a quote gets its full `PHONE_HOLD_MS`, where the old `setInterval` spent ~400ms of it on the scroll - and at the end the rail advances one card (`advance()`, the silent wrap as before). The rail is still the single source of truth for which quote is on screen: a swipe or an advance changes the index, which starts a fresh hold. Pausing, or a finger resting on the wall, freezes the clock and the fill where they are and resumes from there. The clock only runs while the rail is on screen (an `IntersectionObserver` at 0.35), and everything is written to the DOM (`--fill` per segment), never to state. Under reduced motion there is no clock and no control: the segment on screen is simply full. Above 760px the worded pill stays and there is no bar. Measured at 320 and 390: bar and button centres equal to 0.1px, 24px under the card, segments 24-33px wide with eight quotes (14px at 320, 20px at 390 and 57px at 844x390 with twelve); the fill reaches 1.00 at 5.0s and the rail advances; a pause holds 0.99 for 2.5s; a full swipe moves one quote, leaves `scrollY` alone and starts the next segment. Checked in Safari as well as Chrome.
- Contrast is measured, not assumed. Cards are white: quote (`--e-ink`) 17:1, branch line (`--e-ink-2`) 7.6:1, theme chip (`--e-navy-2` on `--color-clinical`) 8.9:1. `--e-ink-3` is 2.9:1 on white and is used only for the pin glyph beside the branch text. On the navy: the head note at `rgba(255,255,255,0.62)` is 6.4:1, and the control's border is `0.45` rather than the hero control's `0.34` because `0.34` measured 2.63:1, short of the 3:1 a control boundary needs.
- The cards carry a shadow because they are the only light objects in a dark band and were lying flat on it.
- Edge fades belong to `drift` only. A rail snaps to whole cards, so the only thing left to fade would be the card the reader is reading.
- `--voices-gap` is 16px at <= 720px, equal to the rail's inset: a 14px gap left a 2px sliver of the previous card at the screen edge after every snap.
- Four of the twelve themes are summarised from public reviews (Visnagar `Staff support` and `Surgery guidance`, Ahmedabad `Doctor communication`, Bharuch `Safe care`); the other eight are drafted and need hospital sign-off before launch - see Known Launch Notes. `Waiting and flow` and `Facilities` were dropped: public reviews of Visnagar and Odhav complain about waiting times, so a theme praising the flow was the one claim on the wall the reviews contradict. All of them are summarised themes, not verbatim quotes, and the section says so on the page.

### Scroll choreography

The end of `src/styles/landing.css` holds **every scroll-linked move on the home page in one block**, and the SHEETS block at the top of the file holds the geometry it plays out. It was built to one brief - the page should feel connected as the reader scrolls from one section into the next - after measuring how the reference sites handle it: Function Health's hero simply scrolls away with nothing linked (no pin, no parallax, measured), Stripe moves background layers at about a fifth of the page's speed, and Apple scrubs every value by scroll position (a headline caught at opacity 0.76 mid-scroll, cards rising 30px, media in 28px-rounded cards). This page takes Apple's model.

**Scrubbed, not triggered.** Every value is a function of the scroll position (`animation-timeline`): scroll down and the next section arrives under the thumb, stop and it stops, scroll up and it plays backwards. It is CSS, so it runs on the compositor beside the scroll - no scroll listener, nothing on the main thread, and it cannot trail a finger on a phone. **Nothing pins, holds or re-times the scroll.**

**Two sheets, at the page's two changes of ground.** `.e-impact` (paper over the film) and `.e-voices` (navy over paper) each carry `margin-top: calc(var(--sheet-r) * -1)`, rounded top corners and a shadow cast back onto what they cover; `--sheet-r` is `clamp(22px, 2.4vw, 34px)`. The hero is extended by the radius (`min-height` and `padding-bottom`), so the sheet's edge is still exactly the fold. The sheets are static layout and apply in every browser, reduced motion included.

**Four moves, one job each:**

- **sink** - the hero's film (`.e-hero__video` and `.e-hero__scrim`, each on its own) travels 44svh over the hero's exit, scales to 1.06 and fades toward the section's navy (0.45); the copy (`.e-hero__inner` from 761px, `.e-hero__copy` below it, where the inner is `display: contents` and has no box) travels 20svh and fades, holding full strength for the first quarter so Book appointment stays in reach; the phone's figures band rides with the film. So the page passes in three depths and the numbers sheet slides over the film. The range is the hero's own named timeline, `view-timeline: --hero block` with `animation-range: exit-crossing`. `.e-hero__shade` travels exactly as far as the page (`--hero-travel`) so it stays under the header while the film sinks.
- **rise** - section heads come up as they enter, each child further than the one before (`--rise`), so a head cascades; the rule over an `.e-head` draws across (`::before`, `scale` 0 to 1; the border goes transparent only inside the block). On a phone the numbers band's proof card rises as one (56px) with its photograph drifting inside it and the link under it 40px, and its Gujarati motto brightens from left to right (`e-impact-dawn`, see ImpactStats). Impact cards and trust tiles rise further per column (`nth-child` per breakpoint), so a row fans out and lands together; on a phone held upright the trust deck does not rise (see WhyChooseUs). The services desk settles from 0.94 and its stage photograph drifts ±5% inside the frame at 1.12 (contrast re-measured over all eleven: eyebrow 5.63:1, headline 9.90:1, sentence 9.67:1).
- **deal** - horizontal rails slide in from the side as they enter (`--deal`, 16vw): the services rail, the doctor rail, and the patient wall's rows from opposite sides. **The patient wall slides without fading** (`e-voices-deal`): it used to arrive from opacity 0 with the whole row moving, so for most of its entry the heading sat over a band of ghosted cards with 230px of bare navy beside them at 1440 - the section read as empty. Now the rows stay put and their tracks slide, on each row's own view of the page (`view-timeline: --voices-row` on the row, which the track reads - the track's own nearest scroll container is the row, which never scrolls). The track's animation list is the drift and the slide (`animation-timeline: auto, --voices-row`, and `reverse, normal` / `paused, running` so the second row's direction and a pause touch only the drift). The first row arrives from the right, so its track rests `16vw` further left (`margin-left`) and the start of the track is never on screen; the phone rail slides the row itself, at full opacity.
- **recede** - as the navy wall rises, the doctor rail and its link sink 14svh, shrink to 0.96 and dim to 0.55: the hero's move at the page's other change of ground. Its range is `cover calc(100% - 100vh) cover 100%` on `--docs` - from the section's foot meeting the bottom of the screen to its foot leaving the top. `exit` only starts there when the section is taller than the screen, and on a 1080p monitor or a 932px phone it is not.

**Where it runs.** Inside `@supports (animation-timeline: view())` and `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: no-preference)`. `SCROLL_DRIVEN` in `src/lib/motion.js` is the same test in JS: where it is true, `ImpactStats`, `WhyChooseUs` and `DoctorHighlights` drop their framer entrance, or both would move one element. Firefox (today) and Safari before 26 get framer's entrances instead (`cardEntrance()`), which replay like everything else in the next paragraph; reduced motion gets neither and the page is simply there. Every move is `translate`, `scale` or `opacity` - individual transform properties, so they compose with framer's `transform` and the hover lifts rather than overriding them - and every one ends on the element's resting style, so a page loaded half-way down renders settled.

**Card animations replay.** The scrubbed moves above replay by nature; the animations _inside_ the cards - the count-up, the procedure bars, the trend wipe, the people and chips on the numbers cards, the six trust tiles' visuals, the services wheel's roll-in - used `useInView({ once: true })` and played once per visit, so a reader returning to a section found it dead. They now use `useInViewReplay(ref, amount)` in `src/lib/motion.js`: `inView` turns true when `amount` of the card is on screen and **false only when none of it is**, so a card never un-plays while any of it is visible (verified: the numbers card held `1,00,000` and 58% with 120px left on screen, and read `0` only once its bottom had left). Each time it goes false `round` increments, and the component keys the count and the visual on `round`, so the reset is a remount at the first frame, off screen, never a visible reverse. A reset is skipped while focus is inside the card, because the remount would drop a keyboard reader's place (the impact chips). `cardEntrance()` is the fallback browsers' card entrance on the same hook, dropping to its start with `duration: 0` while off screen; `DoctorHighlights` drives its stagger from it too. Measured at 4x CPU throttle on a 390x844: the reset while scrolling away costs nothing (worst frame 16.8ms) and scrolling back into the numbers has no frame over 50ms. Under reduced motion nothing resets and 0 animations run. The other pages' one-shot reveals (`Reveal`, the maps, the pathway) are deliberately unchanged - this is the home page's rule.

**Three traps, all found by measuring:**

- **`overflow: hidden` freezes a scroll animation inside it.** `hidden` makes a box a scroll container, and `view()` binds to the nearest one - a box that never scrolls - so the animation sits at its first frame forever (tested in Safari 27: a reveal inside `hidden` never moved; the same reveal inside `clip` did). `.e-cond`, `.e-cond__stage`, `.e-docs` and `.e-voices` are `overflow: clip` for this reason. Anything newly animated here must have no `hidden` ancestor below the page; the hero is safe only because its children use the named `--hero` timeline, which is the hero's own view of the page.
- **A named view timeline's inset defaults to the page's `scroll-padding`** (`scroll-padding-top: 120px` in `global.css`, for anchor links), which started the film sinking 14% before the reader had scrolled. `--hero` and `--docs` carry `view-timeline-inset: 0`.
- **An animated parent is a stacking context** - see the `.e-hero__media` note under Hero. Hit-test every control inside an animated layer.

**The build shortens ranges** (`entry 0% cover 30%` ships as `entry cover 30%`, `exit-crossing 0% exit-crossing 100%` as `exit-crossing`) and drops `overflow: hidden` before `overflow: clip` because every browser in the build target supports `clip`. Both forms were verified in Safari 27 against the production build.

**Measured** at 320x568, 390x844, 768x1024, 844x390, 1440x900 and 1920x1080 in Chrome, and in Safari 27 at phone and desktop width: the sheet's top edge on `innerHeight`, no horizontal overflow at any scroll position, every animated element at opacity 1 and no translate once its top is 35% down the screen, every head rule fully drawn, the header flipping to paper as the sheet's edge reaches it with the shade still pinned under it, the pause control and Book appointment hit-testing and touch-tapping to themselves at rest and 20-25% into the exit, 34-37 scroll-driven animations running (0 under reduced motion), and no console errors. At 4x CPU throttle a real scroll gesture ran at a 16.7ms median frame; the long frames that appeared in some runs had 0-4ms of style and layout and 13ms of script, the rest unattributed image decoding. **Safari on this Mac refuses to autoplay the film** on localhost with or without the choreography (A/B'd in the same page, `paused=true` both ways) - the hero's still-image fallback is what it shows, and that is the existing refusal path, not this block.

## Current Routes

```text
/
/about                (301 to /about/journey on the server; a loader redirect in the app)
/about/journey
/about/vision
/services
/services/:slug
/doctors
/doctors/:slug        (one doctor: the nineteen records carrying a `slug`; any other slug is the not-found page)
/branches
/branches/:slug       (only for a branch carrying a `page` block; others redirect to /branches?branch=<slug>; an unknown slug is the not-found page)
/appointment
/contact
/*
```

There is no `/gallery`. It was a filterable grid of eight stock and event photographs on the old `PageHeader`, and it was removed with `GalleryPage`, `sections/Gallery.jsx`, `galleryData.js`, `gallery.json`, its header and footer links, its sitemap entry, about 330 lines of `.gallery-*` / `.segmented-control*` / `.page-header--image` rules in `global.css`, `PageHeader`'s `image` and `variant` props, and the six photographs only it used (`page-headers/optometry-detail.jpg` and five in `gallery/`; the other three are the LASIK, Squint and Emergency service photographs and moved to `media/services/`). An old `/gallery` link falls through to the not-found page. Each hospital's own photographs are on its page (`BranchInside`), which is a different component and is not affected.

The navigation uses `Our Hospitals` in the header with a dropdown that links each branch through `buildBranchHref()`: a hospital with its own page (`/branches/<slug>`) opens it, the rest open `/branches?branch=<slug>`, which the index answers by lighting and scrolling to that hospital's row. All six carry a page now, so that fallback only serves old links. On a phone, where there is no dropdown, the same link is the drawer's `Your hospital` summary row, and every row on `/branches` is the way into that hospital's page - see "Our hospitals: /branches" below.

## About us

About us is **two pages behind one nav item**, not one page. The header's `About` entry is a dropdown: the reader chooses `Our journey` or `Vision and mission` and lands on that page directly. `/about` itself is a redirect to `/about/journey`, so old links and bookmarks still resolve.

- All copy lives in `src/data/about.json` and is read through `src/lib/aboutData.js`. The pages are `src/pages/AboutJourneyPage.jsx` and `src/pages/AboutVisionPage.jsx`, and both own `src/styles/about.css` (`.ab-`).
- **Both pages are three grounds, in order: paper, then the page's own dark band, then paper again.** Journey is opening / timeline stage / next-chapter plate; vision is opening / mission statements / values and the plate. The ground changes twice and each change is deliberate, which is what keeps a full-viewport dark stage from reading as an unrelated slab dropped into a white page.
- These are the **first inner pages built in the `.e-*` design system** from `system.css` rather than on `PageHeader` and the older `global.css` page styles. They use `.e-sec`, `.e-shell`, `.e-head`, `.e-h1`/`.e-h2`/`.e-h3`, `.e-lede`, `.e-body` and `.e-link`, and `about.css` only adds what those do not cover. Nothing here restyles a shared primitive.
- The page they replaced was a single `/about` route rendering a story panel, a flat journey list and a principles grid. Its `.about-*` rules (about 430 lines of `global.css`), its `about-panel-sheen` keyframe and the `.page-header--about` variant went with it.
- Both pages end by pointing at the other one, and the footer carries the appointment CTA, so neither renders `CTASection`.
- Their SEO lives in `site.pageSeo.aboutJourney` / `aboutVision`. The vision description is written from the approved statements; it used to promise "care of international standards for all sections of society", which neither statement says.

### AboutHead

`src/sections/AboutHead.jsx` is the opening of **both** pages - eyebrow, headline, lede, the stamp beside the lede and `AboutSwitch` - which used to be written out twice. A page passes its own copy block (`page`) and its stamp (`{ label, words }`): the journey's founding date (`journey.establishedCaption` over `establishedLabel`), the vision's three promise words (`promiseLabel` over `promise`). More than one word stacks the stamp.

- **It arrives in reading order**, the hero's `RISE` / `cascade()` device: label, headline, lede, stamp, switch, 80ms apart. **The accent phrase then writes itself in, word by word** (a `clipPath` inset per word, 110ms apart - the appointment head's device), and the stamp's accent rule draws down with its words rising behind it. On a first visit it waits for the opening curtain (`useIntroDone()`), so it is not spent unseen; a page reached in-session starts at once.
- **Each word of the accent is its own `inline-block` (`.ab-top__ink`) and is clipped on its own.** The first build clipped the whole `em`, and a clip on an inline element that wraps is cut to its first line: on every phone, where `an eye / care network` takes two lines, `care network` rested sliced in half. The resting inset is negative so the italic's overhang and descenders are never cut.
- Under `prefers-reduced-motion` everything is simply there (`initial={false}`).
- **The headline's measure is `11.3344em`, never `18ch`** (`.ab-top__title`). It is the same width - 18ch of Inter Tight at 600 - but a `ch` is the "0" of whichever face has loaded, the page first paints in the metric-matched fallback, and Inter Tight is not preloaded: when it landed the measure widened, the balanced headline re-broke a line shorter and everything under it jumped 80px, a 0.11-0.13 layout shift on both pages on every laptop (1366x657 to 1920x1080), in the old build as well as the adopted one. Measured after the change: the same width and height at 768-1920px with the web font (two lines on journey, one on vision at 1440), and no shift on a slow line. Put any other measure on an Inter Tight heading in `em` for the same reason.

### AboutNext

`src/sections/AboutNext.jsx` is the closing plate on **both** About pages - the "both pages end by pointing at the other one" rule, built once instead of twice.

- It reads the same `navigation.header` About `children` that the dropdown and `AboutSwitch` read, so the two pages can never disagree about what the other one is called or where it lives.
- It previews the next page with **that page's own `title`, `titleAccent` and `lede`**, not with a second summary written for the link, and labels the button with the current page's `ctaLabel`. Nothing here is new copy.
- **The button carries two labels and CSS picks one**, the cookie sheet's `bodyShort` device. `Read our vision and mission` is 254px at this size and a 360px phone gives the button 228px, so the label broke to two lines on the SE, the 360 and the iPhone 8; `ctaLabelShort` is the target page's own name (`Vision and mission` / `Our journey`), which is what the header and the switch call it. Below 761px the button is also full width with 20px side padding - the panel is stacked there and its one action takes the column. Measured: one line at 320, 360, 375, 390, 768 and 1440px.
- It is a filled navy panel inset in a paper section, not a full-bleed band. The journey page arrives at it straight off the navy stage, and two dark bands meeting edge to edge would read as one long band with a seam in it.
- Copy left, action right from 901px, stacked below it. Left as one column the panel was two thirds empty on a laptop.
- It replaced the plain `e-link` that used to sit at the end of each page - `.ab-values__next` on vision, and a link inside the timeline's closing stop on journey. A link buried in the last frame of a pinned film is not a page ending.

### AboutSwitch

`src/sections/AboutSwitch.jsx` is the two-up control under the head of **both** pages, and it reads the same `navigation.header` About `children` the dropdown does - the labels can never drift between the header and the page.

- It exists because the dropdown is a desktop affordance: on a phone that choice is buried inside the drawer, so without this the reader would have to open the drawer to move between two sibling pages. Here it is one tap.
- The current page is the filled navy card, so the control doubles as a "you are here" marker. Two columns from 761px up, stacked on a phone.

### Our journey

`AboutJourneyPage` is `AboutHead` stamped with the founding date; the timeline is `src/sections/JourneyTimeline.jsx`, which owns the `.ab-time`, `.ab-stage` and `.ab-rail` rules in `about.css`.

**The page around it.** The paper opening carries the page's identity - eyebrow, headline, lede, the founding date set in the serif, and `AboutSwitch`. The stage then opens on its own **title card** (`.ab-time__intro`): the milestones label, `milestonesTitle` in display serif, the lede and a scroll cue, on the navy. That card is what bridges paper into film - the section head used to sit on paper above the stage, which meant the page stacked two blocks of dark-on-white text in front of a dark stage and read flat. The card is deliberately **flat navy with no glow of its own**: the pinned frame below carries the stage lighting, and giving the card a second gradient drew a visible line across the join. The page then closes on `AboutNext`.

**It is a pinned stage, not a list.** The stage is six screens tall, the frame inside it is `position: sticky` for that whole height, and scroll progress across the stage is what plays the stops. The reader scrolls normally and travels through the years. **Nothing jacks the scroll** - there is no wheel handler, no scroll hijack and no fixed positioning; take the sticky frame away and the page still scrolls at its own speed.

Milestones come from `about.journey.milestones`; the closing `now` stop is the only one whose figures are computed - years from `journey.establishedYear`, hospitals from `branches.json`, specialists from `doctors.json` (the consultants, `specialists` in `doctorsData.js`, the same count the home page prints) - so the count of hospitals can never contradict the rest of the site. The two are flattened into one `stops` array, so the rail, the paint loop and the list all index the same thing.

**The four things that move.**

1. **The photograph wipes open** behind a travelling accent line (`.ab-stage__sweep`) and holds a slow Ken Burns push from 1.16 to 1.
2. **The year rolls.** `YearRoll` is a digit odometer: one column per digit over a strip of 0-9, shifted by exactly one line, each column a beat behind the last. It rolls from the previous stop's year to this one, so 1995 to 2009 visibly turns three digits. A label that is not a plain number - the closing `Today` - is set as a word instead, and `.ab-stage__odo-a11y` carries the year for screen readers because the digit strips are `aria-hidden`.
3. **The same year again, oversized and outlined**, drifts behind the stop (`.ab-stage__ghost`). It rolls with the foreground one, which is what gives the stage depth.
4. **The rail is a film scrubber**, not a menu: the playhead travels it as the reader scrolls, the ticks behind fill in, and any tick jumps to that stop.
5. **The closing figures count up** (`CountUp`, `start={isActive}`) each time the reader arrives at `Today` - the payoff of the stage. `.ab-stage__figures .e-count` takes the figure's own type over the impact band's, and the label rules are `div > span`: a bare `span` rule also caught the spans `CountUp` renders and set the count in 0.82rem grey.

**The timings are deliberately asymmetric, and the reason matters.** The outgoing photograph dissolves over the tail of its own segment and is gone exactly as the next one starts to wipe open, so the frame is never empty and two photographs are never mixed. The copy is a beat behind the picture at both ends, which is what stops one stop's paragraph being read on top of the next one's. The constants at the top of the file are shares of a segment; change them together, not one at a time.

**The paint loop writes styles to the DOM directly, and must keep doing so.** Scroll-linked values handed to a `style` prop get promoted to WAAPI animations by framer-motion, and a stop whose segment starts at progress 0 was mishandled there: the opening stop faded _up_ across the whole stage instead of out at the end of its own segment, so two stops were legible at once and the ghost year of 1993 sat behind 2009. Measured, not guessed - `getAnimations()` on the stop returned an `Animation` with the segment's own keyframes, and computed opacity disagreed with the inline value. Six property writes per stop per frame is the fix; do not move these back onto motion values. framer is still used for the odometer springs, which are discrete and fine.

**Three traps that cost real debugging.**

- **A stop still ahead must have its frame hidden, not just its photograph clipped.** All six sit in one grid cell; a clipped `.ab-stage__frame` stops the picture, but `.ab-stage__media` keeps painting its own panel and drop shadow over whatever is playing. Six shadows were stacking over the live stop.
- **The stops are top-aligned, never centred.** Their copy runs to different lengths, so centring each one inside the shared cell parked the photograph at a different height and the frame jumped between stops - measured at 99px, 147px and 161px for three different stops on one phone.
- **Below 520px of viewport height the stage unpins itself** (`SHORT_VIEWPORT`, checked with `useSyncExternalStore`). A phone held sideways has no height for a photograph, a year and a paragraph at once - it overflowed the pinned frame by 49px - so it falls back to the stacked article, which is also exactly what `prefers-reduced-motion` gets.

**The unpinned fallback is the same DOM, not a second design.** `data-mode="static"` un-sticks the frame, stacks the stops, drops the ghost year and the sweep, and pins the rail to the bottom of the viewport as a jump nav. In that mode the active stop comes from an `IntersectionObserver` on a band across the viewport middle rather than from scroll progress, because there are no segments to divide, and a rail jump is `scrollIntoView` on the stop's own id rather than a scroll offset.

**The sticky rail carries `z-index: 2`, and it has to.** The stops' photograph and copy sit at `z-index: 1`, so without it the next stop's photograph painted straight over the jump nav - on a reduced-motion phone and on every phone held sideways the rail was there, at the bottom of the screen, and entirely invisible. It also sits at `bottom: 0` with its own padding and the safe-area inset, rather than 14px above the edge with a strip of stage showing under it.

**Layout and measurement.**

- Two columns from 1024px **and on any landscape screen** - photograph left, copy right - and one column on a portrait screen below 1024px, photograph over copy. Stacking was keyed on width alone, so a 1000x700 window got a 140px strip of photograph 952px wide. Photographs are 4:3, cropped into `public/assets/media/journey` from curated stock that has since left the site (the old `page-headers` and `gallery` folders), 33-119KB each; the hospital's own files are landscape and a portrait crop of a 770x460 source is 345px wide and unusable.
- **None of the six photographs shows the hospital it describes.** Replace them with the hospital's own photography of Visnagar, the theatre and the Odhav building when it exists; nothing in the CSS needs to change when they land.
- **On a portrait screen the photograph takes the pinned frame's slack.** It used to be a fixed strip (132px on a 390x664 phone, 245px on a 768x1024 tablet) with up to 300px of empty navy under every stop. Now the stops lay their photograph and copy onto their shared grid's two rows (`grid-template-rows: subgrid`, inside `@supports`): the copy row is the tallest copy of the six, the photograph row is whatever the frame has left, and the photograph sits on the foot of that row at up to 4:3 (square below 641px), so it is one size on every stop and never jumps between years. Leftover height gathers above it, where the header returns. Measured: 248px on a 390x664, a 358px square on a 390x844 (its top at 73px, just under the 72px header), 540px on a 768x1024, 579px on an 820x1180. The fixed heights stay as the no-subgrid fallback; unpinned (reduced motion) each photograph simply keeps its 4:3.
- Every stop must still fit inside the pinned frame. The tallest copy is the `now` stop's, and by construction it ends 14px above the rail at every size; what a longer stop costs now is photograph, so measure the photograph's height at each stop before adding anything - on a 375x553 SE it is 112px.
- **The phone-height rules are scoped to `max-width: 1023px`.** `@media (max-height: 700px)` used to apply at every width, and cut a laptop's photograph to a 132px strip beside the copy at 1366x657, 1280x680 and 1440x700 - the most common laptop windows. They now get the desktop frame (`min(60vh, 560px)`: 394px at 1366x657, 560px at 1920x1080).
- **The ghost year's size rules carry two classes** (`.ab-stage__odo.ab-stage__odo--ghost`). Every breakpoint that sizes the foreground `.ab-stage__odo` also matched the ghost, and the later ones won, so on every phone under 700px tall the ghost was a 3rem year hidden behind the copy.
- **`Stop` is memoised.** Every prop but `isActive` is stable, so a change of year re-renders the two stops it concerns rather than all six and their twelve odometers. Measured on a real scroll gesture at 4x CPU throttle on a 390x664: four or five 67ms frames before, a worst of 33-50ms after.
- **The metric under each milestone is a tag, not a button**: the site's tinted pill led by an accent dot (the hospital hero's tags). The outlined pill it replaced read as a control and did nothing when pressed.
- Contrast on the navy, measured: the title and metric 14.3:1 (the metric pixel-sampled on its tint, 14.3:1 on a phone; 4.8:1 worst cell at 1440 only because a cell catches its hairline), body 10.9:1, the accent tag 7.5:1, the counter, the figure labels and the rail's inactive years 7.0:1. The ghost year is a 0.13 stroke and carries no information.
- Measured with no page overflow, no target under 24px and no target under 44px on the rail, at 320, 360, 390, 414, 820, 844 landscape, 1280 and 1920px, in both modes. Re-measured for the phone pass: every stop fits its pinned frame with slack to spare at 320x568 (19px), 360x640 (50), 375x667 (60), 360x780 (84), 390x844 (121), 412x915 (148) and 430x932 (154), no target under 44px on either About page, and the static rail follows the reader (active stop 2009 after scrolling to the third stop) under reduced motion and at 844x390 and 740x360 landscape. Re-measured for the deployment pass on both pages at 320x568, 360x640, 375x553, 390x664, 390x844, 412x915, 430x932, 768x1024, 820x1180, 1024x768, 1180x800, 1280x800, 1366x657, 1440x900, 1920x1080, 2560x1440, 844x390 and 740x360, in both motion modes and on the production build: no overflow at any scroll position, no target under 44px, no console errors, 0 running animations under reduced motion. Tap-tested with real touch at 320x568, 390x664, 390x844, 768x1024 and 844x390: the switch and the plate navigate, and each of the six rail years plays its own stop. Checked in Safari 27: subgrid, the photograph's size and the ghost year match Chrome.

### Vision and mission

`AboutVisionPage` is `AboutHead` stamped with the three promise words, the two statements, the core values and the closing plate. The statements are their own section, `src/sections/VisionStatements.jsx`.

**The two statements are the hospital's own approved words and are quoted verbatim.** They are not paraphrased, shortened or split; the only editorial decision on the page is which phrase in each one carries the promise.

- That phrase is named by `highlight`, a substring of `text`, and is lifted into the accent serif italic. A `highlight` that does not appear in its sentence is ignored rather than dropped or duplicated, so re-wording a statement can never silently lose part of it.
- The emphasis is **colour and italic only, never an underline or a highlight bar**: these sentences run to six lines and more, and a background rule drawn under an inline element breaks at every line box.
- They are full-width editorial rows, not a two-up grid. The mission is 380 characters, and half a shell is not a measure that can carry that at display size - it ran to eleven lines with a third of the row empty beside it. The measure is capped at `48ch` and the marker column uses the same `3fr / 9fr` proportions as `.e-head`, so the statements line up with the section head above them.
- Below 900px the marker becomes a row above the statement (icon, index, kind) rather than a column beside it: a 3fr label column is about 90px there, narrower than the icon itself.

**Each statement lights up as it is read** - the section's one signature move, and what keeps a page of two long sentences from sitting inert. Every word is its own span carrying its place in the sentence (`--at`, 0 to 1); one number per statement, `--read`, is how far the sentence has been scrolled past a reading line 62% down the screen, and CSS turns the pair into each word's opacity, lighting it over the last twelfth before the light reaches it. So the lit text always ends just under the middle of the screen, where the reader's eye is.

- `--read` is written straight to the DOM from a rAF-throttled, passive scroll listener, gated by an `IntersectionObserver` on the list - it is a measurement of the scroll, not render state - and the boxes are all read before any is written. Measured on a real scroll gesture at 4x CPU throttle on a 390x664: every frame 16.7ms.
- **Unlit words keep enough to be read ahead of the light**: white at 0.5 (4.2:1 on the band's glow, pixel-sampled worst cell) and the accent phrase at 0.62 (3.3:1). At the 0.42 the first build used, the accent phrase measured 2.3:1.
- **The ring round each statement's mark fills with it** (`.ab-say__ring`, an SVG circle over the border with `stroke-dashoffset: calc(1 - var(--read))`), and from 901px the marker column is sticky beside its statement, following the header on its own `--hd-slide` timing, so the ring is in view for as long as the sentence it measures. `.ab-say` is `overflow: clip`, not `hidden`, or the sticky column would stick to a box that never scrolls.
- The words are split at render (`splitStatement`), and the count is taken from the rendered tokens, not the sentence: a comma after the highlighted phrase becomes its own token.
- Without `--read` - reduced motion, no script - every word is lit and the ring is its plain border. Checked in Safari 27: the light, the ring and the sticky marker behave as in Chrome.

**The six core values are derived from those two sentences and nothing else** - only what you truly need, affordability, a patient-first culture, evidence and modern technology, one standard everywhere, always learning. `Old case file promise` from the earlier version of this page is gone: it is a real hospital promise and it still has a tile in `WhyChooseUs` on the landing page, but it is not in the approved statements and these six have to be derivable from them.

`src/sections/VisionValues.jsx` renders them as a **ruled ledger, not a grid of cards**. Six cards were six separate objects that said nothing about each other; six numbered rows on hairlines read as one ordered set of rules and scan down a single edge.

- **There is no icon, and that is the point.** Six lucide glyphs in six rings were decoration repeated six times, and they made the section read like a template rather than an institution - the same mistake the hospitals menu made with six `MapPin` tiles and the drawer made with a chevron on every nav row, both since removed for the same reason. The serif numeral is the row's only marker, set at display size, and the copy gets the width back. `vision.values` therefore carries no `icon` field; `VisionStatements` still uses icons, because there are two of them and they are not repeated.
- **Each rule quotes the fragment it comes from.** `source` is an exact substring of the mission or the vision and `sourceKind` names which, set in the accent serif italic the statements use for their own key phrases. That is what makes "quoted from the mission or the vision above" something a reader can check rather than something the page asserts, and it ties the two sections into one argument. **If a statement is ever re-worded, re-check every `source`** - the fragments are verified by hand, not at runtime.
- **The reader's row lights up, and that state comes from scroll position as well as `:hover`.** Hover does not exist on a touch screen, and the section would otherwise be inert on the device most patients use. An `IntersectionObserver` on a thin band across the middle of the viewport marks one row at a time; the mark is the accent drawn across that row's top rule, the same device the timeline's playhead uses. The rows are content, not controls, so nothing here is focusable and no tap target was added.
- The row shape is identical at every width - number, title, quoted fragment, sentence, always in that order. Only the wrapping changes: four columns from 1024px, and below that the quote and the sentence drop under the title. `.ab-values__lead` takes `display: contents` there so its two children can hold their own rows; it is a plain div, so dissolving its box costs nothing semantically - do not do this to an element with an implicit ARIA role.
- Below 560px both the quote and the sentence go full width. Indented under the title they would have about 190px of measure on a 320px phone. The type never shrinks with the frame.
- The cards carry no figures - `ImpactStats` on the landing page owns the numbers.
- A decorative `01`-`06` numeral was dropped from the old cards for measuring 2.9:1 (`--e-ink-3` on paper). It earns its place in the ledger because it is now the row's only marker and the rows are an ordered list a reader can count and refer back to, but it is `--e-ink-2` (7.8:1), and `--e-accent` (5.8:1) on the active row - never `--e-ink-3`.
- Rows align on the **baseline**, not the centre: the numeral, the title and the sentence sit on one line across the row, which is what stops six rows from reading as six loose blocks.

**The opening's promise stamp is the sibling of the journey page's founding date** - same block, same micro-label-over-serif treatment. There it dates the hospital; here it names the three words the vision rests on (`vision.promise`). Without it the lede row is half empty on a laptop, and the two About pages stop looking like a pair.

**The closing plate sits right under the ledger** (`.ab-values + .ab-next { padding-top: 0 }`): both are on paper, and the two sections' paddings met as an empty band 112px tall on a phone and 220px on a laptop.

**Contrast on this page is measured, not assumed.** On the navy: the statements (lit) and their kind labels 14.3:1, unlit words 4.2:1 and the unlit accent phrase 3.3:1, the accent phrase 7.5:1, the notes 7.7:1, the statement index 6.1:1. On paper: value titles 18.2:1, value copy, the source label and the idle index 7.8:1, the quoted fragment 6.4:1 and 5.8:1 on the active row's tint, the promise stamp's label 7.8:1 and its words 18.2:1. One fix came out of measuring - the statement index was `rgba(255,255,255,0.42)` at 3.3:1, under the 4.5:1 that 0.78rem semibold needs, and moved to 0.6.

Measured with no page overflow and no target under 24px at 320, 360, 390, 414, 560, 768, 820, 1024, 1280, 1440, 1920 and 2560px, in both motion modes; re-measured for the deployment pass with the journey page (see its Layout and measurement).

## Services

`/services` is an **index, not a manual**. It shows the eleven services the hospital offers and routes a reader to the right one as fast as possible; what a procedure involves, what to expect and the questions patients ask all stay on `/services/:slug`.

**Eleven is the number that drives every decision on this page.** The build before it carried six services as photograph cards, and that shape does not survive the jump: eleven picture cards is a wall a reader gives up on. The index is a ruled ledger instead, and the result is that eleven services now occupy _less_ phone height than six cards did - 1980px against 2894px.

- All copy lives in `services.json` under `page` and is read through `src/lib/servicesData.js` (`servicePage`). The page is `src/pages/ServicesPage.jsx`, the sections are `ServiceExplorer`, `ServicePathway` and `ServiceEmergency`, and all four own `src/styles/services.css` (`.sv-`).
- It is built in the **`.e-*` design system** like the two About pages, and `services.css` only adds what those do not cover. Nothing here restyles a shared primitive.
- **Four sections, one job each:** who we are (the opening), which service is yours (the explorer), what happens when you arrive (the pathway), and what must not wait (the urgent band). The urgent band is last because it is the exception to everything above it - all of that can wait for an appointment and it cannot.
- The footer carries the appointment CTA on every page, so this page does not render `CTASection`.
- It deliberately carries **no branch cards**. The footer lists all six branches on every page and `/branches` is one nav click away; a third listing here would repeat the landing page's old mistake of introducing the branches three times.

### The opening, and how the page arrives

The deployment pass found the page still: the opening sat there, and the explorer's head, its console and the eleven-row ledger were simply present while the About and doctors pages arrived in reading order. What moves now, all of it ending on the element's resting style and all of it off under `prefers-reduced-motion` (measured 0 running animations at 390, 768 and 1440):

- **The opening arrives in reading order** (`ServicesPage.jsx`, the About and doctors heads' `RISE` / `cascade()` / `WRITE` device): label, headline, the accent `medical word for it.` written in word by word (`.sv-top__ink`, one clip per word, so a phrase that wraps is never cut to its first line), lede, the stamp (its accent rule drawing down, the About stamp's `STAMP`), actions. On a first visit it waits for the curtain (`useIntroDone()` / `isIntroPending()`); in-session it starts at once. Measured at 390x664: head from 0.6s, accent written 1.1-1.9s, everything settled by 2s; on a first visit everything holds at 0 under the curtain and rises as the aperture opens.
- **The stamp's figures count up** (`CountUp`, from `COUNT_AFTER_MS` after the head starts, so the count runs while its words are on screen). **`.sv-top__stamp-label` is a class, never `.sv-top__stamp span`** - CountUp renders spans inside the figures, and a bare `span` rule would set the counting digits as 0.72rem uppercase micro type (the About page's same trap). The digits hold the finished figure's width (`--sv-digits`, `min-width: Nch` on tabular figures), so `11` never shoves `services` along as it passes from one digit to two.
- **The iris draws itself in** (`IrisMark`'s `start` / `delay` props) - see IrisMark.
- **The console switches on the first time it is on screen** (`data-on` / `data-focus` from an `IntersectionObserver` at 0.3 in `ServiceExplorer`): its navy-teal-accent rule draws across, and **the eye chart pulls into focus** the way a refraction test hunts for the sharpest line - blurred, sharp, a little past it, sharp (`sv-focus`, 1.5s after a 250ms beat, `--sv-focus` 7px, 4px on the small readout). It runs once: `data-focus` is taken off after `CHART_FOCUS_MS`, so switching back to the symptoms route never replays it and a hover after it is the chart's own again. The fill is `backwards`, never `both` - a held `blur(0)` would override every symptom's filter. Measured: 5.5px -> sharp -> 1.8px -> sharp, then `filter: none`.
- **Heads, the console and the ledger rise as the reader scrolls** - the home page's model (`animation-timeline: view()`, the block before the reduced-motion rules at the end of `services.css`): the explorer's and the pathway's heads cascade and their rules draw across (the border goes transparent only inside the block), the console rises over the first half of its entry, and the ledger's run headings and rows come up one by one. Scrubbed, on the compositor, nothing pinned; Firefox gets the page static. The phone grid, the answer cards, the pathway's route and the urgent band keep their own entrances. Measured at 1440, 768 and 390: every element in the upper 62% of the screen at opacity 1 with no translate at every scroll position; the same frames with the rises on and off (A/B at 4x CPU throttle). **Traps**: after an instant `scrollTo` or `scrollIntoView`, a rect read in the same task sees the console at its old scroll-driven offset (56px, exactly `--sv-rise`) - re-read positions after a frame or a probe clicks the chip above the one it meant; and none of this may sit under an `overflow: hidden` ancestor (`view()` binds to the nearest scroll container - the home page's trap).

### The eleven services and their categories

`services.json` `items` is the single ordered list, and each item carries a `category` matched against `page.categories`:

- **Surgical care** - Cataract Surgery, LASIK & Refractive Surgery
- **Specialty clinics** - Retina Services, Comprehensive Glaucoma Care, Oculoplasty
- **Children and squint** - Pediatric Eye Care, Myopia Clinic, Squint Services
- **Everyday care** - Comprehensive Eye Examination, Optical Services
- **Urgent** - Emergency Eye Care

**Five of these replaced six older slugs, and every reference to them was remapped rather than left to rot:** `phaco-cataract-surgery` to `cataract-surgery`, `lasik-excimer` and `bladeless-robotic-laser-surgery` both to `lasik-refractive-surgery`, `medical-retina` to `retina-services`, `general-ophthalmology-opd` to `comprehensive-eye-examination`; `glaucoma-care` kept its slug. The remap touched `navigation.json`'s footer Treatments column, every `slug` and `href` in `home.json`, and `public/sitemap.xml`. Two condition slides ended up listing the same treatment twice once the two laser slugs collapsed into one, and those duplicates were removed. **`Bladeless Robotic Laser Surgery` is no longer a service of its own** - bladeless femtosecond work is a technique inside cataract and LASIK, and it is named in the `featureBullets` of both. The hero still uses `heroes/bladeless-robotic-laser-surgery.jpg`; that is a filename, not a slug, and must not be renamed with them.

### ServiceExplorer

`src/sections/ServiceExplorer.jsx` is the whole middle of the page: three routes into one list, and the list itself.

**It was two sections and became one.** A finder rendered its own answer card, and an index a thousand pixels below rendered a second card for the same services - so a reader met the same object twice and had to work out which one was the answer. Merging them removed a redundant component, took the page from 6322px to roughly 5900px, and turned two parallel tools into one instrument.

- **Three routes, one list:** `page.finder.modes` is `notice` (13 symptom chips), `part` (the eye diagram) and `kind` (the five categories). Every route narrows the same list underneath. Whatever the reader picks, the answer is the list.
- **One narrowing at a time.** Choosing in any route clears the other two, and switching route clears everything. Three filters that stack would need explaining; three that replace each other never do.
- **The status line is not decoration.** `Showing 3 of 11`, the criterion in teal, and a Clear button. Without it a narrowed list just looks like a short list, and the reader has no way back.
- Thirteen concern chips, each naming a service by slug: **every one of the eleven services has at least one chip**, so no service is reachable only by already knowing its name. Seven eye parts, each mapping to one or two services; `emergency-eye-care` is deliberately reachable from neither, because it has its own band and must never be something a reader arrives at by browsing anatomy.
- The controls sit on **one raised card** (`.sv-console`) with the routes as a tab bar, the inputs in the body, and the medical note on a `--color-clinical` strip at the foot. Loose on the page the three routes read as three unrelated rows of chips; grouped, they read as one instrument with a switch.
- **The switch is a self-contained pill group in its own padded head** (`.sv-console__head`), not a full-bleed tinted band. Two earlier shapes were both wrong: stretched across the card each tab was ~570px and the filled pill read as a slab; hugging their labels _on a full-width strip_ left two thirds of that row visibly empty. A pill group that is only as wide as its tabs is neither - it fills 42% of the head on desktop and 87-91% on a phone, and looks deliberate at both.
- **Each tab carries two labels and CSS picks one**, the same way the cookie sheet carries `body` / `bodyShort`. The full labels cannot share a phone row; `shortLabel` (`Symptoms` / `Eye part` / `Care type`) fits three across in one row, where stacking three full labels one per row cost most of the first screen before a single chip was visible. Type eases to 0.8rem below 360px rather than a label wrapping - a tab that wraps to two lines breaks the row's height. Measured: no tab label wraps at 320-1920px, tabs stay 46px tall.
- **The phone (<= 640px) has its own finder, not the tablet's made narrower** - the second phone pass, after the hospital said it had no confidence in how the section looked or read. The first phone build was the tablet rail at 390px: a third column of chips cut in half at the card's edge and a group heading cut to `YO`, oval pills wrapping onto two lines, five nested surfaces (card, tab track, tinted well, chart card, blue note band), and nothing that asked the reader anything. Four changes, phone-only except the pager, which tablets and sideways phones now share (the new elements render at every width and CSS shows them below 641px, the pager below 901px):
  - **Each route asks its question in words** (`.sv-ask`, `modes[].question` - `What have you noticed?` / `Where is the trouble?` / `What kind of care do you need?`), in the serif, under the tabs. The tab's short label names the route; the question says what the route wants.
  - **The symptom groups are whole pages under a row of tabs** (`.sv-groupnav` over `.sv-notice__groups`, `groups[].shortLabel`: Vision / Health / Children / Everyday) **at every width below 901px** - phones, tablets and phones held sideways; the phone restyles the options as ring cards, a tablet keeps the console's pills two to a page row. The pager is a native `scroll-snap-type: x mandatory` scroller with `scroll-snap-stop: always`, each page the full width of the card, so a swipe moves exactly one group and a page is never cut at the edge. **The scroller is the single source of truth** (the `ServiceVisit` rule): a tab only scrolls it, `onGroupsScroll` reports the page back, and the indicator follows the finger frame by frame through `--sv-gp` written straight to the DOM (measured 0.41 of the way across on a 41% drag, snapping on release). The group headings inside the pages are visually hidden, not removed, so a screen reader still hears them. **`revealGroupOf` brings in the chip's whole page on the pager, never `scrollIntoView` with `nearest`** (it keys on the pager actually scrolling, not on the phone query, so it serves a tablet too and does nothing on a wide screen): nearest scrolled only until the chip's far edge showed, the pager snapped back to the nearer page, and the first chip of groups two to four was focused out of sight at 375px and up - a Tab walk now lands all thirteen fully inside the pager with the tab following.
  - **One option card for all three routes**: the symptom chips, the eye's part chips and the care-type cards are the same rounded card (58px, 14px radius, the `--sv-edge` boundary) with a ring at the right edge that fills white and draws a navy tick when chosen (`::after`, a data-URI tick grown from `background-size: 0`). The ring is what says "choose one"; a pill that only turned navy said nothing until it had been pressed. Two columns, an odd last card across both (`:last-child:nth-child(odd)`); **below 371px the symptoms are one-line full-width rows** (eight of thirteen broke across three lines in a half-width card) and the part cards tighten their ring. The care-type card swaps its count badge for words under the name (`kindCount` / `kindCountOne`: `3 services`). Measured with `Range.getClientRects()`: no option label over two lines and no group tab clipped at 320, 340, 360, 370, 371, 375, 390, 412, 430, 600 or 640px.
  - **The medical note is a quiet line with an info mark under a hairline**, not a tinted band - the band was the loudest thing in the card and sat between the choice and its answer. The availability note under the list gets the same mark.
- **Below 901px the thirteen chips are the pager above, never a rail.** A two-row horizontal rail served tablets and sideways phones until the deployment pass, with cells computed from the rail's box (`--sv-rail`, `--sv-cell`) and a 36px end fade: at 768px, 641px and 844x390 it cut the third column mid-word at the card's edge (`Eyelid droop or lum`, `Sudden pain or inju`) and at 641px the next group's heading with it (`YOUR EYES AND HEALT`) - the fault the phone had already been rebuilt to remove. The rail's rules, its 720px and 380px overrides and `revealInRail` are gone. Below 901px **each chip carries two labels and CSS picks one** (`shortLabel`, the mode tabs' device): `Blurred or hazy vision` on the pager, the full sentence in the readout, the criterion chip and the desktop wrap; the chosen chip's check sits at its right end over reserved padding, so a choice never re-wraps its label. Measured: no chip label over two lines and no group tab over one at 320-900px; real touch at 320x568, 390x664, 768x1024 and 844x390 - each of the four tabs opens its page, a horizontal swipe moves exactly one page and leaves `scrollY` alone, a vertical drag over the pager scrolls the page; a Tab walk lands all thirteen chips whole inside the pager with the tab following and the chart previewing each.
- The symptoms route's hint carries two strings and CSS picks one **on the pointer, not the width**: `sceneHint` (`Hover or tap...`) and `sceneHintTouch` (`Tap...`), swapped under `@media (hover: none)`. A phone has no hover, and a hint that offers one reads as written for a different device; a tablet with a mouse hovers whatever its width, which is why this one is not keyed on 760px. It sits in the chart's own caption while nothing is shown - where the eye is - not at the foot of the list, where it was once buried under thirteen rows. Verified: touch emulation shows the tap line, a desktop the full one.
- **The chips are grouped under four short headings** (`page.finder.groups`: what you see / your eyes and health / for your child / everyday). Thirteen sentences in one run read as a wall a hesitant reader skips; four lists of two to four do not, and the grouping is the first thing a reader uses to find their own line.
- **The route content slides the way the pill travels.** `direction` is set from the tab order on every switch and fed to the three route blocks as a framer `custom` value, so moving from symptoms to the eye slides the content left and coming back slides it right - a switch reads as moving along a row of three rather than swapping a card. Under `prefers-reduced-motion` the variants collapse to opacity only.
- **Route three is category cards, not chips.** Five chips in an otherwise empty card while the other two routes had a picture to look at was the section's weakest moment. Each `.sv-kind` card now lists the services it contains with their global numbers, so a reader sees what "Specialty clinics" holds before spending a tap on it. `auto-fit, minmax(210px, 1fr)`: three at 768px, five from 1280px. **On a phone (<= 640px) the five cards are a short picker, not a rail**, built from the phone's shared option card (see above): two columns of name and a count in words, the urgent card across both (`data-tone`, never `nth-child`), the members hidden, and one column of one-line rows below 360px, where "Children and squint" broke across three lines beside its count. It was a swipe rail of the full cards, which hid four of the five kinds behind a swipe and then printed the same services a second time as the answers under it - the answer cards are where a kind's services are read on a phone. The console is 403-411px with the picker open on phones from 360px (506px on the 320px SE, one column), and every kind is on screen at once. A touch leaves `:hover` on the tapped card, so under `(hover: none)` an unchosen card does not lift.
- **Both visual routes sit in the same well.** The chart's frame and the eye's `.sv-parts__frame` share one treatment - a soft clinical tint with an inset edge - so the two read as one instrument with two displays rather than two unrelated pictures. The sclera and the chart both read better against the tint than against bare white.
- The console carries **one ornament**: a 3px rule along its top edge, navy through teal to accent. It is what tips a white rectangle over into reading as an instrument, and it is the only decoration the card has.
- **A chosen chip grows a check at its left edge and makes room for it** (`::before`, scale 0 to 1 with a 60ms lead, padding-left 18px to 32px). Choosing reads as a small confirmation rather than a colour flip. Verified: the check goes from scale 0 to 1 at 45 degrees on selection.
- **The readout's figures are set in the serif at 1.55rem** (`.sv-status__num`). The sentence stays in the JSON; `statusParts` splits the template at its `{count}` / `{total}` tokens so the numbers get their own element without the copy leaving the data file. The criterion is a teal chip on a 0.06 tint - 0.08 measured 4.95:1 for a 14px semibold, a pass with no margin; 0.06 measures 5.11:1.
- The filled pill is **one element that travels** between tabs (framer `layoutId`), not a background that blinks off one and on to the other. Because it is a separate absolutely positioned element, a contrast walker reading computed styles sees the button as transparent and reports 1:1; pixel-sampled, the selected label is **14.27:1** on the navy.

**Nothing here navigates or filters on its own, and that rule was learned three times.**

1. The first build **scrolled the page to the index** the moment a chip was picked, which stole the answer out from under the reader and made the answer card pointless.
2. The diagram's `onMouseEnter` was wired to the **same handler as its click**, so a mouse crossing the eye sent the reader to a service page.
3. After the merge, hover and choice **shared one piece of state**, so a mouse crossing the eye silently re-filtered the list - the same fault one level down.

So preview and choice are separate state (`previewPart` / `part`), `EyeModel` (and its `EyeDiagram` fallback) takes separate `onPreviewPart` and `onChoosePart` props even though they do similar things, and the filter reads the chosen value only. **Hover previews, click chooses, and only a link the reader presses leaves the section.** Verified: hovering all seven regions leaves the list at 11 rows and changes no route, at 1440px and 390px.

**The one exception is revealing the results, which is the opposite of navigating away from them.** A choice moves the page **the least distance that puts the whole first answer on screen, and never so far that the status line goes under the header** (`revealFirst`) - at every width, not only on a phone. The wide-screen build used to `scrollIntoView` the count with `block: "nearest"` when less than 240px of the results showed, which brought `Showing 1 of 11` to the foot of the screen and left the answer under the fold: at 1366x657 every one of four test choices ended with the first row below the screen, and on a sideways phone a symptom's single row sat at 436px of 390. On a phone the answers are new cards, so the choice flags `revealPending` and the answer list's callback ref (`revealAnswers`) measures the first card `REVEAL_SETTLE_MS` (260ms) after the choice - the eye route swaps its caption 200ms after a choice, and a hint one line longer pushed the first card 21px under the fold on a 320px phone. On a wider screen the ledger's rows glide into place, so it measures `LEDGER_SETTLE_MS` (480ms) after the choice, and **it measures the first row that is on the new list** (`shownSlugs`): leaving rows stay in the DOM for up to ~610ms, and the first `.sv-row` there was often one on its way out, which read as "already on screen" and scrolled nothing. **A part of the eye waits `EYE_TURN_MS` (1000ms) instead** (`revealResults(settle)`): the model turns to the part first, and scrolling on at 260ms carried the eye off the screen in the middle of its turn. Under reduced motion the eye jumps, so the reveal keeps 260ms. The chip just used stays as close to view as the answer allows - a chip at the top of a tall console leaves the screen, but the status line with the choice named in it and its Clear button stays. Measured with a real mouse at 1024x768, 1366x657, 1440x900 and 1920x1080 on a symptom, a kind and a part, and with real touch on 25 choices at 320-600px: every first answer ends fully on screen, and nothing moves when it already was.

**The list and its pane.**

- **The list is read in runs under a heading, not as eleven equal rows.** The source order is already grouped by kind of care, so `groups` splits `shown` into consecutive runs and each run gets its category once as a `.sv-group__head` (label plus count); the rows under it carry only their own name. The per-row category label this replaced was the same two words printed on every row of a run. Narrowing keeps the shape - two surgical answers still sit under `Surgical care`, and a symptom's single answer sits under its own heading, which tells the reader what kind of care it is. The scroll observer therefore watches `.sv-row` elements, not the list's direct children.
- **The number is global and permanent.** `getNumberedServices()` numbers the source list once, so "07" is the Myopia Clinic whether the reader is looking at all eleven or at the three children's services. Renumbering per filter would make one service two different things.
- Rows animate with framer `layout` inside `AnimatePresence`, so narrowing visibly collapses the list rather than swapping one set of text for another. The active row's title steps 3px toward the reader.
- Chips arrive on a short stagger when a route opens and take a `whileTap` scale, and the status figure cross-fades on its own key so a changed count is visible rather than silently different.
- **The untouched diagram breathes an invitation** (`.sv-parts__frame[data-idle]` animating the globe stroke) and **stops the moment a part is chosen**. An invitation must never keep running after it has been taken.
- **On a wide screen the photograph lives in one sticky pane beside the list, not in eleven rows.** Eleven picture cards is a wall; the pane brings the pictures back and the page pays for exactly one. It is `aria-hidden` - every word in it is a copy of the row it mirrors.
- **Below 1180px, where the pane is gone, each row carries its own picture, small.** `.sv-row__thumb` is a 72px (64px from 980px, 56px from 360px) square of the service's `thumb` - a 240x240 JPEG of 6-10KB cut from its `image` into `public/assets/media/services/<slug>-thumb.jpg` - and is `display: none` on the pane widths, so the same picture is never shown twice. **The thumbnails are encoded, never hand-edited**: `ffmpeg -vf "scale=240:240:force_original_aspect_ratio=increase,crop=240:240" -q:v 4`. `emergency-eye-care.jpg` (the emergency service's photograph) is a four-component (CMYK) JPEG that ffmpeg decodes to noise and encodes to 570KB at 240px; that one goes through `sips` to PNG first and then to JPEG, at 7.7KB.
- **On a phone (<= 640px) the list is a grid of cards while nothing is chosen, and any choice turns it into answer cards.** Both are decided in JS (`phone`, `useSyncExternalStore` over `(max-width: 640px)`, the locate card's device) because they are different markup, not a restyle: the ledger is five labelled `<section>`s and the grid is one `<ol>`, and a section that is `display: contents` on a phone is a landmark with no box.
  - **The grid is the catalogue: three across, all eleven on one screen, after a card-grid reference the hospital supplied.** Four builds came before it.
