It re-runs on every visit to the tab, because AnimatePresence unmounts the panel it lives in; the model parks its canvas, context and compiled shader when it goes and takes them back when it returns, so a revisit is the panel's transition and nothing more (372-389ms to the first frame). It is a small top-level component rather than an effect in `ServiceExplore`, which keeps the timer out of the tab logic.
- **The questions glide open** through `::details-content` with `interpolate-size: allow-keywords`, scoped to `.sd-faq__list` rather than `:root` because the property inherits. Pure CSS, no JavaScript, and a browser without support simply snaps open exactly as before - the `<details>` semantics that section exists for are untouched.
- **Everything tappable gives something back**: tabs, sign rows and the hero buttons take a `whileTap` scale, the sign's ring fills and draws a check, related rows lift their thumbnail and lean their arrow toward the page they open.
- Related rows stagger in on scroll at 70ms apart (framer `whileInView`), and the emergency page's rust band keeps the index's own entrances.
- **`cascade` and `WRITE` live in `src/lib/motion.js`**, shared by this hero and the About, doctors, services and home heads; each head keeps its own `RISE`, because their distances and durations differ.

### Contrast and measurement

**Measured, not assumed**, and pixel-sampled worst-local-cell because the hero sits on photography - a computed-style walker sees `background-color: transparent` and silently measures against the page behind it.

- Hero, worst across all eleven photographs: **10.1:1**.
- Everything else at 1440px and 390px, worst: **6.8:1** (the area caption). Headings 18.2:1, labels and body 7.1-7.8:1, tab idle 7.6:1 and selected 14.3:1, chain step 16.4:1, FAQ question 18.2:1 and answer 7.8:1, action bar 14.3:1.
- **Three sampling traps that produce false failures, all artifacts of the tool rather than the page:**
  - **A wrapper with a styled child cannot be sampled.** Making `.sd-visit__note span` transparent left its `<strong>` painted, and the sampler read 1.7:1 off the glyphs it had not hidden. The note's text now has its own element; measure leaf elements only.
  - **A `display: none` element returns no client rects**, and its zero box samples the top-left of the page - which reported the action bar as 1.06:1 at desktop width, where it is correctly hidden. Skip elements with no rects.
  - **Translucent type must be composited against the pixel behind it** before the ratio is computed. Reading `rgba(255,255,255,0.5)` and measuring only its RGB reported every figure on a navy ground as 14.27:1, including several that are not.
- **Control boundaries need `--sd-edge` (`#7f8b96`)**, the same value the header, the cookie sheet and the services index use: 3.48:1 on white against the 3:1 WCAG 1.4.11 wants. `--e-rule-2` is 1.58:1 and belongs between bands only. There is no navy control on this page any more - the `--sd-edge-light` token the navy-hero build carried for one was never read and has been removed; if a control ever sits on a dark ground here, reintroduce it at `rgba(255,255,255,0.46)` and measure.
- Measured with **no page overflow and no target under 24px** at 320, 360, 390, 414, 768, 820, 1024, 1180, 1280, 1440, 1920 and 2560px, across **all eleven services** and the not-found state, in both motion modes, with no console errors on any route. Re-measured for the phone pass at 320x568, 360x640, 375x667, 390x844, 412x915, 430x932 and 360x780 across the index and all eleven services: no overflow anywhere, no tab label or row title wrapping, the hero's `Book appointment` at 282-371px from the top on every service and size, and no target under 44px other than the visit dots, whose 48px ring the element rect cannot see. Under `prefers-reduced-motion` the card height snaps instead of sliding, every panel renders at full opacity, and the chain steps are simply there.
- **Re-measured for the deployment pass** across all eleven services at 320x568, 360x640, 375x553, 390x664, 390x844, 393x659, 412x915, 430x932, 568x320, 667x375, 740x360, 844x390, 768x1024, 820x1180, 1024x768, 1180x800, 1280x800, 1366x657, 1440x900, 1920x1080 and 2560x1440: no overflow at any scroll position, no target under 44px (hit-tested with `elementFromPoint` over a 44px square, so the dots' ring and the `.e-link` halos count), no tab or chip label clipped, no stop name over two lines or touching its neighbour, `Book appointment` on the first screen everywhere, and no console errors. Real touch at 320x568, 390x664 and 844x390: the back link, both hero actions, every tab, every sign, all six stops, the hospital link, every question (opening and closing), `Ask us directly`, `See all 11`, each related row and all three action-bar buttons land on themselves and do what they say; a sideways swipe moves the step rail and the chip rail by one and leaves `scrollY`, a vertical drag over the step rail scrolls the page and leaves the step. At 4x CPU throttle on the production build at 390x664: the arrival, a real touch scroll of the whole page, three tab switches, the route assembling and three stop presses run at a 16.7ms median with no frame over 34ms; the one long frame on the way in is React committing the route, which the services index has too. New text, pixel-sampled worst cell: stop names 7.8:1 idle and 17.9:1 chosen, the step sentence 18.2:1, the visit hint 7.1:1, short tabs 7.6:1 idle and 14.3:1 chosen, the eye caption's eyebrow 6.8:1 on its well, the causes numerals 6.4:1 in accent, the chart's eyebrow 6.9-7.4:1. Checked in Safari 27 at 390 and 1280px: the segmented tabs, the chip fade, the scroll-driven rises, the route's arrival, the named stops, the causes columns and the whole chart match Chrome.

## Doctors: /doctors

**One head and one roster.** The team is the page: the head names it, and under it every consultant, visiting specialist and optometrist sits in one grid with a single question narrowing it - which hospital. The footer carries the appointment CTA on every page, so there is no closing band.

The build before it was the old `PageHeader` over `DoctorGrid` - nine cards each carrying a number stamp, a specialty pill, a bio, a branch chip row and its own `Book visit` link - with `CTASection` under it. All three are gone, along with about 500 lines of `.doctor-*` / `.cta-*` / `.page-header--doctors` rules in `global.css`, `home.cta` (the only thing `CTASection` read) and `page-headers/ophthalmology-exam.jpg`, its last consumer. `PageHeader` stayed for the not-found page until that was rebuilt, and went with it (see Not found).

- Copy lives in `src/data/doctors.json` under `page` and is read through `src/lib/doctorsData.js` (`doctorsPage`, `getRosterHospitals`, `groupDoctors`, `seesPatientsAt`, `getDoctorPlaces`, `describeInterests`, `isOptometrist`). The page is `src/pages/DoctorsPage.jsx`, the section is `src/sections/DoctorRoster.jsx`, and both own `src/styles/doctors.css` (`.dr` / `.dr-`). Built in the **`.e-*` design system**; nothing here restyles a shared primitive.
- The head is the contact page's: centred, the serif with the promise in italic accent on its own line, and a lede **hidden below 641px and on a phone held sideways** - the filter's own label says the same thing, and on a short screen it stood between the heading and the control.
- **The page arrives in reading order, and on a first visit it waits for the opening curtain** (`useIntroDone()` / `isIntroPending()`, the About heads' device): label, headline and lede rise 80ms apart, **the promise writes itself in word by word** (`.dr-head__ink`, a `clipPath` inset per word - `AboutHead`'s `WRITE`), then the filter's label and readout, then the chips one by one, then the cards on the first screen. `DoctorsPage` passes its own start (`delay`, 0.3s after the curtain) to the roster, so the order holds on both paths - without it the filter rose ahead of the headline on a first visit. It used to rise on mount regardless, which spent the head under the curtain on a link opened straight to `/doctors`.
- **The reference was a filter row over an animated card grid**, rebuilt in this stack rather than ported: no Tailwind, no shadcn and no TypeScript, just `.dr-` classes and the framer-motion the site already carries. Its star ratings, review counts, "gigs" and bookmark button are dropped - a hospital does not rate its own doctors, and there is nothing here to bookmark.

**The filter is the hospital, not the specialty.** Nine people split by specialty gives groups of one (one vitreoretina, one medical officer), and a filter whose answer is always a single card is a list with extra steps. The hospital is also the question a patient actually asks, and it is the axis the rest of the site already turns on.

- The options are derived from `branches.json` in the site's own hospital order, each with its count, and **a hospital with nobody listed is left out rather than offered as an empty answer**. All six have people now, so there are seven chips (`All hospitals 25`, Visnagar 9, Ahmedabad 8, Bharuch 4, Gota 9, Himmatnagar 3, Juhapura 1). A hospital's count includes the specialists who visit it.
- **One narrowing at a time**, and the readout is not decoration: `Showing 3 of 9, at Ahmedabad` in a `role="status"` line, because a narrowed list with nothing saying so just looks like a short list - the lesson the services explorer paid for.
- It is a real **radio group** - `role="radio"`, `aria-checked`, a roving tabstop, arrow keys and Home/End walking the row - because exactly one answer is live at a time. The chosen chip's fill is **one element that travels** (`layoutId`), the site's pill device, not a background blinking off one and on to the next. Verified with real input: a click narrows the grid, `ArrowRight` on the checked chip moves both the choice and the focus, and the `All hospitals` chip brings all nine back.
- **Nothing here navigates, and nothing scrolls the page.** The grid sits directly under the bar at every width, so there is no `revealResults()` to write - measured `scrollY` 0 through a click, a keyboard walk and a clear, at 390px and 1440px.

**The answer is read in three runs** - `Doctors and surgeons`, `Visiting specialists`, `Optometry team` - because on a medical site who is a consultant and who is an optometrist is not a detail, and a specialist who holds clinics on set days is not someone a patient can walk in and see. **A doctor's run depends on the hospital chosen** (`runOf()` in `doctorsData.js`): someone who only visits the chosen hospital is a visiting specialist, so Dr. Dhaivat Vasavada is a consultant under Ahmedabad and Gota and a visiting specialist under Visnagar; with no hospital chosen, the visiting run is the people with no hospital of their own (Harsh Shah, Gunjan Shah, Riddhi Bhatt, Jaimin Modi). The rest split on `isOptometrist()`. A hospital page's team section reads the same rule (`getBranchTeam`, in `doctorsData.js`), so the two can never disagree. The visiting run carries one `note` under its heading (set days; the OPD tells you the next one) rather than a tag on every card. A run with nobody in it is not rendered.

**The team data** is the hospital's own list (October 2026, supplied as a PDF per hospital). Each record carries `name`, `qualifications` (normalised to one style: `MS (Ophth)`, `DO, DNB (Ophth), MNAMS`, `B.Optom`), `specialty`, optional `interests` (the doctors' own "areas of interest", written in the case each takes mid-sentence so `LASIK` stays `LASIK`), optional `photo`, `branches` (the hospitals they are based at, by display name) and optional `visiting` - `[{ "branch": "Visnagar", "schedule": [{ "days": [...], "opens": "10:00", "closes": "17:00" }] }]`, the schedule in the OPD `hours` shape and printed by `lib/hours.js`, so a visiting clinic reads `Tuesday and Saturday, 10:00 AM - 5:00 PM` exactly as an OPD does. The drafted `bio` and `highlight` fields are gone: every word on a card is now the hospital's own or derived from it. The PDF's Sarkhej is Juhapura and Odhav is Ahmedabad.

**The card.**

- A square portrait (or monogram), the specialty in accent micro-caps, the name, the post-nominals, the areas of interest as one sentence (`describeInterests`; a term of 16 characters or fewer holds together with no-break spaces, because `Femto / LASIK` split across lines on a phone), and on the card's foot, under a hairline, **where and when** (`getDoctorPlaces`): `Sees patients at Visnagar and Gota`, then a line per visited hospital, `Visits Visnagar on Tuesday and Saturday, 10:00 AM - 5:00 PM` where the hospital gave the days and `Visits Visnagar` where it did not. The lines are the same under every choice, so a card that moves between runs never changes what it says on the way. The templates are `page.card` in `doctors.json`. A record with no `interests` simply has no sentence; nothing is invented for it.
- **Each hospital named on a card's foot is a link to that hospital's page** (`components/DoctorPlaces.jsx`; `getDoctorPlaces()` returns each line as parts - `{ text }` or `{ hospital }` - from `Intl.ListFormat.formatToParts`, so the sentence reads exactly as `page.card` writes it). The hospital team cards use the same component, so a consultant's "Also sees patients at Ahmedabad and Gota" links the other hospitals. Links inside a sentence (WCAG 2.5.8's inline exception) with a 4px vertical halo only: the foot's lines are 23px apart and a taller halo would put two lines' links under one finger. Touch-tested at 390x664.
- **`DoctorPortrait` is the one portrait component** (the roster, the hospital team, the landing rail, the optometrists' stacked faces): the record's `photo` through `SmartImage`, or - for the 20 of 25 without one - a monogram, the initials (`getInitials`: first and last name) in the serif on a white disc inside a hairline ring, drawn as SVG so it fills any box from 38px to a rail tile (`.e-monogram` in `system.css`, on the box's own tint, `aria-hidden` because the name is printed beside it). Adding `photo` to a record swaps it in with no code change.
- **The post-nominals take their own line at every width.** Run inline they wrapped on four of the nine cards at 1440px and on eight at 1024px, and `Dr. Vishnu S.` / `Patel, MBBS, MS` reads as neither the name nor the qualifications.
- **A doctor is a row at every width** - a square portrait or monogram left (`--dr-face`: 120px, 104px on a phone, 84px at 380px and below), words right - two to a line from 761px and on a phone held sideways, one below; the roster stays capped at 960px (`.dr-roster`, `.dr-foot`), centred under the centred head. **It was a three-column grid of tall portrait cards**, which was right for nine people with nine photographs and wrong for twenty-five where twenty are still a monogram: a 290px square of tint with two letters in it, twenty times, read as a template, and the page ran to nine rows of squares. The 270x260 files are also sharper at 120px than they were at 290. The words column fills the card's height (`align-self: stretch`) so the place lines sit on the foot. Odd last cards are not stretched across the row: the filter's layout animation would have to scale a card's width mid-flight and distort its text. Long titles (`SENIOR VITREORETINA CONSULTANT`) and long qualifications (Dr. Gunjan Shah's fellowship) wrap to a second line on a phone; the type never shrinks to avoid it.
- **Cards carry a resting shadow**, not only a hover one. A phone never hovers, so a bordered white card on a flat ground sat dead flat for the whole visit.
- **Narrowing is the choreography**: `AnimatePresence mode="popLayout"` with `layout` on the cards, so the ones that fail the filter fade and shrink while the survivors reflow into place. **The runs and the page's foot glide with them** (`layout="position"` on each `.dr-run`, and the roster and the `motion.footer` share one `LayoutGroup` in `DoctorsPage`): the foot used to jump to its new place at once, and on `All -> Ahmedabad` a card travelling up from the second row slid across `Book an appointment`. A run that empties fades out rather than vanishing, and the run's count turns over with the choice. **The `<ul>` carries no layout animation** - it has no ground to show, and resizing it scaled the cards toward its middle for a frame or two. Measured per frame at 1440, 768 and 390px through every choice: no visible card crosses the foot's text or links (the one contact is an optometrist card already below 0.55 opacity and fading, for three frames), and no frame over 23ms. Under `prefers-reduced-motion` the grid is simply there - measured 0 running animations through a filter change, with the list narrowed and the readout updated.

**The phone is its own build, and this is the part that had to be got right** - most patients meet this page on a phone, and the first version was the desktop shape with a narrower grid.

- **The four chips are one rail that sticks under the header, never a wrap.** Wrapped they set two ragged rows of two - the shape that reads as a form rather than as a control - and cost 52px of the first screen. The rail bleeds to both screen edges (`margin-inline: calc(50% - 50vw)`) and pads itself back to the shell, so the first chip lines up with the heading above it and the last runs off the edge, which is what says it scrolls.
- **The bar's box is dissolved for it** (`.dr-bar { display: contents }`): a sticky element is confined to its own parent's box, and the bar is only as tall as its two rows, so the rail would have stuck to nothing. It is a plain div - do not do this to an element with an implicit ARIA role.
- **The section's ground goes flat on phones.** The rail is opaque and slides over it; an opaque bar crossing the desktop gradient shows its own seam.
- **The rail follows the header up** (`body:has(.hd--hidden) .dr-filter { top: 0 }`), the device the hospitals index's map and the symptoms readout both use, **on the header's own timing** (`transition: top var(--hd-slide)`). With no transition the rail dropped 72px in one frame when the header came back and the page showed between the two for five frames; now the rail's top edge equals the header's bottom edge on every frame, both ways (measured at 320 and 390px).
- **Its ends fade only where there is something past them** (`data-start` / `data-end`, written straight to the DOM from a passive scroll listener - it is a measurement of the scroller, not render state). The end padding is the fade's width, the services rail's rule, so the last chip stops before the fade rather than under it. Every mask stop is positive: Safari mishandles a gradient whose stops all sit at or below zero, so there is no base rule with a 0px fade.
- **A choice made while the rail is stuck brings the roster back under it.** The head and the readout have scrolled away by then, and a narrowing that shortens the page can leave the reader below its new end - so `choose()` re-anchors, and **only when the rail is sitting on its sticky offset** (its rect's top against its computed `top`). The test used to be "the roster's top is above the rail", which is always true - the readout sits above the rail in the flow - so the first tap at the top of the page moved it 95px under the reader's thumb. Measured at 390x844: `scrollY` 0 through every tap from the top, and 1300 before a stuck tap, 95 after, with the readout back on screen at 159px.
- **The chip reveal is `:focus-visible`-gated, and skipping that guard is a real bug rather than a nicety.** A touch focuses the chip on the way down; scrolling the rail there moved the chip out from under the finger, the `touchend` landed on the rail instead, and the tap selected nothing - reproduced at 320px on a chip peeking at the rail's edge. Keyboard focus still scrolls, because Chrome only scrolls a focused element into view when none of it is visible. Verified with `Emulation.setFocusEmulationEnabled`: `ArrowRight`, `Home` and `End` land every chip fully inside the rail at 320px.
- **Each card rises as it reaches the screen, not on mount.** A run is two and a half screens long on a phone, so a mount-time entrance is spent on cards nobody has scrolled to and the rest of the list is simply there. **`AnimatePresence` must not carry `initial={false}` for this**: it suppresses the entrance for the children present on the first render, which is every card on the page - measured 9 of 9 cards already at opacity 1 before a single scroll.
- **A card arrives in three beats** (`CARD` / `SETTLE` / `LINE` variants in `DoctorRoster`): it rises 20px, its portrait settles in the frame from 1.14 to 1 over 1.2s - the hospital hero's one slow move, transform only - and the specialty, the name and the sentence come up 60ms apart behind it. The scale sits on `.dr-card__frame`, never on the image, so the image's hover zoom never fights it, and `.dr-card__media` carries `isolation: isolate` so the rounded corners clip a scaled frame in Safari. The hover lift is the individual `translate` property, which composes with framer's `transform`.
- **The cards on the first screen wait for the filter; the rest do not.** `shown` is a function variant, resolved when the card reaches the screen: for 700ms after the page is ready it carries a lead (`FIRST_SCREEN_LEAD` plus the head's `delay`), so the page reads top to bottom as it arrives, and after that a card reached by scrolling or brought in by a choice arrives at once (`arrived`, set by a timer in an effect). Before the curtain has lifted there is no `whileInView` at all - framer mounts the in-view feature when the prop appears, so the cards on screen start the moment it does.
- **The trigger is a distance, not a share** (`margin: "0px 0px -24px 0px"`): a card starts once its top is 44px inside the fold, counting the 20px start offset. A quarter of a 470px desktop card was 117px of blank ground at the fold before anything moved.

**Re-measured for the team update** (25 people) on the production build: 25 cards, 20 monograms, no clipped text and no overflow at 1440x900 and 390x664 in Chrome, and at 1280 and 390 in Safari 27 (two columns of 120px faces, one of 104px, the monogram's initials centred in Source Serif); real touch taps at 390x664 on Ahmedabad, Juhapura, Bharuch and All each land on their chip and narrow the runs (8 = 4 consultants + 4 visiting; 1; 4 = 3 + 1 optometrist; 25 = 16 + 4 + 5). The figures below are from the nine-person build and were not re-taken.

**Measured.** No page overflow, no console errors and no `.dr-` target under 44px at 320x568, 360x640, 375x553, 375x667, 390x664, 390x844, 412x915, 430x932, 641x900, 768x1024, 820x1180, 844x390 and 740x360 landscape, 1023x800, 1024x768, 1180x800, 1280x800, 1440x900, 1920x1080 and 2560x1440, in both motion modes, on the dev server and the production build. No card name, post-nominal line, specialty or chip label clips or wraps past one line at any width, every row of cards shares one height, and the chips hold one row on every phone. The page is 4225px on a 390x844 with the first card's top at 409px on every phone size, 3423px at 768px and 3531px at 1440px. Real touch taps at 320x568, 375x553, 390x664 and 844x390: every chip lands on itself and selects, a horizontal drag moves the rail and leaves the page, and both foot links open their pages. Arrival, sampled every 60ms: label, headline, readout, chips, first card and its words in that order, the promise written in and everything settled by about 2s, on both the in-session and the first-visit path. At 4x CPU throttle on a 390x664 a real scroll runs at 16.7ms a frame with none over 17ms and a filter change at worst 33ms; the one long frame after mount is there before this pass too. Checked in Safari 27 at 390x664 and at desktop width: the write-in, the settle, three columns and every on-screen card revealed. Under `prefers-reduced-motion` every card renders at opacity 1 with no transform before any scroll, the promise is written, and a filter change leaves 0 running animations.

- The foot's two `.e-link`s are 25px lines and 45px targets through the halo every `.e-link` carries - measured with `elementFromPoint`, not the element rect, which cannot see it. **Their row gap is 22px, not 14px**: at 320px the two stack, and two 10px halos over a 14px gap overlapped, so a tap in the space between them opened the lower link.
- Contrast, pixel-sampled worst local cell at 1440 and 390px: head label 7.4:1, title 17.2:1, the serif accent 6.0:1, lede 7.4:1; filter label 7.4:1, readout 7.4:1; chip idle 18.2:1 and its count 7.8:1, chip chosen 14.3:1 and its count 9.2:1; run count 7.4:1; card specialty 6.4:1, post-nominals 7.8:1, sentence 7.8:1; foot copy 7.8:1. Chip borders are `--dr-edge` (`#7f8b96`), the header's edge value, for the 3:1 WCAG 1.4.11 wants on a control boundary - do not lighten it to match the band rules.
- Two sampling traps, both artifacts of the tool: read every element's real `color` **before** any glyph is made transparent, or a child read after its parent was blanked returns `rgba(0,0,0,0)` and the accent measures exactly 1:1; and `html` carries `scroll-behavior: smooth`, so a rect read straight after `scrollIntoView` is read mid-travel and a halo probe lands on whatever is really at those coordinates.
- The head, the filter labels, the three run headings and the visiting note, the card's place templates, the readout templates and the closing line are drafted and need the hospital's sign-off with the rest of the doctors copy.

## One doctor: /doctors/:slug

**Nineteen pages, one per doctor, every word the hospital's own data.** A doctor's name is the query a returning patient types ("dr vishnu patel visnagar"), and the competitor research found every rival's doctor pages thin, generic or invisible to crawlers - none with Physician markup tied to a hospital, none naming clinic days. These answer that query with who the doctor is, where and when to see them, what to see them for, and who else works there.

- **Only a record with a `slug` has a page** (`hasProfile()`, `profiles` in `doctorsData.js`): the nineteen doctors. The six optometrists do not - a page holding a name and a degree would be thin, and nobody searches for an optometrist by name - and their cards stay unlinked. The slug is stored, not derived (`dr-` and the name, the way patients search), so a corrected spelling never moves a URL; if one must move, add the old path to `src/data/redirects.json`.
- **The page is `src/pages/DoctorProfilePage.jsx` over four sections**, all owning `src/styles/doctor.css` (`.dp-`): `DoctorHero` (the roster card made large: portrait or monogram, the specialty in the accent micro-caps, the name as the page's serif h1, the post-nominals, the areas of interest, `Book at <hospital>` and `Call <hospital> OPD`), `DoctorVisits` (a card per hospital in the site's order: `Based here` with that hospital's OPD hours from its own `hours` block, or `Visiting` with the clinic days where the hospital gave them and `page.profile.daysUnknown` where it did not; book, call and directions on each), `DoctorCare` (the treatments, as rows with the service's thumbnail and sentence) and `DoctorColleagues` (up to six doctors sharing a hospital, the most hospitals in common first, each a way into their page).
- **The treatments are each doctor's `services` in `doctors.json`**, drawn once from their own listed interests and specialty and nothing more: "glaucoma" is Glaucoma Care, a vitreoretina consultant is Retina Services, a general ophthalmologist is the comprehensive eye examination. "Phacorefractive" did not become LASIK, and pterygium has no service to point to yet. The hospital corrects a list by editing it.
- **A call is offered only to a confirmed number** (`getConfirmedPrimaryPhone()` in `lib/contact.js`): Gota's, Juhapura's and Himmatnagar's operation desk still carry placeholders, so their doctors' pages offer booking and directions and no call until the flag comes off. The rest of the site still prints the placeholders - this page is where the rule started.
- **The head is written from the data** (`getProfileSeo()`, the `page.profile` templates): `Dr. Name, Specialty | Aakash Eye Hospital`, or without the brand where that runs past 65 characters, and the richest description that fits 160 - every interest, then the first three ("Interests include", which stays true of a shortened list), then none, each with the qualifications and then without. The structured data is a `ProfilePage` whose `mainEntity` is the doctor as an `IndividualPhysician` with its own URL and `@id` (`/doctors/<slug>#physician`), `practicesAt` and `hospitalAffiliation` the hospitals, `availableService` the treatments and `hasCredential` the degrees.
- **Every doctor card is the way in.** On the roster, the hospital team and the home rail the name is a link whose `::after` covers the card, the hospital links in its foot sit above it, the focus ring goes round the card (`:has(:focus-visible)`), and the roster and team cards end on a `View profile` line - a finger gets no hover to find out a card opens something.
- On a phone the head is the roster's row - portrait beside the specialty, name and post-nominals, the interests and the two buttons (one above the other, full width) under them; `.dp-hero__body` takes `display: contents` there and returns as the second column from 761px, where the portrait is 200-260px. The hospital, treatment and colleague grids are `auto-fill` (one column, two on a tablet, three on a laptop - so six colleagues close on full rows). The page arrives the site's way: the head in reading order after the curtain, the rest rising with the scroll.
- **Measured:** all 44 pages at 390x664 and 1440x900 with no overflow, one h1, one canonical, one JSON-LD script and no console errors; real touch taps on cards (roster, hospital team, home rail) opening the right profile and on a card's hospital link opening the hospital; the card's focus ring on `Tab`; schema 357 nodes with 0 issues; Lighthouse mobile (devtools throttling) 87-90 performance and 100 accessibility, best practices and SEO, LCP 2.4-2.7s, CLS 0; Safari 27 at 390 and 1280 through a self-reporting page (the row head, the stacked buttons, `:has`, the self-hosted serif).
- **What would make these pages better needs the hospital**: a 1200x1500 portrait for the 14 doctors without one, a short bio, languages, OPD days at each hospital, a "practising since" year and the medical registration number (with consent) - competitors show none of these, so each one is a lead. Add them to the record and to `DoctorHero`; nothing else changes.

## Our hospitals: /branches

**One head and one instrument.** A drawn map of Gujarat beside six rows, one per hospital, and the two answer each other. The page's job is to get a reader to the right hospital and, if that is all they need, to its number or its door without another page; each hospital's own page carries everything else, so nothing about a hospital is written twice. The footer carries the appointment CTA, so there is no closing band.

The build before it was three listings of the same six hospitals on one screen - a tab strip, a showcase card with a Google embed, and six dense cards each carrying every desk number, the email, two buttons and the hours - on the old `PageHeader` and about 800 lines of `.branch-*` rules in `global.css`. All of that is gone, with `BranchCards.jsx` and the `page-headers/hospital-corridor.jpg` it was the last consumer of. The page is `src/pages/BranchesPage.jsx`, the section is `src/sections/HospitalFinder.jsx`, the drawing is `src/components/HospitalMap.jsx`, and all three own `src/styles/hospitals.css` (`.hs-`). Copy lives in `branches.index`; each hospital's place on the map is its `map` point in `branches.json`.

### How the page arrives

- **In reading order, and on a first visit it waits for the opening curtain** (`useIntroDone()` / `isIntroPending()`, the doctors head's device): label, headline and lede rise 80ms apart and **`Gujarat` writes itself in** (`.hs-head__ink`, one clip per word, `WRITE`). The map's draw-in waits too - `played` is its own `useInView` **and** the curtain having lifted, and `HospitalMap` takes a `lead` that holds the whole drawing behind the head - and the rows on the first screen arrive 70ms apart behind it (`arrived` ends that window about 1.2s in; a row reached later by scrolling arrives at once). Before this the head, the map's 1.7s draw-in and the rows all played under the curtain on a link opened straight to `/branches`. Measured at 1440x900: everything held at 0 under the curtain, then head from 0.1s after it lifts, accent written by 0.8s, rows by 0.9s, the outline drawn by 1.5s and the traveller in at 2.4s; in-session the same order from mount.
- **The lede goes on a phone and a phone held sideways** (`max-width: 640px`, and the sideways query) - the doctors and contact heads' rule: `Choose your hospital` over the rows says the same thing, and without it the whole map, the list's label and the first hospital share the first screen of a 390x664 (first row at 557px) and a 320x568 (at 512px). The note under the rows no longer repeats the lede's `books its own appointments`.
- **A phone held sideways sets the title on one line** (`clamp(2rem, 5vw, 2.6rem)`, the `em` inline): the label, a two-line title and a two-line lede filled the first screen of an 844x390 and the map began under the fold; now the drawing and all six names are on it.

### The rows are the controls

- **Each row is the whole way into a hospital**: the name in the serif, the locality, `Head office` where it is true, and the two things a person in the street wants first - `Call OPD` and `Directions` - as pills. The whole row opens the hospital's page through a stretched link (`.hs-row__main::after`) and the pills sit above it (`z-index: 1`); the `View hospital` marker at the right is `aria-hidden` and takes no pointer, because the link is the row. WhatsApp and booking are deliberately not here: the header and the drawer carry both for the chosen hospital, and the hospital page carries them for this one.
- **The lit row is the one the map shows.** On a fine pointer the row under the cursor lights its dot (`previewSlug`), a click on a dot chooses it (`selectedSlug`), and the two are separate state so a mouse crossing the map can never change what the reader chose - the eye diagram's rule. `active = preview ?? selected`. At rest the lit hospital is the one the header has stored for this reader (`getStoredBranch`), so the map and the number at the top of the screen never disagree; `?branch=<slug>` from an old link wins over it and scrolls to its row **two frames later**, because `RouteScrollManager` scrolls every new route to the top on the next frame and would cancel it.
- **Preview is for a mouse and a keyboard only** (`onPointerEnter` with `pointerType === "mouse"` on the rows and the dots, `onFocus` only when the target `matches(":focus-visible")`), and the pills' navy fill is `(hover: hover) and (pointer: fine)` for hover, `:active` for a finger. A tap fires the compatibility mouse events and focuses the link it lands on, and nothing on a phone ever undoes either: **a tap on `Call OPD` pinned the map to that hospital for the rest of the visit** while the rows scrolled on beneath it, and left the pill filled navy. Reproduced with real touch before the fix, gone after.
- **Below 1024px the map sticks under the header and the row passing beneath it is the one lit.** `stacked` is read with `useSyncExternalStore` over `matchMedia`, and a rAF-throttled scroll handler reads the boxes rather than an IntersectionObserver band, because the map's own edge moves by the header's height as the header hides and returns. The reading line is 64px under the map (`READ_LINE`), so the first row is lit the moment the map sticks and each row takes over as it passes - the first version lit the middle of whatever was visible, which on a 390x844 meant the third row lit as soon as the map stuck. **Stuck is the dock sitting on its own computed sticky `top`, never a constant**: a fixed `STUCK_AT = 66` sat under the 72px header, so on every scroll back up - the header returns, the map sits at 72 - the lit row froze while four rows passed under the map. Verified with real touch both ways at 390x664, the lit row equal to the row under the line at every step, and the same in Safari 27. The walker also stands down where the dock is not sticky (a phone held sideways, where there is no map on screen to answer the rows).
- **What sticks is the dock (`.hs-dock`), and the map is the card inside it.** Stacked, the dock is paper - 8px between the header and the card, 10px under it - and while it is stuck (`data-stuck`, written by the walker, a measurement not state) a 28px paper fade hangs under it, so the rows slide away under a clean edge. Cut by the card alone, a row's pills and street showed as slivers round its rounded corners on every phone. The fade only shows while stuck because at rest it would lie over the list's label; the section's ground goes flat stacked (`--e-paper-2`) so the paper dock shows no seam. The dock follows the header up at every width on `--hd-slide` (`top: 0` stacked, `20px` from 1024px, where it used to leave a 108px gap over the map): measured per frame, its top edge on the header's bottom edge to 0.02px through the hide and the reveal at 390, 1180 and 1440px.
- **A dot tap and an old `?branch=` link bring the row to the reading line, not the middle of the screen** (`bringToRow`). Centred, the row sat above or below the line depending on the header, and the walker lit its neighbour the moment the reader touched the page. The target allows for the header hiding on a scroll down past 160px and returning on a scroll up, and the walker stands down for the length of the smooth scroll (`TRAVEL_LOCK_MS`) so the hospitals passed on the way do not light one by one. Measured at 390x664 and 768x1024: four dot taps and a deep link each land their hospital under the line, and a nudge afterwards keeps it lit.
- **Flex, not grid, for the two columns**: a sticky grid item is confined to its own row and never sticks. The dock is `flex: 0 0 clamp(400px, 42%, 540px)` beside the list from 1024px (46% between 1024 and 1179px, where the pills are glyphs and the list needs less), sticky at `top: calc(var(--hd-height) + 20px)`, and full width above the list below that with the drawing's height capped at `--hs-map-cap` so the rows keep most of the screen. **The cap is `svh`** (`min(40svh, 380px)`, `min(38svh, 300px)` on phones, `62svh` sideways; `vh` where `svh` is not understood) because Safari's `vh` is the height with its toolbars hidden - on an iPhone 38vh was a 300px map in a 664px screen, not the 252px a headless capture showed. The three caps sit together at the end of `hospitals.css`, sideways last: behind the phone rule it was a 190px drawing at 667x375.
- **The row sheds its words before its street wraps.** Beside the map the list is narrower than the page: `View hospital` goes below 1300px and the pill words below 1180px (and below 700px), where the pills are 44px glyph circles with the hospital's name in `aria-label`. Measured with `Range.getClientRects()`: no locality wraps from 1024 to 1920px, and `M.N. College Road, North Gujarat` - the widest at 226px - has 240-244px of column from 1300px up.
- A row is 75px on a 390x844 phone (97px for Visnagar's tag), so **the stuck map and all six rows share one screen** there; six rows are 456px under a 269px map.
- **The phone title is two lines, `Six hospitals` over `across Gujarat`.** With the desktop's `16ch` cap and the accent word forced onto its own line, every phone from 320 to 430px set three lines with `across` stranded alone between them; below 700px the cap lifts and the `em` goes inline. Measured with `Range.getClientRects()`: 2 lines at 320, 360, 375, 390, 412 and 430px.
- **Below 520px of viewport height the dock unpins** (`(max-width: 1023px) and (max-height: 520px)`): a phone held sideways has no height for a stuck map and the rows under it - at 844x390 the map was a 156px drawing marooned in an 844px navy card that never left the screen, with the status detail line back across it. There it is a plain card at the top, `width: fit-content` around a 62svh drawing, the detail line dropped as on a phone, and the rows are the controls - the same call `JourneyTimeline` makes when it unpins its stage.

### The map

- **It is a sketch of Gujarat, not an embed.** A simplified outline (`OUTLINE`, projected once from ~45 coastline and border points and Catmull-Rom smoothed - Kutch, the two gulfs, Saurashtra, the eastern border), a dashed route threading the six hospitals north to south, a dot per hospital, and the state's name behind it at a 0.07 stroke. A Google map of a whole state is a grey field with six identical pins. The geometry is a 660x492 space - 60 units wider than the state needs, so the labels on the right of the eastern hospitals have room on a 320px phone.
- **The drawing is `aria-hidden` and holds no controls a reader depends on**; the rows are the controls. Each dot carries a 17-unit invisible hit circle (hover previews for a mouse, a click or tap chooses and brings the row to the reader), and nothing on the map is a required tap target on a phone. **Only the hit circles take the pointer** (`.hs-map__svg * { pointer-events: none }`): SVG hit-tests a shape at opacity 0, so each pin's 30-unit glow lay over its neighbour's dot and the traveller parked on the lit one - Visnagar's own centre answered to Himmatnagar's glow. Measured: all six dots hit themselves at their centres at 390 and 1440px, and a mouse resting on a dot keeps its preview while the traveller arrives under it.
- **The three Ahmedabad hospitals are nudged apart in `branches.json`.** At true scale Gota, Odhav and Juhapura sit inside one dot; spread by 30-50 units each keeps its own dot and label. Label sides are per hospital (`map.side`): Visnagar, Gota and Juhapura read to the left, Himmatnagar, Ahmedabad and Bharuch to the right. **Gota and Juhapura were opened to 50 units apart** (`y` 35.8 and 46): at 36 their labels' text cleared by 1px at 320px and a lit pill for either sat on the other's name, at 320-375px and on every phone held sideways. Measured: no two label boxes overlap at 21 sizes from 320 to 2560px, whichever hospital is lit. Re-check every width if a point moves.
- **Lines and dots are SVG; the labels are HTML in percentages over the scene**, at a real type size (BranchMap's rule). The scene keeps the drawing's proportions (`aspect-ratio: 660 / 492`) at every width so the percentages always land on their dots; where the card is wider than the scene, the navy carries on either side. `translate`, never `transform`, on the labels - framer writes their fade-up to `transform`.
- **The lit hospital's label turns into a white pill in place - that is the map's readout.** A card floated over the dot with the name and the street was built and removed twice: at rest it covered the labels of the two other Ahmedabad hospitals, and on hover it only repeated what the pill and the lit row already said. The pill grows out of the label's own margins (`padding: 3px 8px; margin: -3px -8px`) so the text never moves, and it grows outward from the dot, so a pill on one side of the cluster never reaches a label on the other.
- **It plays when it comes into view** (`useInView`, `amount: 0.35`, once): the fill lands, the outline draws itself (1.7s), the route follows through a mask (a `pathLength` animation replaces the dash pattern and the line comes out solid - BranchMap's trap), and the dots land north to south on a spring with their labels a beat behind. Under reduced motion everything is already drawn: 0 animations, every label and pin at opacity 1, verified on phone and laptop.
- **One thing keeps moving afterwards: the traveller**, a dot that rides the route from the hospital that was lit to the one that is. Each hospital's place along the route is measured once from the drawn path (`getPointAtLength`, 720 samples) rather than assumed from the point list, and the position is written straight to the DOM on every frame of the spring - it is a measurement along a path, not render state. It fades in after the route has drawn and does not exist under reduced motion.
- The head-office dot carries a dashed ring; the lit dot grows (`r` is transitioned as a CSS property), takes a glow and, with motion allowed, two ripples.
- The OPD status pill (`BranchStatus`, the hospital page's component) sits bottom left over the sea - the one corner empty at every width - and drops its detail line on phones. It takes the lit hospital (`activeBranch`), so it is part of the map's readout: the six can keep different hours, and the pill answers for whichever row is lit rather than stating one figure for all.

### Measured

- No page overflow and no console errors at 320x568, 360x640, 375x667, 390x844, 412x915, 430x932, 768x1024, 1024x768, 1440x900 and 1920x1080, and at 844x390 and 740x360 landscape, in both motion modes; no `.hs-` target under 44px.
- **Re-measured for the deployment pass** at 320x568, 360x640, 375x553, 390x664, 390x844, 393x659, 412x915, 430x932, 844x390, 740x360, 667x375, 568x320, 768x1024, 820x1180, 1024x768, 1180x800, 1280x800, 1366x657, 1440x900, 1920x1080 and 2560x1440, on the dev server and the production build: no overflow at any scroll position, no target under 44px, no map label clipped or touching another, no console errors, and 0 running animations under reduced motion with every part at opacity 1 and the accent written. Real touch at 320x568, 390x664, 844x390 and 667x375: every row's name, `Call OPD`, `Directions` and route marker land on their own link and open what they say (24 controls each). At 4x CPU throttle on a 390x664 a real touch scroll down and back up the list runs at a 16.7ms median with no frame over 50ms (worst 33ms in most runs). Checked in Safari 27 at 390 and 1060px through a self-reporting page: the dock's stuck state, its fade, the walker, the `svh` cap and the header-follow match Chrome.
- Verified with real CDP input: hovering a row and a dot lights the right pin and changes no route; a dot click chooses and the pointer leaving keeps the choice; focus on a row link lights its pin and blur restores the choice; a real touch drag over the list scrolls the page 308px and opens nothing; scrolling a 390x844 phone walks the lit row Visnagar -> Ahmedabad -> Bharuch -> Gota -> Himmatnagar -> Juhapura with the map stuck at 65px, then 0 once the header hides.
- Pixel-sampled worst cell, text ranges not element boxes: labels 7.8-8.0:1 (white on the drawing) and the lit pill navy on white; status pill 13-16:1 and its detail 7.7:1; row name 13.9:1, `Head office` tag 5.1:1, locality 7.2:1, `View hospital` 6.2:1, pills 14.3:1, lede 7.3:1, list label 6.7:1 on phones - the map's shadow was lightened there (`0.22` at `34px`) after the label under it measured 4.6:1 in the laptop's deeper shadow; re-measured 6.6:1 with the tighter 18px gap under the dock, and the written accent 4.9:1 (display type, 3:1 needed). Pill borders are `--hs-edge` (`#7f8b96`), 3.48:1, the header's edge value.

## One hospital: /branches/:slug

**Six blocks, each answering one question a visitor has about a building**: what is this place (the hero), where is it and how do I reach it (the locate band, on the page's one dark ground), who will I see (the team), what can be treated here (care), what does it look like inside (the mosaic), and is another hospital closer (the way on). A phone reader also gets an action bar that follows them down.

**One thing on the page is genuinely alive, and it is a fact rather than an effect.** `BranchStatus` says whether the OPD is open right now, re-reads the clock every minute, and breathes a dot while the doors are open. There is no loop on this page except the map's pin and that dot, and both are pointing at something.

**Everything else moves with the reader, and that is what the deployment pass added.** The page read as still under its hero: every section arrived through `Reveal`, which fades from 0.96 and moves 10px, and the hero rose on mount - under the opening curtain on a link opened straight to a hospital. Now:

- **The hero arrives in reading order after the curtain** and writes the city in (see BranchHero).
- **Everything under it rises as the reader scrolls** - the service page's model, in the block before the reduced-motion rules at the end of `branch.css`: every value a function of scroll position (`animation-timeline: view()`), on the compositor, nothing pinned. The five heads cascade and their rules draw across; the locate card and the gallery rise over the first half of their entry; doctors, care runs and the other hospitals come up one by one, further the further right they sit (`--br-rise` per column), so a row fans in and lands together. `Reveal` and the sections' framer `whileInView` entrances are gone from all five sections. Firefox gets the page static, as on the service page. **Nothing animated here may sit under an `overflow: hidden` ancestor** - view() binds to the nearest scroll container.
- **The gallery's photographs drift as the stage is swiped** and its caption rises in on each change (see BranchInside).
- **The map draws itself** when it comes into view, as before.

**The phone is not the desktop page stacked, and that is the whole second pass on this page.** The first build laid every block out in one column and ran to 5450px on a 390x844 phone - six and a half screens, most of it label-above-list - and it read as complex and inert: the first screen offered one button, the locate band was a 1504px wall, and nothing on the page answered a tap. It is now about 4890px with _more_ in it, because four things changed at once: the hero carries every way in rather than one, the locate band became a card the reader taps through, the treatments became uniform rows, and the photographs open. **The height came out of the frame and out of duplication, never out of the type** - nothing on the phone is smaller than it is on a laptop, and the two pieces of copy that were shortened were rewritten in `branches.json`, never truncated.

- **Only a branch carrying a `page` block in `branches.json` has a page.** `BranchPage` sends any other slug to `/branches?branch=<slug>` and renders the not-found page for a slug that names no hospital (the server answers it with a 404, and the page must not say otherwise), so a link to `/branches/<slug>` resolves for every hospital whether or not its page has been built. **All six now carry one**, so every hospital link on the site - the header menu, the drawer, the footer column, the impact chips, the branches index - resolves to that hospital's own page through `buildBranchHref()`. The reference was Disha Eye Hospitals' single-hospital pages.
- **The six pages are one component set reading six data blocks, never six layouts.** Nothing in `src/sections/Branch*.jsx` knows which hospital it is rendering; everything that differs is a field in `branches.json` or a row in `doctors.json`. That is what keeps them consistent, and it is why supplying real copy and photography later changes no code.
- **What a hospital does not have, its page does not show.** A hospital with no named consultants renders no team section, the way a service with no `causes` renders no tab; a block with no `establishedYear` renders no `Since` tag; an address naming no road leaves the drawn map's road unlabelled; and a block with no `services` array lists everything the network offers rather than guessing a shorter one. **All six show six sections** since the hospital's team list arrived; before it, Gota, Himmatnagar and Juhapura had nobody in `doctors.json` and showed five.
- All shared labels live in `branches.page` (button names, section titles, the three `locate.tabs`, the short forms the phone swaps in, the WhatsApp message) and the hospital's own copy in its item's `page` block (`seo`, `lede` and its phone-length `ledeShort`, the hero photograph and its phone encode, four `facts`, two or three `landmarks`, `gallery`, and the optional `establishedYear`, `postcode` and `services`). `src/lib/branchData.js` reads it (`getBranchBySlug`, `getBranchTeam`, `getBranchServices`, `getOtherBranches`); `src/data/README.md` shows the block to copy.
- Built in the **`.e-*` design system** like the About and service pages; `branch.css` only adds what those do not cover. The pages are three grounds in order - paper, the navy locate band, paper again - so the dark band reads as deliberate rather than dropped in.
- It carries **no branch cards** (the footer lists all six and `/branches` is one click away), and **does not re-explain the six steps of a visit**.

### BranchHero

`src/sections/BranchHero.jsx` is the service hero's shape, reused on purpose: an editorial head on warm paper, then one wide media card with the hospital's own photograph and a rail of glass facts along its foot.

- **The headline is the network's name with the city in italic accent** - "Aakash Eye Hospital / _Visnagar_" - because that is how the hospital is spoken of, and the city is the one word that changes from page to page. `max-width: 20ch` keeps the name on one line from tablet up; below that it wraps to two and the city takes a third. There is a real space before the `em` - the name read `Aakash Eye HospitalVisnagar` to a screen reader until the deployment pass.
- **It arrives in reading order, and on a first visit it waits for the opening curtain** (`useIntroDone()` / `isIntroPending()`, the service hero's code line for line): the way back, the headline, the sentence and the actions rise 90ms apart, **the city writes itself in** (`.br-hero__ink`, one `WRITE` clip per word), then the frame lifts in, its chips follow one by one and the photograph settles. It used to rise on mount, which spent it under the curtain. Measured at 390x664 on a first visit: held at 0 until the curtain goes, head from 0.6s, city written 1.2-1.6s, chips by 1.4s. Under reduced motion it is simply there with the city written.
- The two glass tags are derived (head office or branch; `Since <year>`), and the four chips are the item's `facts` - the only list on the page that says what this building offers. Below 640px the tags go.
- **Below 901px the chips are a rail that runs to the frame's edges and fades at an end only where there is more past it** (`.br-chips__wrap`, `data-start` / `data-end` from a passive scroll listener) - the service hero's device, which this hero was missing: the second chip stopped at the foot's padding cut mid-word (`Separ` / `LASIK`) with nothing saying the rail went on, on every hospital at every phone and tablet width. The fade is a shade on the wrapper, never a `mask-image` on the rail, which would stop the chips' glass blurring the photograph.
- **A phone held sideways** (`(max-width: 1179px) and (max-height: 520px)`, the service hero's rule) sets the title on one line at `clamp(2rem, 5vw, 2.6rem)` with the city inline, takes the short sentence, and returns the frame to `21/9`: at 844x390 `Book at Visnagar` sat under the fold and a 3:2 frame was 541px tall. Below 641px the appointment and the three tiles share one row (`minmax(0, 1fr) repeat(3, 76px)`) so `Book at Himmatnagar` holds one line. Measured: the booking on the first screen for all six at 844x390, 740x360, 667x375 and 568x320.
- **Every photograph on all six pages is the hospital's own, and four hospitals now have their own shoot.** Visnagar's six files live in `public/assets/media/branches/visnagar`: `facade-2000` / `facade-1000` (the building straight on, the Gujarati signboard centred), `waiting-hall-2000` / `waiting-hall-1000` (the double-height hall under the window wall), `atrium` (the same hall from the first floor), `first-floor` (the walkway over it), `entrance` (the door and the stone lettering from the road) and `reception` (the OPD doors and the patient notice board). The curated stock they replaced - `skylight`, `exam-room`, `street`, and the earlier `atrium` and `facade` cut from a 16320x12240 original - is deleted. **Ahmedabad (Odhav) has its own set in `branches/ahmedabad`**: `facade-1280` / `facade-1000` (the signboard on Siddhi Vinayak Arcade with the HDFC Bank branch under it - the street landmark a visitor actually looks for), `reception` (two patients at the counter, backs to the camera), `waiting-area`, `consulting-room` (the slit lamp and desk) and `retina-imaging` (the OCT with a scan on screen). **Juhapura (Sarkhej) has its own in `branches/juhapura`**: `facade-1600` / `facade-1000` (the corner of Amber Tower with the signboard wrapping it), `entrance` (the garlanded door, portrait), and from a second batch of six interiors, `reception` (the counter under the lit emblem and the `Your Eyes In Safe Hand` fascia, portrait), `waiting-hall` (the rows of chairs under the wood panelling, the one landscape frame) and `consulting-rooms` (the corridor of glass-fronted rooms, portrait). The `street` frame (the same corner as the facade, wider) left the gallery when the interiors arrived and its file is deleted: the hero already shows that corner, and the door is the one exterior fact the facade does not carry. Two of the six were left out - a second angle of the same hall and a small side waiting area. **The optical counter (`optical.jpg`, portrait, no price banner) is the gallery's second frame**: it was added by hand as a raw `spectacles.jpeg` with the entrance's caption and alt copied onto it and the camera watermark still in its corner, and the deployment pass cropped it to the recipe below and gave it its own words. **Every frame in that batch carries a `Galaxy S24 FE` camera watermark in its lower-left corner** (rows 1485-1509 of a 1200x1600, 1085-1109 of a 1600x1200), so each is cropped to 1200x1470 or 1600x1067 before encoding - floor only - and the crop must be redone from the source if a frame is replaced. Twenty-two Odhav frames were supplied and four were chosen for the gallery; the rest either repeat one of these, frame patients' faces at close range (the waiting hall, a patient at the autorefractor), or show a back office. The facade chosen for Odhav is the wide one because it is the only one whose whole signboard fits inside the phone's 5:6 crop - the tighter frames cut it to `AKASH EYE HOSPIT`. Measured with the crop windows drawn over each candidate, not by eye. **Himmatnagar has its own set in `branches/himmatnagar`**, from a new fit-out photographed before it opened, so no patient is in any frame: `facade-1600` / `facade-1000` (Upadhyay M.A. Corporate House with the red Gujarati signboard across its glass front), `reception` (the desk under backlit wood slats and the rows of waiting chairs), `consulting-room` (the slit lamp and autorefractor beside the desk, portrait), `operation-theatre`, `recovery-room` (the beds after surgery, portrait) and `optical` (the counter and the lit frame walls, portrait). **It arrived in a folder named `BHARUCH_branch_images`** - the signboards (the Gujarati reads "Aakash Eye Hospital Himmatnagar") and the building's name, which is Himmatnagar's address, are what placed it, and the user confirmed. Read the signboards before trusting a folder's name. Of the sixteen frames, six are other angles of the same doctor's cabins, one repeats the waiting hall, and three are social-media tiles with a caption and an inset photo baked in (`Hospital Building`, `Operation Theatre (OT)`); the theatre is the one tile used, cropped inside its white rounded border and above its caption (1152x864 from x 64, y 211 of the 1280 square). `reception` is cut to its left 1100px so a phone's near-square stage centres on the desk rather than the chairs - the strip lost is the edge of the optical wall, which has its own frame.
- **A hospital's hero is its facade only when the facade is its own. Bharuch and Gota still open on Visnagar's waiting hall**: a photograph of the Visnagar building on the Bharuch page would tell a patient that is the building they are looking for, and an interior claims nothing about a street. That is also why those two carry no `entrance` photograph and their `imageAlt` and gallery captions say `an Aakash Eye Hospital`, never a city. Every hero carries a large and a 1000px encode that a `<picture>` serves below 900px, where the frame is nearly square.
- **The photographs are encoded from the hospital's sources, never hand-edited.** Visnagar's nine sources are 4160x3120 webp in `~/Downloads/archive`; the recipe is Pillow, `LANCZOS`, `progressive`, `optimize`: heroes at 2000px q68 and 1000px q74-76, gallery frames at 1280px q70-72. They are phone photographs and expensive to compress - the same quality that costs 120KB on a studio shot costs 200KB here, which is why the gallery stops at 1280px rather than matching the hero. **The Odhav and Sarkhej sources arrived as WhatsApp forwards** (`~/Desktop/AAKASH_HOSPITAL_BRANCHES_PHOTOS`, 1280x960 and 1599x720 for Odhav, 1600x1068 and a 1066x1600 portrait for Sarkhej), so their heroes are encoded at the source width (`facade-1280`, `facade-1600`) rather than upscaled to 2000; ask for the camera originals and re-encode when they come. The 1599x720 frames are the phone's 20:9 mode and are kept whole - `cover` crops them to the stage and the viewer shows all of them. The second Sarkhej batch is 1200x1600 / 1600x1200 from the same route, encoded at source width after the watermark crop, q72. **That Desktop folder is not readable from this process** (macOS TCC denies the terminal `~/Desktop` outside the project). A Finder `duplicate` through `osascript` reached the first batch and has failed since (-5000, or -15266 with an empty folder left behind); Finder can still list the names, so ask for the folder in `~/Downloads` and work from a copy in the scratchpad.
- **Himmatnagar's facade is portrait, so its framing is baked into the two encodes.** The source is a 2880x5120 `_enhanced_HQ` AI upscale of a 720x1280 WhatsApp frame - soft at 100%, so it is encoded at 1600 and 1000px, never at 2880 - and the hero's one `object-position: 50% 56%` was set for landscape facades. `facade-1600` is cut to exactly 21:9 (the band from 29% of the source's height), so the CSS crops nothing and the signboard sits at 28-44% of the frame, clear of the chips (from 67%). `facade-1000` runs from just above the roof to the shutters (11% to 72.6% of the height), which at 56% gives the phone's 5:6 the whole building with both names, the tablet's 3:2 a band that starts under `Upadhyay M.A. Corporate House` rather than through its letters, and a sideways phone's 21:9 the signboard between the tags and the chips (which start at 57% there). Verified at 1440x900, 768x1024, 390x664 and 844x390. Re-derive both from the source if the frame ratios or the foot's height change.
- `reception.jpg` is the one crop: the source frames the OPD doors over a close row of waiting patients, so it is cut to the upper-left 62% at 4:3, which keeps the doors and the notice board and leaves the nearest faces out. Redo it from the source rather than editing the JPEG.
- The signboard sits at 58% of the facade's height and the mural band at about 40% of the waiting hall's, so `object-position: 50% 56%` holds both in every crop from 21:9 down to 5:6. Verified at 320, 390 and 1440px on both heroes.
- `BranchActions` is the service page's action row for a fixed hospital: the number itself sits under `Call OPD` (there is no branch to name), `Book at Visnagar` carries `?branch=<slug>`, and Directions is the fourth action. Every caption under the quiet actions is the city, not the locality - `M.N. College Road, North Gujarat` pushed Directions onto a second row at 768px.
- **Below 640px that row becomes a foot of tiles: the appointment across the top, then Call OPD / WhatsApp / Directions as three icon-over-label tiles.** The first build hid all three there, on the argument that the sticky bar carries them - but that bar only rises once the hero has scrolled away, so the phone's first screen offered a single action and the page opened like a brochure. Now every way into this hospital is on the first screen and the bar still watches this row, so the two are never visible at once. The tiles are 72px tall with one-line labels at 320px, the glyph drops its own ring because the tile carries the boundary, and the number under `Call OPD` is dropped - the tile is the action, not a directory entry.
- **The phone gets the hospital's own shorter opening line** (`ledeShort`), swapped with CSS at 640px like the cookie sheet's `bodyShort`. Two sentences is the right opening on a laptop and four lines of preamble before the first action on a phone.
- **The photograph settles rather than appearing**: one 10s push from 1.05 to 1, never repeating, keyed on the frame's arrival (`.br-frame[data-in]`) and on the individual `scale` property so it composes with framer's lift. One slow move on one image is what keeps the opening from reading as a printed page; a loop would be decoration. Under `prefers-reduced-motion` it is simply there at its final size.
- Contrast, pixel-sampled worst cell on the facade: tags 9.9-10.2:1, the foot label 10.8-17.4:1, chip text 17.2:1 at 390 and 1440px.

### BranchLocate and BranchMap

`src/sections/BranchLocate.jsx` is the navy band: a drawn map beside the address, the landmarks, the desk numbers and the hours. `src/components/BranchMap.jsx` is the drawing. **This is the page's signature section and the one the reader is meant to enjoy.**

**Under the heading is a live status, not a sentence.** A paragraph used to sit there explaining that the map's landmarks come from the postal address - true, and nothing a reader could act on. `src/components/BranchStatus.jsx` replaced it in the same position: a pill reading `OPD open now` / `OPD closed now` / `OPD closed today`, with `Closes 6:00 PM`, `Opens 9:00 AM` or the Sunday note on a line beneath it.

- **The clock is the hospital's, never the device's.** A patient in Visnagar and a son in Toronto must be told the same thing, so the weekday and the time are read through `Intl.DateTimeFormat` in the hospital's time zone (`hospitalNow` in `lib/hours.js`). It re-resolves every 60s, so a page left open crosses 6:00 PM correctly. **The state is resolved in render, not held**: the pill takes a `branch` prop and the branch changes under it on the switchboard, the slip and the index map, so a held value would go on describing the old hospital until the next tick - the tick is a bare counter that only forces the re-render. **The prerendered pill states the hours instead** (`OPD hours` over the hospital's first row, `8:00 AM - 8:00 PM`): the build cannot know the time it will be read at, so the live answer arrives as React adopts the page, under the curtain or the veil.
- **The state is a pill and the detail is a line under it.** Both inside one pill, `Emergency and prior appointments only` wrapped it to five lines on a phone and the shape stopped reading as a status.
- It sits in the body column under the heading at every width. In the label column it read _ahead_ of the heading the moment the two stacked on a phone.
- **The dot is the glance, the words are the meaning** - colour is never the only thing saying whether the doors are open. Green `#57d39b` breathes a 2.6s ring while open, and `prefers-reduced-motion` stops it dead without changing what the pill says. Pill 10.8:1, detail 8.2:1, pixel-sampled.
- **The hospital's own `hours` block is the structured truth and every consumer reads it through `lib/hours.js`**: this pill, the Hours tab's printed rows, `JsonLd`'s `openingHoursSpecification` (each hospital's node carries its own; the organisation node carries the head office's), the appointment flow and the footer. A schedule with a Saturday half day or a lunch break is a second slot, and `getOpenState()` answers between slots (`Opens 4:00 PM`), after the last slot (the next open day's first opening) and on a day with no slot (`closedToday`, worded with the hospital's `closedNote`). The Hours tab's hedge is the block's `note`, and a hospital without one prints none.

- **The map is a sketch of the postal address, not an embed.** Every element on it is a fact from the address - M.N. College Road, New Court, GEB, the PIN - laid out the way a local would draw it on the back of a card. A Google map at this size is a grey field with one pin and tells a first-time visitor nothing they can repeat to a driver; the sketch names the things they will look for. `Show live map` swaps the sketch for the Google embed inside the same frame (`LazyMapFrame`, loading on toggle), and `Show sketch` brings it back.
- **It plays when it comes into view** (`useInView`, `amount: 0.45`, once): the road's casing draws across, the dashed centre line follows, the cross street draws, the two landmark blocks pop onto the road's own points, the hospital block fills under an accent glow, the pin drops on a spring, three ripples run from its foot, and a dot travels the road to the hospital (`animateMotion`, `keyPoints 0;0.6`) - the thing that says "this is how you arrive". Under `prefers-reduced-motion` every stroke is already drawn, there are no ripples and no traveller; verified 0 animations and every label at opacity 1.
- **The dashed centre line is revealed through a mask, not a `pathLength` animation.** framer's `pathLength` replaces `stroke-dasharray` with its own and the line came out solid; a masked path draws along the road and keeps its dashes.
- **Lines and shapes are SVG; the labels are HTML placed in percentages over it**, because SVG text scales with the viewBox and 13px in a 600px drawing is 8px on a phone. HTML labels keep a real type size at every width.
- **Centre the labels with `translate`, never `transform`.** framer-motion writes each label's fade-up to `transform`, and a CSS `transform: translate(-50%, -50%)` was thrown away with it - every label landed off its block until the audit caught it. The pin card is centred above the pin (`translate: -50% -100%`) with a `::after` pointer.
- **The place labels are anchored to the frame's edges, not centred on their blocks.** Centred, a long name ran off the drawing - `Healing Touch Hospital` is 148px on a 358px map and was cut in half at Bharuch's left edge, a fault Visnagar's short labels (`New Court`, `GEB office`) never exposed. Anchored left and right with a `46%` cap, every label grows inward and wraps rather than clipping, whatever an address happens to name. Re-check clipping on **all six** hospitals after touching this, not just one. **The right-hand label hangs from its top edge (`translate: 0 0`, `top: 47.5%`) rather than centring on its block's row**: a name that wraps then grows downward into open ground instead of upward into the hospital block - `Gujarat Vepari Maha Mandal` on two lines reached the hospital's own block and the traveller on the Ahmedabad map at 320-360px. The left label stays centred; its block is at the foot of the drawing and nothing sits above it.
- **The traveller stops where the road meets the hospital block, not inside it.** The road's centre line crosses the block's edge at 0.55 of its length (measured with `getPointAtLength`, not read off the curve's parameter - the two differ by about 0.1 here), so `keyPoints="0;0.53"` lands the dot on the road touching the block from outside, which reads as arriving at the door. At the 0.6 it shipped with it came to rest inside the building, and a white dot in the corner of the hospital read as a stray mark on every one of the six maps.
- **Every corner is spoken for, and it was measured, not guessed.** The stamp (`PIN 384315`) is top left, the toggle bottom right, New Court bottom left, and the road label sits at 59% down the left edge. The toggle started bottom left and covered New Court; moved top left it covered the hospital's own label on a phone; the stamp started bottom right under the ghost name, then top right where it met the pin card at 320px. A scale bar and a compass were drawn and removed - the sketch has neither scale nor orientation, and a map that implies both is a lie. Verified with a bounding-box walk: no two labels overlap and none is clipped at 320, 360, 390, 412, 768, 1440 and 2560px. On a small map the stamp drops its `PIN` word and the place labels cap at 40% - a 288px map has no room for either.
- **The small-map rules are container queries on the map's own width** (`container: br-map / inline-size`; `@container br-map (max-width: 328px)` and `(max-width: 388px)`, the maps a 360px and a 420px phone draw), not viewport queries. Keyed on the viewport they missed the map a phone held sideways draws beside its switch - 294-348px on a 568-667px screen - and the pin card met the stamp and the left label met the toggle there. Measured clean on all six hospitals at 320, 360, 390, 568x320, 667x375 and 844x390.
- The city's name sits behind the drawing at a 0.07 stroke (`.br-map__ghost`), the way a printed map carries its title - it says whose map this is and nothing else.
- **The frame is sticky from 1024px** (`top: calc(var(--hd-height) + 20px)`) so the map stays on screen while the reader runs down the numbers beside it; below that the map leads and the details follow, capped at 720px wide.
- Beside it: the address in the serif (with each `-` made unbreakable in `BranchLocate`, so a balanced wrap can never open a line with the hyphen of `Visnagar - 384315` - it did, on a 390px phone), the landmarks as chips, `Open in Google Maps` (filled) and `Copy address` (a real clipboard write, with a `Copied` state and a polite live region - for the reader about to hand their phone to a driver), the desk numbers as `tel:` links, and the hours derived from the hospital's own `hours` block (`getHoursRows`) with its `note` under them. **The desk numbers carry the `.e-link` halo** (`::after`, `inset: -6px 0`): a 32px line is a 44px target, verified with `elementFromPoint`.
- **A hospital's own social accounts are one more row of the Numbers panel** (`.br-desk--social`, `locate.followLabel`, an `AtSign` glyph like the desks' `Phone`): the brand mark and its name as a link, in the numbers' type, `aria-label` naming the hospital. Only `confirmedProfiles(branch.socialLinks)` render, and the same list is the hospital node's `sameAs` - so a lead flagged `"unconfirmed"` is never shown or claimed. Visnagar (Instagram, Facebook) and Ahmedabad (Facebook) have one today.

**Below 1024px the same three blocks become one card the reader taps through, and this is the section's central decision.** Stacked in a column they measured 1504px on a 390px phone and 1565px on a 768px tablet - four screens of label-and-list for three facts, and the single biggest reason the page read as complex there. The card is the map, a three-way switch (`Address` / `Numbers` / `Hours`), one panel, and the actions under it: 894px on a phone and 1025px on a tablet, and the reader is never scrolling past two answers to reach the one they wanted.

- **The threshold is 1024px because that is where the two-column grid gives up**, not a separate phone breakpoint: wherever the map sits above the details rather than beside them, the details are a card. The map is `5/4` below 760px, where the sketch needs height to read, and `16/9` above it, where a 5:4 map in a 1000px card would be 800px tall. **A phone held sideways lays the card's map beside its switch** (`(max-width: 1023px) and (max-height: 520px)`, `1.1fr / 0.9fr`): stacked, the map alone was taller than the screen.
- **`stacked` is decided in JS, not by CSS** (`useSyncExternalStore` over `matchMedia`, the same device `JourneyTimeline` uses for its short-viewport fallback). The tabs are real ARIA controls, and a `role="tab"` that is `display: none` at the width where every panel is open at once is a lie to a screen reader. Verified by resizing live in one session: the shape flips at exactly the threshold with no exceptions thrown.
- **The panel carries no label of its own on a phone** - the tab above it has just said the same word, and `ADDRESS` printed under a tab reading `Address` was the section's one piece of visible redundancy.
- **The actions stay under the card at every width, never inside a tab.** Directions is what a reader came for; it must never be one tap behind a label. On a phone they share a row as `Directions` / `Copy` (the two-label CSS device again) and stretch to half the card each - `flex-wrap: nowrap` with the button's side padding eased to 12px, because at 320px they wrapped into two full-width rows, a screen's worth of button for two actions.
- **The card grows to the panel rather than snapping to it**, so the actions never jump out from under a thumb. Same mechanics as the service page's explore card, and the same two traps: the height is written **straight to the DOM** (a state setter trips `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect`, and the value is a measurement) from a **callback ref, not an effect keyed on the tab** - `AnimatePresence mode="wait"` holds the outgoing panel until its exit finishes, so an effect would measure the panel that is leaving. Measured: 174px on Address, 235px on Numbers, 133px on Hours.
- The filled pill is **one element that travels** between tabs (framer `layoutId`), not a background that blinks off one and on to the next - the same element the services console and the service card use. Tab border is `--br-edge-light`; label contrast is 8.7:1 idle and 14.3:1 selected, pixel-sampled.
- Contrast on the navy and on the drawing, pixel-sampled worst cell: road label 13.4-14.2:1, place labels 11.0-11.7:1, pin card 14.3:1, stamp 6.1-14.4:1 (it crosses the road casing on a phone), toggle 13.1-17.3:1, address 14.7:1, landmarks 14.8:1, desk names 8.4:1, numbers 12.8:1, hours labels 8.5:1, note 7.8:1, lede 7.5:1.

### BranchTeam, BranchCare, BranchInside, BranchOthers

- **Every section drops to `padding-block: 44px` below 760px**, with the head's rule tightened to match. Six sections at the desktop rhythm spent 768px on padding alone - most of a phone screen of nothing between blocks that were already too far apart.
- **`BranchTeam` shows the people who see patients at this hospital, and nobody else**, in three parts: the consultants based here as cards, then the specialists who visit it under the doctors page's own `Visiting specialists` heading and note (read from `doctors.json` so the two pages cannot name it differently), then the optometrists as one line with their faces stacked beside it (`supportNote`, or `supportNoteOne` for one - "Supported by 1 optometrists" was the bug Bharuch's single optometrist would have shipped). `getBranchTeam` (in `doctorsData.js`) uses the roster's own rule for who is visiting. **The cards are the doctors page's row card** (`.br-doc`, 120px face, two to a line from 761px, one column for a hospital with one doctor at up to 560px) and **their place lines are written from this hospital** (`getDoctorPlaces(doctor, here)`): a consultant based here shows only `Also sees patients at ...` (every other hospital, visits included) or nothing; a visiting specialist shows where they are based and `Here on Tuesday and Saturday, 10:00 AM - 5:00 PM` where the days are known. Visiting cards are `h4` under the group's `h3`. The paragraph below describes the portrait-card build this replaced and is kept for its history. `/doctors` owns the team; the head's link is the way there. **The grid is sized by how many consultants the hospital actually has** (`data-count`): three columns for three, a pair for two, a single 380px card for one - Bharuch's lone medical officer was otherwise marooned in a third of the page with two empty columns beside him.
