The phone rule names `.br-team__grid[data-count]` as well: the attribute rules outrank a bare class, and a two-doctor hospital would have kept two squeezed columns on a phone. **A phone held sideways gets the phone's row, two to a line** (the doctors page's call), the odd last one across the line: as cards, each was taller than the screen. A record whose `qualifications` repeat its `specialty` prints the words once. The post-nominals are their own span (`.br-doc__quals`, with the comma in `.br-doc__sep`) so the phone row can drop the comma and set them on their own line: in the ~170px column beside the portrait, `Dr. Vishnu S. Patel, MBBS, MS` broke inside the name. The per-card `Book a visit` link was built and removed: three identical links to the same URL under a hero that already books here. Its lede lost its second sentence for the same reason - `Optometrists and the surgical team support every visit` said what the support row under the cards already says, and a sentence printed twice is 48px of phone screen spent on nothing. Three columns hold down to 641px - two left the third card orphaned and upscaled every portrait - and below that a doctor is a row, portrait left and words right, so three consultants take one screen.
- **`BranchCare` is read in runs under a heading, packed two cards across.** Three builds came before it and each failed differently: a cloud of eleven pills said nothing about how the treatments relate to each other; eleven uniform full-width pills were tidy but flat; and a ruled ledger of eleven rows - a name at the left edge, an arrow circle pinned to the right - spent most of every row on nothing, which is exactly how it read on a phone.
  - **The runs stay.** The source order in `services.json` is already grouped by kind of care, so they are free, and a heading reading `Specialty clinics` is the first thing a hesitant reader uses to find their own line. It is the shape the services index reads in, which is where every one of these cards goes. A run is a grid item, never a column flow - `columns: 2` packs tighter but can split a heading from the treatments it names, which is worse than a ragged edge. Three columns of runs from 1100px, two below, one on a phone.
  - **What changed is the object under them**: a compact card, two to a row at every width. `repeat(auto-fit, minmax(130px, 1fr))` - `auto-fit`, not `auto-fill`, so a run of one collapses the empty track and takes the full width, which is the emphasis the urgent run should have anyway. And **an odd run's last card spans both columns** (`li:last-child:nth-child(odd)`), because a lone card in the left half is the same empty half-row the ledger was full of.
  - **No arrow on the card and no count on the heading.** Eleven identical arrows tell a reader nothing - the reason the hospitals menu dropped its six map pins and the drawer its chevrons - and the cards are the count. Both were pure width, and width was the problem.
  - The urgent run keeps the danger colour on its heading, its card's border and its name. Contrast: run heading 7.1:1, card name 18.2:1, the urgent run 6.7-6.9:1.
- **`BranchInside` is the hospital's own photographs of itself** - the hall a visitor waits in, the atrium over it, the floor where patients are seen, and (on Visnagar only) the door from the road. Visnagar and Ahmedabad show four, Juhapura five (its entrance, the optical counter, reception, waiting hall and consulting-room corridor), Himmatnagar five (reception and waiting, a consulting room, the operation theatre, the recovery room and the optical counter), and Bharuch and Gota show Visnagar's three. **The lede is per hospital where the set is** (`page.insideLede`, falling back to the shared `inside.lede`, which names the Visnagar set the two borrowers still show), so the sentence always says what is actually in the photographs; re-word it if a set changes. **Keep a gallery to five**: the thumbnails carry a 44px floor (`min-height`), because five at 4:3 on a 320px phone are 38px tall; a sixth would squeeze them to 42px wide. **That floor comes back through the aspect ratio as a 59px minimum width**, and five of those need 327px of the 288 a 320px phone gives them: from the day Juhapura reached five, its thumbnail row widened the whole page to 341px there. `min-width: 0` on the thumbnail's `li` and `minmax(0, 1fr)` on `.br-gallery` are both needed - the first lets the items shrink, the second stops the grid's column growing to the row's min-content - and five are now 51x44 at 320px. **A probe will not see this as overflow**: on a mobile viewport Chrome widens the layout to fit the content, so `scrollWidth - innerWidth` stays 0 while `innerWidth` itself reads 341 at a 320px device. Check `innerWidth`.
  - **One instrument at every width: a stage, a caption, a row of thumbnails.** The build before it was three galleries - a three-up mosaic on a laptop, a two-column grid on a tablet and a swipe rail on a phone - so which gallery a reader met depended on their device, and only the laptop showed the photographs at a size worth looking at. Now only the measurements change: the stage is `clamp(300px, 46vw, 520px)` tall, and from 1024px the caption and thumbnails move _beside_ it (same three parts, same DOM order, the way a section head reflows) so the stage stops spanning the full 1240px shell.
  - **Swiping the stage is a parallax, not a slide.** Each landscape photograph (and a portrait's blurred fill) drifts ±7% inside its own frame as the frame crosses the stage, at `scale: 1.18` so it always covers: a view timeline on the stage's _inline_ axis (`animation-timeline: view(inline)`), which works because the stage is the photograph's nearest scroll container. **So the slide is `overflow: clip`, never `hidden`** - `hidden` made the slide button the scroll container and the drift sat at 0 through a whole swipe. Centred when it is the one on screen. The caption is keyed on the photograph and rises in over 320ms when it changes. Measured at 390px in Chrome and Safari 27: 0% at rest, +/-3.5% half-way, the frame covered at every step.
  - **A phone held sideways gets the laptop's shape**: the caption and thumbnails beside the stage (200px, three to a row) and the stage as tall as the screen can show (`max(200px, 100svh - 120px)`). Stacked, the stage alone was 388px on a 390px screen.
  - **The stage fills with a landscape photograph and contains a portrait one.** `cover` crops any landscape frame - Visnagar's 4:3 or Odhav's 20:9 - to the stage, losing an edge and never a subject. A portrait frame cropped to a landscape stage keeps a third of itself, so a gallery entry carrying `"portrait": true` renders a blurred copy of the same file behind it (`.br-gallery__fill`, `blur(22px)` and darkened) and the photograph `contain`ed over it; Juhapura carries four - its garlanded entrance, the optical counter, its reception and its consulting-room corridor - and Himmatnagar three (the consulting room, the recovery room and the optical counter). The flag is data, not a runtime `naturalWidth` check, so the fill is in the first paint rather than arriving after the image decodes. **Do not crop a portrait to fit** - mark it. Verified at 390 and 1440px: the fill sits only behind the flagged slide, the viewer shows the portrait whole (358x537 at 390px) and reduced motion runs 0 animations.
  - **The thumbnails are the navigation at every width**, so no control exists on one device and not another. Scroll position is the single source of truth: a thumbnail does not set the index, it scrolls the stage and the stage reports back - the `ServiceVisit` rule, and what keeps the two in step whichever the reader used. The active thumbnail is marked by a ring drawn _inside_ it (`::after`), never a border, so the row cannot shift by a pixel as the reader moves.
  - **A roving tabstop on the stage**: only the photograph on screen takes one (`tabIndex` 0 / -1), so the gallery costs the keyboard five stops rather than nine, and the thumbnails reach the rest. Verified with real touch events (`Input.dispatchTouchEvent`): a horizontal drag moves one photograph and leaves the page where it was, a vertical drag over the stage scrolls the page 299px without changing the photograph, and only a real tap opens the viewer.
  - **Every photograph opens.** A 320px-wide thumbnail of a waiting hall shows almost nothing, and a picture a reader cannot enlarge is the one place a phone page silently withholds something. The viewer is a **scroll-snap rail of the whole set**, opened at the photograph that was tapped. **Stretch its slides, do not centre them**: with `align-items: center` the portrait exam-room photograph was content-sized and rendered 269px tall in an 844px viewer, because `max-height: 100%` had nothing definite to resolve against. Chevrons appear only from 761px - on a phone the next photograph is a swipe away and an arrow only covers the picture.
  - The viewer is a real dialog: `aria-modal`, `Escape`, a backdrop tap, a `Tab` trap that skips the disabled chevrons, focus into the close button on open and back onto the photograph that was tapped on close. **The page behind is locked with a body class, not an inline style** - `react-hooks/immutability` rejects writing to `document.body.style`, and the class is one rule this stylesheet owns. Its `z-index` is 240: above the header and the drawer, below the cookie dialog, because it is a layer the reader opened rather than a decision they owe.
- **`BranchOthers` is one card at every width**, three across on a laptop, two on a tablet, one on a phone - nothing here is a different component on a different device. Each card is the whole link, routed through `buildBranchHref`.
  - **No row ends on an empty slot.** Five other hospitals is three and two: from 1024px the list is six tracks with a card across two, and a last row of two takes three tracks each (`li:nth-child(3n+1):nth-last-child(2)` and the one after it) - measured 405/405/405 then 614/614 at 1440. Two across, the odd fifth takes the row. The six-track rule sits inside its own `min-width: 1024px` block: at every width the smaller layouts would otherwise have to out-rank its `+ li` selector to undo it, and the first attempt did not.
  - **Hover is for a real pointer, and a finger gets the press.** Every hover state on this page - the back link, the quiet actions, the map toggle, Copy, the desk numbers, a doctor's portrait zoom, the gallery's zoom mark and thumbnails, the viewer's buttons, the care and hospital cards - is gated on `(hover: hover) and (pointer: fine)`; a tap left `:hover` on the doctor's portrait (zoomed), the gallery thumbnail and the hospital card (lifted) for the rest of the visit. Touch gets `:active` instead: the tiles tint, the care and hospital cards press to 0.98.
  - **The five identical map pins are gone.** They repeated one glyph five times and said nothing about which hospital was which - the mistake the hospitals menu made with six `MapPin` tiles and the drawer made with a chevron on every row, both removed for this reason. What differs between these five is the city and the street it is on, so those carry the card: the city in the serif this page uses for the things it names, the locality under it, and a 2px hairline down the left edge that fills accent on hover - the card's one signature.
  - A `Head office` tag renders on the card of whichever hospital is the head office. It never appears on the Visnagar page, because Visnagar _is_ the head office; it is there for the moment a second hospital gets a page.
- **`BranchActionBar`** is the service page's bar for a hospital: sticky at the bottom of `<main>`, rising when the hero's actions leave the screen (verified: hidden at the top, pinned mid-page, released above the footer at the end). Four actions - call, WhatsApp, directions, book - so below 641px the three quiet ones drop to glyphs and below 420px `Book at Visnagar` swaps to `Book` (`bookShortLabel`), the cookie sheet's two-label device; at 320px the row is still 48px tall and one line. The glyph threshold was 560px, and on a phone held sideways `Book at Himmatnagar` ran 52px off the screen beside three worded buttons at 561px (the page did not overflow - the bar clips - so only a measurement of the row found it); from 641px every hospital's row fits with its words.
- Measured with **no page overflow, no console errors, and no target under 44px** (the desk numbers through their halo, and the `.e-link` heading links through theirs) at 320, 360, 390, 412, 640, 768, 1024, 1280, 1440 and 2560px, in both motion modes. Re-measured for the deploy pass on **all six hospitals** at 320x568, 360x640, 375x667, 390x844, 412x915 and 430x932, plus 844x390 landscape and reduced motion: no overflow, no console errors, no target under 44px, the doctor name never breaks inside the name, no address line opens with a hyphen, Directions and Copy share one row at 320px, and the page is 6.7-8.5 screens on a 390x844 depending on the hospital (Gota, with no team section, is the shortest). `Book at Visnagar` clears the fold at 460px on a 320x568 and 396px on a 390x844. No care row, tab label, action-tile label or hospital-card name wraps at any width; tabs are 82px wide and tiles 72px tall at 320px, and the gallery thumbnails are 66x50 there.
- Under `prefers-reduced-motion`: 0 animations on the page, every reveal at opacity 1, the tab panel swaps with no glide, and the viewer opens, swipes and closes without one.
- **Re-measured for the deployment pass** on all six hospitals at 320x568, 360x640, 375x553, 390x664, 390x844, 393x659, 412x915, 430x932, 568x320, 667x375, 740x360, 844x390, 768x1024, 820x1180, 1024x768, 1180x800, 1280x800, 1366x657, 1440x900, 1920x1080 and 2560x1440, split between the dev server and the production build: no overflow at any scroll position, no target under 44px (hit-tested over a 44px square, so the halos count, and skipping points under the sticky bar), no clipped label, no map label touching another, and no console errors; the booking on the first screen everywhere. Real touch at 320x568, 375x553, 390x664, 568x320, 844x390 and 768x1024, a different hospital at each: the back link, the booking, all three tiles, the chip rail swipe (the rail moves, the page does not), the map toggle both ways, all three tabs, every desk number, Directions and Copy, every care card, every thumbnail, the viewer (opens on the photo tapped, swipes, closes), `View all hospitals`, every other-hospital card and all four bar actions land on themselves and do what they say (58 checks on Visnagar), and a vertical drag over the gallery scrolls the page. Keyboard: Enter on the photograph opens the viewer with focus on Close, Tab stays inside, Escape returns focus to the photograph. At 4x and 6x CPU throttle on the production build a touch scroll through the whole page plus two gallery swipes runs at a 16.7ms median with a worst frame of 33ms and none over 50ms. Checked in Safari 27 at 390 and 1100px through a self-reporting page, visible: the arrival, the written city, every scroll-driven rise, the drift, the chip fade and the others grid match Chrome. **A hidden Safari tab freezes `setTimeout`**, so a self-reporting page there must time its waits some other way - it waited on a local listener that held each request for the delay - and it reads scroll-driven values stale; only a visible window gives trustworthy motion numbers.
- Pixel-sampled worst cell on the new phone elements: tab label 8.7:1 idle and 14.3:1 selected, action-tile label 16.9:1, the phone lede 7.3:1, the status pill 10.8:1 and its detail 8.2:1, the care run heading 7.1:1 and card name 18.2:1, the gallery caption 18.2:1 and its counter 7.8:1, the hospital-card city 18.2:1 and its locality 7.8:1, and the address, landmarks and `Copy` on the locate card 13.4-13.7:1.

## Contact: /contact

**One head, one instrument, one directory - and no form.** Booking lives on `/appointment` and the footer carries the appointment CTA on every page, so the contact page does one thing: connect a reader to the hospital they chose, and list every desk number at every hospital. It is about 3 screens above the footer on a 390x844 phone (2547px), and **the switchboard - six hospitals, the number, the status and the call row - is on the first screen at the heights Safari actually shows** (390x664, 393x659), on a phone held sideways (844x390, 740x360, 667x375) and on a 1366x657 laptop; the page it replaced was four sections - a photographic hero, three cards, the whole appointment form a second time, and a Google embed with a phone directory beside it - on `PageHeader` and about 300 `.contact-*` rules in `global.css`. All of that is gone, along with `site.pageSeo.contact` (the page's SEO now lives with its copy in `contact.json`).

- Copy lives in `src/data/contact.json` and is read through `src/lib/contactData.js` (`contactPage`, and `mergeDesks()`). The page is `src/pages/ContactPage.jsx`, the sections are `ContactDesk` and `ContactDirectory`, and all of them own `src/styles/contact.css` (`.ct-`). Built in the **`.e-*` design system**; nothing here restyles a shared primitive.
- The head is the hospitals index's: centred, the serif with the promise in italic accent on its own line (`The right desk, / one call away.`) and a lede. **The lede is hidden below 641px** - its sentence is what the card's own label says, and with it the number and the call button sat under the fold. The page's one route to `/appointment` is a quiet `.e-link` **under the card** (`.ct-book`), not in the head: it is the least urgent thing on the page, and in the head it cost the phone's first screen 45px. A link, not a button, because the footer's button is two screens down.
- **The page arrives in reading order, and on a first visit it waits for the opening curtain** (`useIntroDone()` / `isIntroPending()`, the doctors and hospitals heads' device): label, headline and lede rise 80ms apart, **`one call away.` writes itself in word by word** (`.ct-head__ink`, `WRITE`), then the card makes its connection (see below) and the booking link rises last. It used to rise on mount, which spent the head and the card's connection under the curtain on a link opened straight to `/contact`. Measured at 390x664 in session: head from 0.05s after the route commits, card 0.25s, cable 0.85s, number rolled in by 1.2s, everything settled by about 1.5s; on a first visit everything holds at 0 until the curtain lifts.
- **A laptop window under 760px tall** (`(min-width: 900px) and (max-height: 760px)`) sets the title a size smaller (`3.4rem`) and closer to the card, lede kept: at 1366x657 the call row was 75px under the fold.

### ContactDesk - the switchboard

`src/sections/ContactDesk.jsx` is a navy card (the index map's ground) split in two: six hospitals on one side, one readout on the other, and a cable between them.

- **The card arrives the way it works, and that is the page's signature moment.** It rises (`CARD`), its six hospitals light up one by one (`LAMP`, opacity only), the readout's parts rise behind them (`PART`), and only then - `CONNECT_AT`, 0.75s after the head starts - does it connect: the cable draws to the reader's hospital, the pulse lands and **the number rolls up into place from 0** (`NumberRoll`'s `live`: until the first connection the strips sit at 0 and the line is clear, `.ct-odo[data-landed]`). It used to connect at mount, under the curtain or while the card was still transparent. What the cable attaches to - the hospitals and the readout's jack - only fades, never moves, so the line is never measured off a part still rising. A tap before the connection arms it at once. Under reduced motion the whole card is simply there, connected.
- **The readout reads: label, number, status, the four actions, then the address** (`.ct-readout__where`, a pin and the address behind a hairline, its `-` made unbreakable as on the hospital page). The address used to stand between the status and the actions, and pushed the call row under the fold on a phone and on a laptop. **The status pill and its detail share a line where they fit** (`.ct-readout .br-status` is flex-wrap: `OPD open now` / `Closes 8:00 PM`) and wrap where they do not (`Emergency and prior appointments only`).
- **The page follows a choice as a transition.** `storeBranch()` runs inside `startTransition`, so the card the reader is watching connects first and the header, the directory and the footer catch up a frame later: a choice was one 50ms frame at 4x CPU throttle (83ms at 6x) and is now at most 33ms (67ms at 6x) on the production build.

- **Choosing a hospital is the whole interaction, and the card shows the connection being made.** A cable draws from that hospital's jack to the readout jack (`DRAW_MS`), a pulse rides it across (`PULSE_MS`, `getPointAtLength` in a rAF loop, the index map's traveller device), the readout jack takes the hit as it lands (`.is-hit`, a one-shot ring), and at that moment the OPD number rolls over digit by digit to the new hospital's. Under `prefers-reduced-motion` the cable is simply there, there is no pulse, and the number swaps.
- **The number is an odometer** (`NumberRoll`): one column per digit over a strip of 0-9, the timeline's `YearRoll` device, in the serif with tabular figures so every column is the same width. **The visible number holds until the pulse lands** (`ROLL_DELAY`, a state timer inside `NumberRoll`), then the punctuation swaps and the digits roll together - swapping the shape first showed a nonsense number (`(91)-760-...`) for a third of a second, because the punctuation of `+91-760-008-2710` and `(079)-22901515` differ. The real number is in a visually hidden span updated at once, and the strips are `aria-hidden`.
- **The whole number is the `tel:` link** with an `.e-link`-style halo (`::after`, `inset: -8px 0`): a 31px line is a 45px target on a phone and 69px on a laptop, measured with `elementFromPoint`. `BranchStatus` sits under it with the chosen hospital as its `branch`, so the pill answers for the hospital the number belongs to - the card sets `--br-edge-light` so its pill border resolves.
- **The choice is the header's choice.** It opens on `getStoredBranch()`, writes back through `storeBranch()`, and follows `BRANCH_CHANGE_EVENT`; `Header` now listens for the same event, so choosing Bharuch here moves the OPD number in the header's strip at once, and the directory below moves its open fold (phones) or its lifted row (wider) to Bharuch. The hint under the list says so, and is hidden stacked, where the cable's lane runs, and sideways.
- **The rows are a radio group** (`role="radiogroup"`, `role="radio"`, `aria-checked`, roving `tabIndex`) with `ArrowUp`/`ArrowDown`/`ArrowLeft`/`ArrowRight`/`Home`/`End`, and a polite live region announces `Connected to <hospital>`. The chosen row's tint is one element that travels (`layoutId`), the site's pill device, and its ring lights. The rings sit at the **right** edge of the rows, on the gutter side, so the cable leaves the row without crossing its own text.
- **The cable is geometry written straight to the path**, never render state: `lay()` measures the two rings against the card, writes `d`, the `viewBox` and the dasharray, and **only touches the offset when no connect is in flight**; `connect()` cancels any running one and replays. They are separate because the first build had one `draw()` and a `ResizeObserver` effect keyed on it: every hospital change re-subscribed, the observer's initial callback fired at once, and the static redraw cancelled the cable draw and the pulse before either was seen - the connect sequence never ran once and nothing in the DOM said so. The observer is subscribed once, skips its first callback, and the pulse reads the path's length fresh on every frame so a cable re-laid under it by a resize carries it rather than losing it. Verified by sampling the pulse's opacity and the strips' transforms through the sequence: pulse at 1 through 480ms, gone by 700ms, digits mid-roll at 480ms, settled by 1500ms.
- **Side by side (>= 900px) the cable is a cubic with horizontal tangents** at both ends, so it crosses the gutter and never runs back through the rows; the control distance is half the gutter plus a share of the vertical travel, capped, which keeps row six's cable inside the gutter.
- **Stacked - a phone or tablet held upright, `(max-width: 899px) and (min-height: 521px)` (`STACKED_QUERY`) - the six hospitals are a two-column grid of chips, all visible at once, and the cable is a bus down the gutter between the columns.** Each chip's ring faces the gutter (`nth-child(odd)` ring right, `nth-child(even)` ring left), so the cable leaves every chip straight into its lane, runs down the 24px gutter to just under the grid, across to the readout's column and down into its jack, every corner a `Q` arc. Two earlier phone shapes failed: a scroll-snap rail of pills hid four of the six hospitals behind a swipe (one and a half pills showed at 390px), and an S-curve from a far pill grazed its neighbours' bottom edges on a tablet. The chip drops its locality line and its `Head office` tag - the fold in the directory carries both - and holds its name on one line, always.
- **The chip is sized around `Himmatnagar`, which is 99px at 0.98rem and 96px at 0.94rem.** Two columns at 390px give a chip 148px; from 399px down the chip gives up padding and ring (8/10px, a 10px ring, 0.94rem) rather than the name, and **below 357px two columns cannot hold it at a readable size, so the chips stack in one column** with every ring at the right and the bus down the card's right margin (`lay()` detects the single column from the chip's width against the grid's). **Check with `scrollWidth` against `clientWidth`** - a `Range` line count reported a clipped name as one clean line, which is how the 360 shipped with `Himmatnaga` once. Verified: no chip name clipped at 320, 340, 356, 357, 360, 375, 390, 399, 400, 412, 430, 600, 768 or 899px.
- **A phone held sideways keeps the desktop's two columns, compacted** (`(max-width: 1023px) and (max-height: 520px)`): the title on one line, no lede, the hospitals as one-line 44px rows with their rings at the right (so the desktop's cubic cable serves), no hint and no status detail - the hospitals index's call. Stacked, the chips alone filled an 844x390 screen and the number started at 598px; now the number is at 212-222px and the call row on the first screen at 844x390, 740x360 and 667x375 (the 568x320 SE shows the number and the status).
- **A phone's first screen is measured at Safari's visible heights**, and the air came out of the gaps (`@media (max-width: 640px) and (min-height: 521px)`): 44px chips, 6px row gap, a 26px cable lane, 14px readout gaps. At 390x664 and 393x659 the number is at 470px and Call OPD ends at 654px, Sundays included, when the status detail wraps; at 360x640 the call row ends 10px under the fold on a Sunday only; at 375x553 and 320x568 the number is on the first screen and the actions one short scroll down.
- **Hover plates are for a real pointer** (`(hover: hover) and (pointer: fine)`) on the hospitals, the number, the four actions, the emergency row, the ledger's rows, pills and links and the email; a finger gets `:active`. A tap left the tapped action's border white for the rest of the visit.
- The four channels - Call OPD (filled white), WhatsApp, Directions, Email - are one row from 641px and two-by-two below it. Their hrefs all come from `src/lib/contact.js`; the WhatsApp message is `desk.whatsappMessage`.
- Contrast, pixel-sampled worst cell on the card's gradient at 1440 and 390px: the two labels 5.5:1 (raised from 0.62 to 0.7 after measuring 4.71:1 under the teal glow - a pass with no margin for 0.72rem uppercase), the readout label 6.0:1, the number 10.7:1, locality 6.5:1 (4.9:1 on the tinted chosen row), address 7.1:1, status detail 6.7:1, hint 6.1:1, the `Head office` tag 6.3:1, channel labels 13.3:1 and 14.3:1. Two sampling traps: the odometer's hidden strips extend the text `Range` ten lines below the card onto paper (sample the element box), and the first unchecked pill on a phone can be scrolled under the card's edge (a white glyph reads 1.3:1 against the page).

### ContactDirectory - every desk

`src/sections/ContactDirectory.jsx` is the paper band under the card: the emergency line, then a ruled ledger of the six hospitals with every desk, then email and hours.

- **It arrives as the reader scrolls** - the services and hospital pages' model, the block before the reduced-motion rules at the end of `contact.css`: the head cascades and its rule draws across, and the emergency line, each hospital and the two foot cells come up under the thumb (`animation-timeline: view()`). It replaced a framer fade that played once, 10px, and read as still. Firefox gets the band static. Measured at 390, 768, 844x390 and 1440: every element in the upper 62% of the screen at opacity 1 with no translate at every scroll position; nothing here may sit under an `overflow: hidden` ancestor.
- **The reader's hospital is marked on the ledger.** Its name carries the header's `Your hospital` tag (`directory.chosenTag`, accent with the switchboard's lit dot, 6.4:1 on white and 5.8:1 on paper), and from 900px its row lifts off the ledger as a white card - the FAQ's open row - with the hairlines either side of it stepping aside (`:has(+ [data-chosen])`) so a rule never runs into its corners. Both move when the reader chooses another hospital above; verified with the keyboard: `ArrowDown`, `End` and `Home` in the switchboard move the card, the tag and the foot's hours with the choice.

- **The emergency line leads** because it is the one number that belongs to no single hospital: a white row with an accent left rule, a beacon on the glyph (the page's one continuously moving thing, opacity only, stopped under reduced motion), and the whole row is the `tel:` link. The number is `site.header.emergency.phone`; the sentence is `directory.emergencyNote` and, like every other emergency wording on the site, needs a doctor's sign-off.
- **Bharuch answers its OPD and operation desks on the same three numbers**, so `mergeDesks()` folds consecutive desks with an identical number list into one `OPD / Operation` line rather than printing the list twice. The other five are unchanged by it.
- Each row's name is the way into that hospital's page (`buildBranchHref`), with the `View hospital` marker `aria-hidden` inside the link; nothing else about a hospital is written here. The accent draws across the row's top rule on hover and focus - the values ledger's device. Numbers are 44px white pills with the `--ct-edge` boundary (3.48:1), never `--e-rule-2`.
- **Stacked (<= 899px) each hospital is a fold, not an open row** - a native `<details>` with the name and street on the summary, a `+` that turns into an `x`, and the desks inside as uniform label / number lines (`.ct-line`, 50px, one line per number, the desk named on its first line only) with `View hospital` as the last line, the shape of a phone's own contacts list. `stacked` is decided in JS (`useSyncExternalStore` over `matchMedia`, the locate card's device) and the markup differs by width, because a `<summary>` that cannot close on a laptop is a lie to a screen reader. **The hospital chosen in the switchboard is the fold already open** - `openSlugs` starts from `getStoredBranch()` and follows `BRANCH_CHANGE_EVENT`, so the two sections always agree; a reader may open several. **The open fold moves with the choice rather than adding to it** (`chosenSlug`): a reader tapping through the six hospitals to compare them was left with all six folds open, the wall the folds exist to avoid; a fold they opened themselves stays open. **Opening a fold deals its lines in one by one** (`.ct-fold[open] .ct-line`, `--i`, 45ms apart behind the glide). The glide is `::details-content` with `interpolate-size`, the FAQ's device. Two builds came before it: six open rows of pills ran to 2685px on a 390px phone, and the same rows flattened (label inline with its pills) were 2242px and ragged; folded, the six names fit one screen and the whole page is 4467px against 5163px. Verified with real touch: a tap opens Gota, a second closes it, Bharuch shows `OPD / Operation` once over three lines, Enter and Space toggle a focused summary (with `Emulation.setFocusEmulationEnabled`, the CLAUDE.md trap). **Hover and the row's accent rule are scoped to `(hover: hover) and (pointer: fine)` and `:has(:focus-visible)`** - a tapped fold kept its row lit through `:focus-within` on a phone. The band also drops to `padding-block: 36px 56px` stacked.
- Email and hours close the list: the head office's address (`getPrimaryBranch`) with a halo on the link (49px target), because one address reaches every hospital, and the **chosen hospital's** hours as a `<dl>` - the six can differ, so the cell's label names the hospital (`OPD hours · Bharuch`) and a note under it says the hours are that hospital's. `chosen` starts from `getStoredBranch()` and follows `BRANCH_CHANGE_EVENT` alongside `openSlugs`, so the switchboard, the open fold and the foot always describe the same hospital. They repeat the footer on purpose - a contact page that sends the reader to the footer for the email is not a contact page.
- The emergency row is named grid areas (`mark label number` / `mark note number` beside each other; stacked, `mark label` / `mark number` / `note note`), so on a phone the number sits directly under its label and the sentence follows, rather than the number arriving after three lines of note.
- Contrast on paper: urgent label 6.4:1, urgent note and foot copy 7.8:1, the urgent number 16.6:1, localities, desk labels, `View hospital`, tags and the lede 6.8-7.1:1, number pills 13.5:1, hours 7.8:1 and 18.2:1.

### Measured

**Re-measured for the deployment pass** at 320x568, 360x640, 375x553, 390x664, 393x659, 390x844, 412x915, 430x932, 844x390, 740x360, 667x375, 568x320, 768x1024, 820x1180, 1024x768, 1180x800, 1280x800, 1366x657, 1440x900, 1920x1080 and 2560x1440: no overflow at any scroll position, no target under 44px (hit-tested with `elementFromPoint` over a 44px square), no chip name clipped (`scrollWidth` against `clientWidth`) and no console errors. Call OPD on the first screen at every size except 360x640 on a Sunday, 375x553, 320x568 and 568x320, where the number is. Real touch at 320x568, 360x640, 390x664, 768x1024 and 844x390 (56 checks each): every hospital connects, stores and moves the number and the actions with it; the number, the four actions, the booking link, the emergency row, every desk line of the open fold, each fold opening and closing, and the email land on their own control; tapping through all six leaves one fold open; a vertical drag over the chips scrolls the page and chooses nothing. At 4x CPU throttle on the production build the arrival, a touch scroll through the page and a fold opening run at 16.7ms a frame with no frame over 34ms once the route has committed (the long frames on the way in are the route's commit, as on every page); at 6x one 50ms frame as the connection starts. Under `prefers-reduced-motion` everything is there from the first frame, connected, with 0 running animations before and after a choice. Checked in Safari 27 at 390 and 1024 through a self-reporting page, visible: the arrival, the write-in, the cable, the pulse, the number rolling up, a change of hospital, the moved card and fold, and the scroll-driven rises match Chrome.

## Book an appointment: /appointment

**One head and one instrument: five questions in a card, and the slip that writes itself beside it.** The reader answers who, where, what, when and a note, one step at a time, and every answer lands on an appointment slip as a line of the message that will be sent. The slip _is_ the WhatsApp message - Send opens WhatsApp with that text addressed to the chosen hospital's line, and the reader presses send there. Nothing is posted from the site; the hospital replies on WhatsApp and confirms the slot. **The first step - the head, the rail, both fields and Continue - is on the first screen at the heights Safari actually shows** (Continue's foot at 652px on a 390x664 and a 393x659) and on every laptop window (640px on a 1366x657, 647 on a 1536x730, 892 on a 1440x900); see Measured for the sizes where it is not.

The page it replaced was a `PageHeader` over a two-column Web3Forms post: a six-field grid with a `preferredDate` date input, an email nobody needed, a honeypot, a Web3Forms transport (`src/lib/submitAppointment.js`, `VITE_WEB3FORMS_ACCESS_KEY`, `.env.example`) and a navy branch aside, on about 250 `.appointment-*` / `.field-grid` / `.form-status` rules in `global.css` plus the `.page-header--appointment` variant and `page-headers/clinic-reception.jpg`, its last consumer. All of that is gone, along with `appointmentSchema` in `schemas.js` and `site.pageSeo.appointment` (the page's SEO lives with its copy in `appointment.json`).

- Copy lives in `src/data/appointment.json` and is read through `src/lib/appointmentData.js`, which also owns the validation (`appointmentSchema`, zod, per-step through `STEP_FIELDS` / `isStepComplete()`), the mobile-number parser (`parseMobile`), the open-day calendar (`getOpenDays`) and the message builder (`buildAppointmentMessage`). The page is `src/pages/AppointmentPage.jsx`, the flow is `src/sections/BookingFlow.jsx` with its five panels in `src/sections/BookingSteps.jsx`, the slip is `src/components/BookingSlip.jsx`, the chip radio group is `src/components/ChoiceGroup.jsx`, and all of them own `src/styles/appointment.css` (`.ap-`). Built in the **`.e-*` design system**; nothing here restyles a shared primitive.
- The head is the contact page's: centred serif with the promise in italic accent (`Tell us when, / we hold the slot.`). The lede is hidden below 641px, as on the contact page, so the first step reaches the first screen.
- **The first screen is measured, and three tiers take the air out of it - never the type or a 44px target.** A phone (`max-width: 640px` and `min-height: 521px`) tightens the head's margin, the rail's, the step's gaps and the foot's. **A laptop window under 900px tall** (`min-width: 1024px` and `max-height: 899px` - 1366x657, 1536x730, a 13-inch MacBook's ~1470x830 once the browser's bars are counted) sets the head on one line a size smaller, lede kept, and tightens the card's rhythm: at 1366x657 Continue was 88px under the fold. **A phone held sideways** (`max-width: 1023px` and `max-height: 520px`) sets the head on one line with no lede, so the card's rail and the first question reach the screen: at 844x390 the head filled it and the card began at 327px of 390.
- **The name and the mobile share a row wherever the card is wide enough** (`.ap-pair`, a container query on `.ap-card` at 560px of content): beside the slip at 1440, on a tablet and on a phone held sideways; stacked on a phone and in the narrower card beside the slip between 1024 and 1180px. It is the card's width, not the window's, because the card beside the slip is narrower than the card on a tablet. The mobile field's hint (`The hospital replies on WhatsApp to this number.`) said what the step's lede says, and folded into it (`...and the WhatsApp number it will reply to.`) - the 26px it cost is part of why the phone's first screen fits.
- **The five steps and their fields**: `who` (patient name, mobile - both required), `where` (the hospital, required, preselected), `what` (the reason - one of the eleven services or `Not sure yet`, required, preselected from `?service=`), `when` (a day and a time of day, both optional), `send` (an optional note, then Send). Continue validates only the fields of the step it leaves (`trigger(STEP_FIELDS[id])`), a failed step lands focus on the first typed field that is wrong, and a field that has shown an error re-checks as the reader types so the error clears the moment the value is right - `mode: "onSubmit"` alone left the error standing until the next Continue. Enter in a text field is Continue.
- **A new step takes the focus on its heading** (`.ap-step__title`, `tabIndex={-1}`, no ring - it is not a control), which carries a visually hidden `Step 2 of 5:` (`stepOfLabel`). Before, Enter in a field unmounted the field with the panel and focus fell to `<body>`, so the next Tab started at the top of the page. The focus replaced a polite live region that announced the step, which would now say it twice. The receipt's heading takes the focus the same way.
- **A step change moves the page the least distance that shows the whole new step** (`revealCard()`, run as the new panel mounts, from `measurePanel`): never so far that the card's top goes under the header, and a step taller than the screen opens on its top, 20px under the header. The header slides away on a scroll down past 160px, so a target past that is measured against the top of the screen (the slip beside the card follows: `body:has(.hd--hidden) .ap-aside { top: 20px }` on the header's `--hd-slide`). It aims at the foot's buttons, not the card's padding under them, and ignores a move under 12px - at 1920x1080 it once scrolled 6px for padding. It replaced a `scrollIntoView` that fired only when the card's top was above 0, so a card top under the sticky header, or a new step's Continue below the fold, never moved.
- **The rail across the top is five dots on a track that fills behind the reader.** Five equal grid columns put the dot centres at 10/30/50/70/90%, which is why the track runs 10% to 90%. A step reads as answered (check) when it is complete and not current - at once for a required step, because a hospital or treatment the reader arrived with _is_ an answer, and only once passed for an optional one, because "nothing chosen" is not an answer until the question has been seen (`REQUIRED_STEPS`). A visited step can be reopened from the rail; a step ahead cannot be skipped to (`furthest`). The rail carries no answer text under its labels - the slip carries the answers, and a first build's ragged two-line summaries under 60px columns were removed.
- **The hospital is the header's hospital.** It opens on `?branch=` (which the header has already adopted) or `getStoredBranch()`, writes back through `storeBranch()` when chosen here, and follows `BRANCH_CHANGE_EVENT` - the number the slip is addressed to is always the one at the top of the screen. Verified: choosing Ahmedabad stores `ahmedabad` and the header's number reads `tel:07922901515` at once.
- **`ChoiceGroup` is one radio group of chips serving four questions** (hospital, reason, day, time): `role="radio"`, `aria-checked`, a roving tabstop, arrow keys walking one flat list even when the options are read in runs under headings (the reasons are grouped by kind of care, the services index's order), the chosen chip's tint as one element that travels (`layoutId`), and a ring that fills accent and draws a check so "this one" is never only a colour. Three shapes by `data-shape`: `row` (two columns from 641px, **full-width rows on a phone** - in a 160px column `Comprehensive Glaucoma Care` ran to three lines under the ring), `tile` (the day rail) and `segment` (the time of day: two by two, and four across only where the card has 620px of content - a container query, so the narrower card beside the slip keeps two. `auto-fit` set three and a lone `Evening` between 1024 and 1180px, and four across at 1440px broke `9 AM - 12` / `PM`; the chip gives its range the ring's gap and some padding, and the range's `AM` / `PM` are joined to their times with a no-break space, so at 320px it breaks only at its dash).
- **Arrow keys bring the chosen chip into view**: a row group with `scrollIntoView({ block: "nearest" })`, and the day rail with `clearOfFades()` in `ChoiceGroup`. Chrome's `nearest` leaves a tile alone once it is inside the rail's box, even under a fade, and a scroll to anywhere but a snap stop is pulled back by the rail's proximity snapping - both measured - so the rail moves to the first snap stop that clears the tile. The rail's `scroll-padding-inline` (30px / 36px) is the fades' width, which is what places those stops.
- **The day is a rail of tiles, not a date input.** `Any day`, then the next ten open days **of the chosen hospital**, on its clock (`getOpenDays(branch)`: its own closed days skipped, and today dropped once its OPD has closed for the day - the rail is rebuilt when the hospital changes), then `Pick a date` which reveals a native date input for anything further out. Dates are ISO strings handled at UTC noon so adding days and reading the weekday can never drift across a midnight in the reader's own zone. The schema refuses a past day or a closed day either way, so a Sunday typed into the date input is caught with the same error - **as an object-level `superRefine`, not a field refine**, because which days are closed depends on `values.branch`, and only the whole form knows that. That is also why `isStepComplete()` runs one full parse and reads the issues for the step's own fields rather than `pick()`ing a sub-schema, which would drop the object-level check from the one step that needs it (zod 4 still runs the refinement when other fields carry continuable errors - verified). The error and the note under the rail are templates filled from the hospital's block (`Choose an open day (Monday to Saturday)...`; `Sunday is not offered here: the Visnagar OPD is emergency and prior appointments only.`), and a hospital open every day gets no note. The chosen tile carries an accent bar along its foot in place of the ring, and **the rail fades at an end only where there is more past it** (`data-start` / `data-end`, written by `ChoiceGroup` from a passive scroll listener, the doctors rail's device; every mask stop positive, for Safari). It used to fade its right edge always - at its end too - and never its left.
- **The mobile field carries a `+91` in its margin that steps aside when the reader types a plus.** `parseMobile` reduces any input to its digits, drops an Indian `91` or trunk `0`, and accepts a ten-digit mobile starting 6-9 - or, with a leading plus, any 8-15 digit international number (a son booking from Toronto). The slip and the message carry the formatted form (`+91 98765 43210`). **A check grows at the field's right end the moment the number reads as a real mobile** (`data-valid`, `--color-success`, 6.46:1), so the reader knows it is right before Continue says so.
- **Send is a plain `<a>` to `wa.me`, never `window.open` after an awaited validation** - that is what popup blockers exist to stop. The link is only reachable through the steps, so it is pressed on a complete request; if it somehow is not (`ready` is a synchronous `safeParse`), the click is cancelled and the reader is taken to the first step still missing something. Pressing it sets `sent` and the card becomes the receipt: what to do in WhatsApp, the OPD hours, `Open WhatsApp again`, `Edit request`, and `Book for someone else` (which clears the patient fields and keeps the hospital). The slip's stamp turns from `Ready to send` to a green `Sent`, and its note changes with it.
- **The message** (`buildAppointmentMessage`): a bold heading naming the hospital, one line per field in the JSON's own templates - patient, mobile, reason, preferred day (`Any day` when none), preferred time (`Any time`), note - with an empty line dropped rather than sent as `Note: -`, and a closing line. Verified end to end: `wa.me/919998807891?text=*Appointment request - Aakash Eye Hospital, Ahmedabad* ... Preferred day: Wednesday, 16 September 2026 ... Please confirm a slot. Thank you.`

### The motion, and the rule it follows

**Things arrive in sequence, progress is seen to travel, and the slip shows where the reader is writing.** None of it is decoration, and every piece renders finished under `prefers-reduced-motion` (verified: 0 animations on load and after every step, through the receipt).

- **The page arrives in reading order, and on a first visit it waits for the opening curtain** (`useIntroDone()` / `isIntroPending()`, the contact page's device): the label, the headline and the lede rise 80ms apart (`cascade` / `RISE`), the card rises, the slip beside it, the rail draws, the first step's rows assemble, the slip's lines print, and the call link comes last. `AppointmentPage` passes `start` and `delay` to `BookingFlow`; the card, the rail's track and dots, the aside and the call link are `hidden` / `shown` variants on that start, the first panel is held at `enter` until it, and the slip beside the card takes `printed` / `lead`. It used to play all of it on mount - under the curtain on a link opened straight to `/appointment`. Measured in session: label from 445ms, everything settled by about 1.8s; on a first visit nothing moves until the curtain lifts (379ms) and the head starts 0.3s after.
- **The head's promise writes itself in, word by word** (`.ap-head__ink`, one `WRITE` clip per word), the About, doctors and contact heads' device - it was one clip on the whole `em`.
- **The first step rises with the card rather than sliding** (`custom` on the panel is `{ dir: 0, lead }` until the reader first moves); every later step slides the way the reader travels.
- **The rail assembles**: the track draws across and the five dots pop in on a spring, 80ms apart. **Moving forward, the dot the fill reaches takes a one-shot ring** (`.is-hit` on the dot, written by `go()` after `RAIL_HIT_MS`, the contact jack's device) - progress is seen to arrive, not to switch.
- **Every step assembles chip by chip.** `ChoiceGroup`'s chips carry the panel's named `enter` / `center` / `exit` states and the group wrapper staggers them at 35ms (`groupVariants` in `BookingSteps`), so a step's answers land behind its head rather than as one block. **Choosing sends one ring out from the mark** (`.ap-chip__mark::before`, a CSS animation that runs when `data-on` starts matching - which is also when a panel opens on a chip already chosen, and that is the reader being shown their current answer).
- **The slip prints itself.** Its lines arrive in sequence when it mounts - on a laptop with the page, on a phone inside the last step, where the reader watches their request print. **A pen sits in the margin beside the line being written** and travels down the slip as the reader goes (`activeLine` from the step, `layoutId="ap-slip-pen"`; on the first step it moves from Patient to Mobile once a name is in). **A chosen answer lands with a highlighter sweep** across its line (`.ap-slip__wash`, keyed on the value so it runs once per change). The teal light in the navy head drifts on an 18s loop, at the same strength the contact card carries, so nothing measured on it changes.
- **A mouse over the desktop slip tilts it** (`rotateX` / `rotateY` on springs, 3.5deg, mouse pointers only, never on the compact slip) - what says it is paper and not a panel.
- **The send button arrives with one sheen** across it and a small wave of the WhatsApp glyph; Continue's arrow leans on hover.
- **When a step becomes answered, Continue's arrow steps forward once** (`data-ready`, `ap-nudge`) - on the tenth digit of the mobile, on the first reason chosen. It runs as the attribute arrives, so a step that opens already answered (the optional ones) does not nudge.
- **The receipt's check mark sends two rings out** (`::before` / `::after`, `ap-ring`) - the pin's ripples on the hospital map, for the same reason: something just landed. The rings stop at 1.65x because the mark sits at the card's padding edge and a wider ring is cut off on a phone. **The step frame's clip reaches past its panel** (`.ap-card__frame`: padding over a matching negative margin, `box-sizing: content-box` so the written height is the content's) - 16px at the top, which every tier's rail margin clears, 20px at the sides: at 0 it cut the rings to a quarter-circle and a chip's focus ring at the frame's edge.

### BookingSlip

`src/components/BookingSlip.jsx` is the paper token a hospital desk hands over, and it is exactly the message that will go. A navy head names the hospital, its **WhatsApp number** (`branch.whatsappNumber`, not the OPD line - Bharuch's differ) and the live OPD status (`BranchStatus` for the chosen hospital; the card sets `--br-edge-light` for it), because "when will they reply" is the question after "what am I sending". A dashed perforation with a notch cut into each edge separates it from the lines, which are a `<dl>`: an unanswered line is a dotted leader, a filled one is serif ink, and the chosen-field lines cross-fade on their value (`AnimatePresence popLayout`) while the typed ones simply fill. The two optional lines stay blank until the reader has passed the When step, then read `Any day` / `Any time` - which is what is sent. When every required line is in, a rubber stamp lands (spring from scale 1.7 and -18deg to -7deg; `mix-blend-mode: multiply` inks it onto the paper).

- **Beside the card from 1024px, sticky under the header; inside the last step below that.** `stacked` is decided in JS (`useSyncExternalStore` over `matchMedia`, the locate card's device) because the slip is rendered in one of two places, never both - verified live: resizing mid-flow moves the one slip from `.ap-aside` into `.ap-step` and back with nothing thrown. On the receipt it stays in the card on a phone so the reader can check what went.
- **The page ground is flat (`--ap-ground: #f8f7f3`), not a gradient**, because the perforation notches are painted in it and a notch has to match the pixel behind it. Inside the white card (`[data-compact]`) the notches are white.
- `.ap-slip__to` is a `<p>` and takes `color: inherit`: `global.css` colours every `p` slate, and the hospital's name rendered grey on the navy until the audit caught it.
- **The card grows and shrinks to the step rather than snapping to it**, with the same two traps as the service page's explore card: the height is written straight to the DOM from a callback ref, never from an effect keyed on the step, because `AnimatePresence mode="wait"` holds the outgoing panel until its exit finishes. Measured at 390px: who 304, where 429, what 959, when 466, send 1014 (with the slip inside).
- **The foot on a phone**: Back is a 52px square with the arrow (its word stays in `aria-label`) and Continue / Send takes the rest of the row - `Send on WhatsApp` and `Back` cannot share a 326px row as two worded buttons. At 320px not even that fits, so Send takes the row and Back sits under it with its word.
- **Back's border is `--ap-edge`** (3.48:1): the outline primitive's `--e-rule-2` is 1.58:1, and a border that is the only thing making a button a control needs 3:1. **The foot's and the receipt's buttons drop their hover fill under `(hover: none)`**: the foot outlives the step, so a tap left Continue in the hover's ink for the rest of the visit.
- **Choosing a hospital stores it inside `startTransition`**, the contact switchboard's device: the chip moves first and the header and footer catch up a frame later. At 4x CPU on the dev server the choice went from one 83ms frame to 50ms; on the production build it measures 17ms (33ms at 6x).
- A quiet `.e-link` under the card - `Prefer to talk? Call the <hospital> OPD` - is the way out for a reader who gives up on the form; it follows the chosen hospital.

### Measured

No page overflow, no console errors and no `.ap-` target under 44px (the call link is a 25px line with the `.e-link` halo) at 320x568, 360x640, 390x844, 430x932, 768x1024, 1024x768, 1180x800, 1440x900 and 1920x1080, on every step, and at 844x390 landscape; under `prefers-reduced-motion` 0 animations run and a failed Continue still shows its errors. Verified with real input: errors on an empty Continue land focus on the name; typing clears them; `ArrowDown` on the checked hospital chooses the next and moves focus with it, and the slip follows; `?name=` prefills the name; `+1 416 555 0199` is accepted; a Sunday in the date input is refused and a Monday lands on the slip as `Monday, 5 October 2026`; `Book for someone else` returns to a clear first step with the hospital kept.

Pixel-sampled worst cell, 1440 and 390px: head label 7.3:1, accent 5.7:1, rail label 5.6:1 idle / 11.1:1 on, rail numeral 7.8:1 / 14.3:1, ledes and hints 7.8:1, error 7.4:1, chip title 18.2:1 idle / 14.3:1 chosen, chip sub 6.3:1 / 7.3:1, `Head office` tag 6.4:1 / 10.9:1, run heading 7.8:1, tile weekday 7.8:1 / 9.3:1, tile day 17.9:1 / 14.2:1; slip kicker 5.5:1, `To` 5.9:1, hospital 10.8:1, WhatsApp line 6.7:1 and its number 12.0:1, status detail 7.6:1, line labels 7.7:1, values 18.1:1, note 7.7:1, stamp 6.3:1 (sample it unrotated - the rotated box's corner cells catch its own border and report 4.5:1), Send 10.1:1, call link 9.0:1. Two probe artifacts to know: a parent whose child carries its own `color` rule (the WhatsApp line's `<b>`, the chip title's `<em>`) must have its descendants made transparent too or the child's glyphs are sampled as ground; and `captureBeyondViewport` re-lays the page out, so a chosen day tile once read 3.9:1 through a shifted clip while a direct capture of the tile showed solid navy.

**Re-measured for the deployment pass** on the production build at 320x568, 360x640, 375x553, 390x664, 393x659, 412x915, 430x746, 568x320, 667x375, 740x360, 844x390, 768x1024, 820x1180, 1024x768, 1180x800, 1280x800, 1366x657, 1440x900, 1536x730, 1920x1080 and 2560x1440, through every step and the receipt: no overflow, no target under 44px (hit-tested with `elementFromPoint` over a 44px square, so the call link's `.e-link` halo counts), no chip, rail, tile, button or slip label clipped, no console errors. Continue is on the first screen everywhere except 320x568, 375x553 and 360x640 (both fields are; 360x640 misses by 8px, and a real small Android shows ~360x584 with its toolbar) and the sideways phones, where the rail and the question are. Real touch at 320x568, 390x664, 844x390 and 768x1024 (103 checks each): every chip, tile, time and rail dot lands on itself and chooses; an empty Continue shows both errors and focuses the name; each step's heading takes the focus and the card lands 20px under the header (or under the top of the screen once the header has gone); a sideways swipe moves the day rail and leaves `scrollY`, a vertical drag over it scrolls the page; the fades follow the rail's ends; Back, a rail jump and `Edit request` keep the answers; Send opens `wa.me/919998807891` with Ahmedabad's message; `Book for someone else` clears the patient and keeps the hospital; the call link dials Ahmedabad; Continue keeps its navy after taps. Keyboard at 1440x900, 768x1024, 390x664 and 320x568: Tab reaches name then mobile, Enter continues, the arrow keys, Home and End walk each group, and every day tile reached by the arrows lands clear of the fades. At 4x CPU on a 390x664 every step change and choice runs at a 16.7ms median with a worst frame of 17ms on the production build (33ms at 6x); the one long frame on the way in is React committing the route. Checked in Safari 27 at 390x664 and 1280x700 through a self-reporting page, visible: the arrival order and the written accent, the paired fields, Continue's place on the first screen, the check and the nudge, focus on each heading, the reveal with the slip level beside the card, the rail's fades, the time ranges on one line and the receipt's rings match Chrome.

## Not found: /404

**Say plainly that the page is not here, guess what the reader was after from the address they opened, and put the site's main ways in - and the emergency line - one tap away.** It replaced the old `PageHeader` with a `Go Home` button under it, the last page on the pre-`.e-*` styles; `PageHeader`, `ButtonLink` and the `global.css` rules only they used (`.container`, `.section`, `.section-band`, `.eyebrow`, `.button-link*`, `.page-header*`, about 2KB of the shared sheet) went with it. The service page's own not-found state (`.sd-missing`, `services.json`'s `page.detail.notFound`, `ServiceRelated`'s `title` prop) went too: an unknown hospital, doctor or service slug now shows this same page.

- The page is `src/pages/NotFoundPage.jsx` and owns `src/styles/not-found.css` (`.nf-`); the copy is `site.notFound` and the logic is `src/lib/notFoundData.js` (`describeTried`, `suggestFor`, `getWays`, `NOT_FOUND_PATH`, which `entry-server.jsx` re-exports for the prerender).
- **The address is the reader's own.** A host answers every unknown path with `404.html` (`data-path="/404"`), so React builds the page fresh there rather than adopting it, and reads the real path in its first render. Opened as `/404` it has no address to echo and makes no guesses - which is exactly what the build wrote, so the adopted page matches. The address is shown under the lede (`You opened www.aakasheyehospital.com/gallery`, monospace, the path under an accent wavy underline, the spelling checker's mark), cut at 72 characters, breaking after a `/` (`<wbr>`), never mid-word.
- **The guesses are worked out from the data, not listed.** Every service, every doctor with a page, every hospital and the `site.notFound.pages` are candidates; the address is split into words (file-name noise like `php`, `html`, `index`, `aakash`, `eye` dropped) and each word is matched against each candidate's words exactly, as a prefix (`catar`, `doctor`), within one or two slips (`catarct`, `surgry`), or run together (`cataractsurgery`). A word is worth less the more candidates share it (`patel` names nine doctors and picks out none; `vishnu` names one), the first part of the path favours its kind (`/doctors/...` prefers a doctor), and only scores of at least 0.5 and within 35% of the best are shown, up to three. **No guess is better than a wrong one**: gibberish gets none. Checked: `/doctors/dr-vishnu-patel` -> Dr. Vishnu S. Patel, `/branch/sarkhej` -> Juhapura, `/branches/odhav` -> Ahmedabad, `/himatnagar` -> Himmatnagar, `/catarct` and `/cataractsurgery` -> Cataract Surgery, `/gallery` -> Our Hospitals, `/our-doctors.php` -> Doctors, `/about-us` -> Our journey, `/foo/bar` -> none. A new service, doctor or hospital is matched with no change here.
- **A guess is one card whatever it names** (`.nf-match`: the kind in accent micro caps, the name in the serif, one line of detail - the service's `shortDescription`, the doctor's specialty and hospitals, the hospital's locality, the page's description - and a navy arrow disc; the whole card is the link, its border `--nf-edge` for 3:1). Under them, **the four ways in** (Services, Doctors, Our Hospitals, Contact) as a ruled ledger, less any a guess has already offered, two columns wherever the body is 520px wide (a container query); then the contact directory's emergency row, the whole row the `tel:` link.
- **The eye chart is the page's one picture**: the services readout's Snellen chart (`src/lib/snellen.js`, shared with `SymptomScene`) on a white board in the console's tinted well, its top line reading `4 0 4`. It pulls into focus once as the page arrives (`nf-focus`, the console's refraction device, `backwards` so no filter is held) and is `aria-hidden`. Sticky beside the words from 1024px (following the header up on `--hd-slide`, its width capped by `svh` so it never runs under a short laptop's fold); beside the head on a tablet; on a phone a masthead - the label at the left, the chart's top three lines at the right (the board's aspect ratio crops the same drawing), `.nf-head` a plain div with `display: contents`.
- **The hospital, doctor and service pages load it on demand** (`components/LazyNotFound.jsx`, `React.lazy` in a `Suspense` with no fallback). Imported, its stylesheet would be linked, render-blocking, on every one of those pages; lazily, Vite fetches it before the module resolves, so the page is styled the frame it appears. Those slugs are never prerendered, so it only ever renders in the browser; on a first visit the curtain's one-second floor covers the load.
- It arrives the site's way: on a first visit it holds under the curtain, then label, title (the accent written in word by word), lede, address and actions rise 80ms apart, the chart 120ms behind the head, then the guesses and the ways. Under reduced motion it is simply there with 0 running animations.
- **Measured** on the production build at 320x568, 390x664, 768x1024, 844x390, 1366x657 and 1440x900: no overflow, one `h1`, no `.nf` target under 44px, both actions on the first screen everywhere but the 320x568 SE (where the header's `Book` pill is). Real touch taps at 320x568, 390x664 and 844x390 land every control on its own link (23 of 23), with nothing left lifted or tinted after a tap and scroll. The development build adopts `/404` and builds every other address fresh with 0 hydration messages and 0 errors, first visit and returning, at 390 and 1440. Not yet checked in Safari.
- The headline, the lede, the two guess headings and the four ways' descriptions are drafted and need the hospital's sign-off with the rest of the site copy.

## Opening curtain

The curtain is the first thing a visitor sees, once per session: **the hospital's own logo on paper, which opens like an eyelid onto the page.** It is static HTML in `index.html` (`#app-intro`), styled by `src/styles/preloader.css` (`.pl`), and `src/components/AppPreloader.jsx` takes it over once React runs. `#app-wait` (`.pl--wait`), also static HTML in `index.html`, is the reload veil and shares the stylesheet. The timing constants, the storage key, the done event, `introElapsed()` and `useIntroDone()` live in `src/lib/intro.js`, which the hero and the cookie sheet read - the three never read each other. `splitTagline()` is in `src/lib/brand.js`, plain JS with no React import, because `vite.config.js` reads it too.

**Two builds came before it and both were sent back.** The first was a navy splash with an orbit spinner, a scan line, breathing rings, a background grid, a fake progress bar and the hospital's name three times, on a fixed timer over a page that was already there. The second was a drawn iris - a saturated blue disc with 72 teal fibres, spinning dotted rings, white catchlights and a gaze that wandered - which then zoomed through its own pupil until the screen was glowing blue fibre. The hospital's verdict was "not even 1%": it was shiny in a way that hurts the eyes, it did not look like anything else on the site, and a cartoon eye is not this hospital's brand. **The rule now: the brand is the logo, the palette is the site's own (paper, the logo's colours, a navy hairline, the serif tagline with its accent year), and there is one gesture - the eye opening - which means something for an eye hospital.** Do not bring back a drawn eye, a spinner, glints, glows or a zoom.

**The sequence** (all from the static frame's first paint, `window.__aakashIntroStart`):

1. **The logo arrives in three parts.** The blue square scales in from 0.88 (640ms from 60ms), the white mark rises into it inside its clip (820ms from 200ms), and the name slides out from behind the square's right edge (`.pl__name` clips, only the name inside moves; 1000ms from 360ms, ease-out-expo). The tagline (`site.brand.tagline`, its `since <year>` in the accent italic) rises under it once the serif has loaded and not before 560ms. The logo has fully arrived by about 1s.
2. **A fine navy line between the logo and the tagline fills from the centre as the page really loads** (`.pl__seam`, `--pl-p` 0 to 1, a `scaleX` transition). The steps are real milestones, never a timer pretending: 0.3 at 380ms (the frame is up) and 0.52 once the serif is in - both written by the inline script in `index.html` - then 0.72 when the app mounts, a slow creep towards 0.9 while the page loads, and 1 when the first screen is ready (fonts plus the decoded first picture under `main`, the hero poster or a hospital's facade). The value only ever rises - both writers check. At 6x CPU on the dev server the line visibly sits at half while the app loads; on a fast load it simply fills as the name arrives.
3. **The eye opens along that line.** The two halves of the lockup part (logo up, tagline down, 36px) and fade, the line spreads and dissolves, and two paper lids part from the centre: the opening starts as a slit across the line, becomes an almond with pointed corners, widens, and the lids leave the screen (1040ms after a 60ms beat, `cubic-bezier(0.7, 0, 0.2, 1)` - slow at first so it reads as an eye starting to open). On `/` the hero film is held at its first frame until this moment, and that frame is the film's focus pull - so the eye opens and the picture comes into focus.

**When it opens** (`AppPreloader`): once the line is full (`SEAL_MS`, 380ms after the first screen is ready) and the logo has finished arriving - `INTRO_BRAND_MS` (1000ms) on every route, `INTRO_FLOOR_MS` (1400ms) on `/`, so the complete logo is seen for a moment before the film - and never later than `INTRO_CAP_MS` (3200ms). A cap or a tap that arrives before the line is full closes it in 220ms first: the eye always opens on a full line. Dismissible after `INTRO_DISMISS_AFTER_MS` (500ms from mount) by a tap, Enter or Escape. There is no longer a "dropped, not opened" path for a fast non-home load: with the brand floor, every curtain plays the same short sequence. Measured on the production build at 390x664: open at 1457ms and gone at 2597ms at 4x CPU, open at 1471ms and gone at 2656ms at 6x; `/doctors` opens at 1040ms.

**How the lids work - transforms only, on purpose.** Each lid (`.pl__lid--top` / `--bottom`) is paper with a half-ellipse cut out of the edge facing the other one, drawn as a `radial-gradient` (`51% var(--pl-dip) at 50% 100%`, transparent inside, paper outside). The two overlap by 1.5px at the centre and by the curve's depth at the sides, so together they cover the screen with no seam; opening is a `translate` on each, nothing else. `--pl-dip` (`min(16.667vw, 30vh)`) makes the almond about three times as wide as it is tall when its corners reach the sides of the screen. Under both lids sit two **shades** (`.pl__shade--*`), the same geometry with a soft navy band just inside the curve - the dark a lid casts on the page at its edge. **They are layers of their own because a band drawn on a lid lies across the other lid's paper wherever the two overlap**, which at rest is the whole width. The lockup sits above both lids (`z-index: 3`), so a curved edge can never cover a letter. Every moving part - the square, the mark, the name, the tagline, the line, the lids, the shades - is a `transform` or an `opacity` on an HTML box (the logo's parts are separate `<svg>` elements, not groups inside one SVG, because SVG children are not animated on the compositor). **That is what keeps it smooth while the page's script loads underneath**: at 4x and 6x CPU on the production build the only frame gaps over 40ms are in the first 0.2s, while the square is still fading in from nothing, and none through the opening; at 6x on the dev server, where the bundle is unminified, the logo's arrival dropped nothing while the app evaluated.

**Two traps:**

- **Chrome rejects `calc()` as a radial-gradient size.** `radial-gradient(calc(50% + 2px) 60px at ...)` is thrown away whole - `background-image: none` - and the lids were invisible: the page showed straight through the curtain from first paint. `51%` puts the arc's ends just past the screen edges instead. Probe a gradient by setting it on an element and reading it back before trusting a capture.
- **The page is told a frame after the opening is committed** (`data-phase="open"`, then `INTRO_DONE_EVENT` two `requestAnimationFrame`s later). The hero starts its film and its copy on the event, and that work landing in the opening's first frame delayed it (a 55ms frame at 6x CPU). The lids' 60ms beat covers the frame.

**The static frame.** `index.html` carries the markup (`#app-intro`: two shades, two lids, the brand, the seam, the tagline) and `%PL_*%` / `%BRAND_*%` tokens that `curtainHtml()` in `vite.config.js` fills on every transform, in dev and in the build: `%PL_CSS%` is `preloader.css` with its comments and indentation stripped (`minifyCss()`), and the logo is split by `splitLogo()` from `site.brand.logo` - the `<rect>` becomes the square's box (its fill and its 870:854 proportions, set inline), the white path is the mark, the other paths are the name, whose viewBox starts where the square ends. **If the logo file stops being a square, a white mark and a name, `splitLogo()` throws** rather than shipping a broken curtain. The plugin reads the files at transform time, so an edit to `preloader.css`, `site.json` or the logo shows on the next reload of the dev server - no restart (the plugin it replaced read `site.json` once at config time). Because the curtain inlines its own stylesheet it reads no token from `system.css`: its colours are the site's own values, written out on `.pl`. `index.html` is 19KB and 7.3KB gzipped with the curtain in it. An inline script stamps the clock and adds `intro-seen` to `<html>` when `sessionStorage.aakash_intro_seen` is set, which hides the frame before first paint; a `<noscript>` rule hides it without JavaScript, and a 10s animation lets go of the page if the script never arrives.

- **`AppPreloader` renders nothing, and waits for the pass after React adopts the page** (`useHydrated()`), so the veil, the lock and the curtain's line all start from the moment a page built fresh used to mount; that pass also lifts `html.app-adopting` once it is laid out. It finds `#app-intro`, locks the page (`body.pl-lock`, `inert` on `#root`, `touch-action: none` on the frame), drives the line, sets `data-phase="open"` and removes the frame on the top lid's `animationend` (`pl-lid-up`), with a timer as the fallback. Under React's double mount in development its cleanup releases the lock and the second run takes it again; the frame itself is never touched by a cleanup. It writes the session key as the opening starts, so a reload mid-opening skips it.
- **Sizes.** The logo is `clamp(200px, 15vw + 160px, 440px)` - 208px on a 320px phone, 218px at 390, 275px on a tablet, 376px at 1440, 440px from 1867px - and the tagline is sized off it (`clamp(0.98rem, logo * 0.056, 1.5rem)`). The line the eye opens along is `--pl-split` (48%), a little above the middle, so the lockup - more of it above the line than below - sits on the optical centre. **A phone held sideways** (`max-height: 520px`) gets a 180-240px logo and the split at 46%.
- **Reduced motion: the lockup is simply there, the line simply fills, and the curtain is removed when the page is ready** rather than opened - no floor, no opening, 0 running animations (decided in JS with `matchMedia`, and the CSS clears the entrance animations and their delays under `prefers-reduced-motion`, because the global rule zeroes durations but not delays). Measured: removed at 299ms.
- It is `role="status"` with `aria-label` "<name> is opening"; the logo is `aria-hidden` and the tagline is real text.
- **The reload veil (`#app-wait`) is bare paper, and then the still lockup.** Every page is prerendered, so a reload later in the visit paints the static copy at once; the veil (shown only under `html.intro-seen`, by the same inline script that hides the curtain) covers it until React has adopted the page and the reader's own values have arrived, because the static copy is the laptop shape, names the head office and holds the entrances' starting poses. `main.jsx` waits for the route's own module before React's first render, so that render is the page itself, and `AppPreloader` removes the veil in the pass after adoption - the moment a page built fresh used to mount. A cached route arrives in 50-150ms, so for 350ms it is paper and nothing else; only a genuinely slow reload gets the logo and the tagline, faded in (`pl-wait-in`) exactly where the curtain sets them. Without JavaScript the `<noscript>` rule hides it, and the 10s `pl-safety` animation lets go of the page if the script never arrives. It replaced `HydrateFallback`, a React component that did the same job before there was any static copy to cover.
- **Checked in Safari 27** (the user's desktop and phone browser) at 390x664 and 1180x700: the gradients are valid, the lids opaque, the logo, the serif and the accent identical to Chrome, the opening the same slit-to-almond-to-full in the same 1.87-2.2s, the frame removed and `#root` released. **Freezing composited animations does not work for a Safari check** - the method the iris curtain was checked with (pause every animation, then block the page with a synchronous XHR while a listener captures the window) captured frames well ahead of their labels, because Safari keeps composited animations running while the page is blocked. Record the window instead: `screencapture -x -o -v -V 4 -l <window id>` from the listener, then read frames with AVFoundation (`AVAssetImageGenerator` in a few lines of Swift - the ffmpeg in the Playwright cache cannot read QuickTime).
- The logo is `public/assets/logo.svg`, the one file the header, the drawer, the footer, the reload wait frame and the curtain (split by `vite.config.js`) all read through `site.brand.logo`; `JsonLd` reads `site.brand.logoRaster` instead, because structured data and social cards want a raster. It is the hospital's own vector lockup - the blue emblem, `AAKASH` in magenta and `EYE HOSPITAL` in grey - with the C2PA provenance manifest stripped (7.7KB of base64, 48% of the supplied file, shipped to every visitor and saying nothing about the brand). 8.3KB raw, 3.5KB gzipped, against 29.5KB for the PNG it replaced, and resolution-independent at every size the site renders it. **It carries no `YOUR EYES IN SAFE HAND` line**, which the PNG did: at header sizes that line was illegible noise, and the tagline is already set as real type on this curtain and in the footer. The lockup is 4.14:1 rather than the PNG's 3:1, so at the same width the wordmark is half as tall again - which is why `width`/`height` attributes are `207`/`50` everywhere an `<img>` carries them. **`EYE HOSPITAL` is `#5A5B5A` and measures 2.6:1 on navy**: the lockup is for light grounds only, which is every ground it is used on today. Re-export from the hospital's vector master, never trace the PNG.
- **`public/assets/logo.png` is still shipped, and its only jobs are `site.brand.logoRaster` (JSON-LD, social cards) and being the source the browser icons were cut from.** It is a 720x240 encode of the hospital's 3552x1184 raster master (`~/Downloads/logof.png`), and it carries the `YOUR EYES IN SAFE HAND` line the SVG drops, so it is **not** an interchangeable fallback for the SVG - do not swap one for the other. **The master's "transparent" background is opaque white** (alpha 255 everywhere, pixels at 252-254 rather than 255), so it is keyed, never used as shipped: white-matte removal (`alpha = 255 - min(r,g,b)`, colour un-premultiplied against white) over the whole image, with the blue emblem square (bbox 0,0-870,853 in the master) forced fully opaque because its white is paint, not background; then alpha under 16 zeroed and over 240 snapped to 255, because the source's near-white noise left a haze that PNG could not compress (178KB) - cleaned and quantised to 128 colours it is 29KB. Re-encode from the master with that recipe, never edit the shipped PNG.

## Header

`src/components/Header.jsx` is the only consumer of `src/styles/header.css`. It is built to the **Function Health** header, and the reason that header works is that it is not a bar on top of the page but part of whatever it sits on.
