Measured on functionhealth.com before building: a 40px promo strip, then an 80px capsule inset 64px from the window at 1440, `border-radius: 12px`, `rgba(0,0,0,0.07)` with `backdrop-filter: blur(20px)` over the film; scrolled onto paper it becomes a flat full-width band in the page's own colour, the logo turns brand-coloured and the pill fills.

**Three parts.**

1. **The strip** (`.hd__strip`, 44px, `--e-navy`, desktop only) is the reference's promo band carrying what a hospital's should: the emergency line with its number on the left, and on the right the hospital this reader has chosen with that hospital's own OPD number and the picker. **It is not sticky** - it scrolls away with the page, as the reference's does, and it is rendered as a sibling before the `<header>` (the component returns a fragment), so it is outside the sticky box.
2. **The capsule** (`.hd__cap`) is one element with two shapes, chosen by `data-tone` on the header:
   - **`film`** - only on `/`, while the hero is under it: inset (`min(100% - 32px, var(--e-shell) + 64px)`, so its content lands exactly on the page's shell), 68px tall, 14px radius, `rgba(7,20,33,0.2)` glass with `blur(18px)`, a 1px white edge at 0.14, the white logo (`logo-light.svg`), white links, and a cream pill with navy type - the hero button's own colours.
   - **`paper`** - everywhere else, and on `/` once the film has gone: the full width and full height of the sticky box, `rgba(255,255,255,0.92)` frosted, padded so its content sits on the shell, the brand-coloured logo, ink links and a navy pill with cream type. At rest it has no edge and merges into the page's first section; once the page has moved (`hd--scrolled`) it takes a hairline and a soft shadow.
     Every property that differs is transitioned (460ms, the site's ease), so crossing the bottom of the film is one continuous morph with the logo standing still, not a swap.
3. **The phone menu** below 1180px - a full-screen sheet revealed by a circle of paper growing from the menu button; see the Mobile menu section.

The build this replaced was a white two-row bar (six objects on a 76px brand row over a nav row, then a tidier rail-and-bar version) with no relationship to the film under it: the page's most expensive asset began below a slab of white.

- **`--hd-height` is the sticky box** - the capsule and the air above and below it (`--hd-cap-h + 2 * --hd-cap-gap`): **88px on desktop, 72px below 1180px**. The paper band fills exactly that box, so both tones are the same height and **changing tone never reflows the document** - verified: `scrollHeight` and `main`'s offset are identical across the switch in both directions. `--hd-strip-h` (44px, `0px` below 1180) is its own token because the strip is outside the box. Every sticky offset on the site (`top: calc(var(--hd-height) + 20px)`, `body:has(.hd--hidden) ... { top: 0 }`) reads `--hd-height` and followed the change without edits; the sweep checked all eleven routes.
- **The film runs up under the capsule on `/` only.** `.hd[data-over]` (set when the path is `/`, a constant per route, never toggled by scroll) carries `margin-bottom: calc(var(--hd-height) * -1)`, and the hero's `min-height` is `100svh - var(--hd-strip-h)` - so the hero still ends exactly on `innerHeight`, measured at 320 to 1920px. **`.hd` is `display: flow-root`**: without it the film capsule's `margin-top` collapsed through the header box and opened a white strip between the navy band and the film at every width.
- **The box is `pointer-events: none` and only the capsule and the drawer take the pointer**, so the transparent air around the film capsule never covers the hero. **`pointer-events` inherits, so each of those two has to set `auto` itself** - `.hd__cap` does, and `.hd__drawer` does. The drawer shipped once without it: every tap on the sheet went through to the page below, so the old drawer's `Emergency` pill (which sits over the capsule's `Book` pill) opened `/appointment` instead of dialling, and no row, action or close button in the sheet responded. Keyboard input still worked, so only a real touch test shows it. Anything new placed inside `.hd` outside the capsule needs its own `pointer-events: auto`. The full-screen sheet that replaced it is tap-tested the same way - see the Mobile menu section.
- **The tone is decided in JS** (`overFilm`, a scroll/resize listener keyed on `isHome`): film while the top edge of the section after the hero - the numbers sheet, `hero.nextElementSibling` - is below the header's own bottom (the strip's remaining height plus the box), paper once it passes. It read the hero's bottom before the sheet overlapped it; that edge now sits `--sheet-r` under the paper. The hero is a lazy route module and may not exist when the listener subscribes, so the state starts at film and is reset to film on every route change, which is what the first screen of `/` is. Verified at 1440 and 390: film at the top and at 200px of hero left, paper at 60, back to film on the way up.
- **The glass is measured against the film, not assumed.** The hero carries a layer that belongs to the header - `.e-hero__shade`, a navy shade across the top `--hd-height + 90px` of the film (`+ 70px` on phones), held under the header for the whole of the hero's scroll exit - because the skylight and exam-room ceiling are the brightest frames in the loop. Pixel-sampled worst cell across seven timestamps, glyphs made transparent with the glass left painted: links 7.04-11.55:1 at 1440, the phone menu glyph 4.15:1 (3:1 needed); the pill is navy on white at 14.3:1. The glass started at 0.34 and was lightened to 0.2 against those numbers, to look more like the reference; **if the film or the scrim changes, re-measure before lightening further.**
- **Two logo files, one lockup**, stacked and cross-faded with the capsule: `logo.svg` in the brand colours and `logo-light.svg` - generated from it with the wordmark white and `EYE HOSPITAL` at 0.84, the emblem keeping its own blue square. The light one is only mounted on `/`. **Regenerate `logo-light.svg` from `logo.svg` whenever the logo changes** (swap the two wordmark fills; nothing else).
- **The nav is centred on the page at every desktop width** (`minmax(0, 1fr) auto minmax(0, 1fr)`), and it can be because it carries **five links: the logo is the way home**, as on the reference, and the gallery page is gone. The drawer keeps Home - a menu the reader opened to go somewhere should list everywhere they can go. Links are 44px pills with 10px padding and a soft hover plate, 2px apart. Measured on both tones: the nav's centre on the window's to the pixel from 1180 to 1920px, the booking pill 105px clear of `Contact` on the film at 1180 (129px on paper) and 183px from 1366. With six links the film capsule was 12px short at 1180, and a `1180-1320px` override centred the nav between the logo and the actions with tighter padding; it went with the sixth link. The five links would stay centred down to about 1000px (they meet at 970), but the 1180px split stays, because every sticky offset and short-screen rule on the site is measured against it.
- **One indicator travels between sections** - `--hd-ink-x` / `--hd-ink-w` / `--hd-ink-o` written to the nav from a location-keyed effect and a `ResizeObserver`, not a framer `layoutId`, so the root bundle carries no framer-motion (verified in the built chunk). **It is measured with `getBoundingClientRect()` against the nav, never `offsetLeft`**: a dropdown trigger's offset parent is its own `.hd__mega-wrap`, so `offsetLeft` read 0 and the bar sat at the nav's left edge on every About and hospitals page. It runs under the label, inset by the link's padding; white on film, accent on paper.
- **The capsule carries one action - the booking pill - and nothing beside it.** A red `Emergency` text link with a siren sat there in the reference's `Log in` slot and was taken out: a second, differently coloured call to action next to the pill read as clutter and fought it. The emergency line is not lost - on desktop it leads the strip, on phones it is in the menu sheet's head, and it is in the footer and on the contact page.
- **Film-tone overrides on the pill are scoped to `.hd__cap`.** The phone menu lives inside the header, so an unscoped `.hd[data-tone="film"] .hd__cta` would restyle anything in it carrying `.hd__cta` whenever it is opened over the film - that once turned the old drawer's booking white on its light foot. The sheet's own booking is `.hd__sheet-book` and does not carry the class.
- **The pill is the header's one deliberate departure from the site's 3-4px rectangles** (the hero's phone CTA was the first): the header is built to the reference and the reference's action is a pill. **It is drawn in the hero's two colours**: cream (`#fbf7ef`) with navy type on the film, the same object as the hero's button under it, and navy with cream type on paper (the menu sheet's booking is the same navy and cream), with a soft navy shadow and a 1px inner highlight. The colours transition with the capsule (360ms), so crossing the bottom of the film turns the pill over rather than swapping it. **A magenta pill was built and sent back** - on the film it jumped out of a calm hero; do not reach for the accent to make the action louder. The desktop pill carries the calendar glyph the phone's always had. Pixel-sampled worst cell: label 13.35:1 on both tones at 320, 390 and 1440px; the nav keeps at least 108px of clearance from the pill at 1180-1440px.
- **Visnagar answers its OPD on the emergency line**, so the strip compares `cleanTel()` of the two and prints one number under `emergency.combinedLabel` when they match - the footer's rule.
- **Below 1180px** (not the old 1040): the strip, the nav and the desktop actions go, and the capsule carries the logo, the booking pill and the menu glyph; `--hd-cap-h` is 56 and `--hd-cap-gap` 8. Six links, the emergency line and the pill need about 1040px of capsule, and below 1180 it has less; a tablet held sideways gets the menu sheet, as on the reference. **There is still no band on a phone** - the strip is desktop-only, for the reason two earlier phone bands were removed (a band under a browser toolbar whose colour the page does not control). The phone's hospital picker rises from the menu sheet's dock.
- **The phone film capsule is inset 12px with 13px before the logo and 3px after the menu glyph** (plus its 1px edge), so the glyph's right edge is 16px in - where the paper band puts it and where the menu sheet's close button lands. Measured: the two coincide horizontally to 0.0px in both tones at 320, 360, 390, 430, 768, 1024 and 1179px, and vertically to 0.0px on paper (film carries a 0.5px vertical offset from the glass edge).
- **On a phone the capsule always carries a compact booking pill** - on every screen, the home film included. Two shapes were tried first and both were sent back: a full-width white `Book appointment` pill packed the capsule edge to edge, and hiding the pill while the hero's own button was on screen left the capsule as a logo and a menu glyph, which read as an empty bar on the most visited screen of the site. The pill is 38px drawn and 44px to the finger through an `::after` halo, with a calendar glyph so the short label (`bookShortLabel`, `Book`) cannot be misread; `Book appointment` returns from 600px, where a tablet has the room. Cream on film, navy on paper, like the desktop pill. The emergency pill lives in the menu sheet's head, in this pill's slot and at its size. **The menu button is a bare glyph**, as on the reference - three navy CSS bars (`MenuGlyph`) at 14:1 identify the control on their own, and the sheet's close button is the same glyph in the same rect turning into a cross.
- **Compaction is gone.** Nothing in the header changes its height on scroll; `isScrolled` only gives the paper band its hairline and shadow. The old build shrank a row (and then parked a rail on a negative sticky `top`) and needed thresholds and a settle window to stop the flicker that caused. Hide-on-scroll-down and instant return on scroll-up are unchanged (see the Hospitals menu section for how direction is read).
- **Checked in Safari** (the user's desktop browser), which is where a `backdrop-filter` would misbehave: the strip, the glass, the white lockup and the pill render as in Chrome. The capsule carries `-webkit-backdrop-filter` as well, and an `@supports not` block gives the glass the density the blur would have lent it where neither is supported.
- **Do not add a first-visit "choose your hospital" prompt.** A first visit already has the opening curtain and the consent sheet; a third interruption is what makes people close the tab. The menu sheet's dock names the chosen hospital, and whether its OPD is open, every time it opens - the non-blocking version.
- `index.html` sets `theme-color` to white and declares `color-scheme: light`.
- **`--hd-navy` and `--hd-blue` read `--e-navy` / `--e-navy-2`**, the design system's pair, so the header can never drift from the pages and the footer.
- The header keeps a selected branch under `aakash_selected_branch` through `storeBranch()` - in `localStorage` once the reader allows `Website experience`, in `sessionStorage` until then (see Cookie consent). It drives the strip's OPD number, the Book query string and the menu sheet's call/WhatsApp/directions actions, and it adopts the branch whenever a page is opened for one (`?branch=<slug>`, or `/branches/<slug>`). **It writes the key only once React has adopted the page**: the prerendered header holds the head office, and storing that would overwrite the reader's own hospital; the location sync runs again in that pass, so a hospital's own page still names its hospital rather than the stored one. **The strip is outside `headerRef`, so the outside-click handler checks `stripRef` too** - without it the picker closed on `pointerdown` before its option's click landed. **Being outside `.hd`, the strip also declares the header's tokens itself** (`.hd, .hd__strip { --hd-navy ... }`): it used to read them from nowhere, so its picker inherited the strip's white text - the panel's title and `View all hospitals` were white on the white panel, with no border and no blue on the chosen row. **And the picker closes when the page scrolls** (a wheel, trackpad or keyboard scroll past 8px; a touch scroll already closes it through `pointerdown`): it hangs from a bar that scrolls away, and left open it rode up over the sticky header and covered the booking pill. The nav menus hang from the header itself and stay open.
- **The dropdown heading is a `<p>` and resets its margin** - global.css's paragraph margin had opened 16px of empty panel under `ABOUT US` / `OUR HOSPITALS`.
- **Every hover plate in the capsule and the strip is gated on `(hover: hover) and (pointer: fine)`**, with `:active` for a finger. A tablet held sideways (iPad Air 1180x820, iPad Pro 1366x1024) gets the desktop header under a finger, and a tap leaves `:hover` on the link it opened; the header outlives the route, so the grey plate stayed on `Doctors` for the whole doctors page. Verified by touch at both sizes: dropdowns open by tap and close on a tap elsewhere, the strip picker sets the OPD number, a flick hides the header.
- **On the film and on the navy strip the focus ring is the headline's cream (`#fbf7ef`)**, as on the hero's own controls. The site's magenta measured 2.05:1 against the desktop glass and about 1.1:1 on the phone capsule over the skylight - invisible; cream is 10.9:1 (median ring pixel) on the desktop film, 13.4:1 on the strip and about 4.2:1 on the phone capsule, where the ring's outer edge crosses onto the bright film. It is scoped to the named controls (`.hd__brand`, the nav links and triggers, `.hd__cta`, `.hd__iconbtn`, the strip's items and picker toggle), because the white dropdown panels hang inside the capsule and the strip and a cream ring would vanish on them. Paper keeps the magenta. Measure a ring by diffing a focused and an unfocused capture and comparing each changed pixel with what was under it.
- Nav items come from `navigation.header`; an item with a `dropdown` key renders `NavDropdown` (`"branches"`, `"about"`). All header copy - labels, the emergency line and its `combinedLabel`, `bookLabel` / `bookShortLabel`, `menuLabel`, `opdLabel`, the branch picker's strings and the menu sheet's `drawer` block - lives in `site.header`; `logo`, `logoLight` and `logoRaster` in `site.brand`.
- **A dropdown's `onClose` only clears `openMenu` if that menu is still the open one**; a fine pointer's click on a trigger keeps the menu open rather than toggling it; the panel hangs 9px below the capsule with a 13px transparent bridge (`.hd__mega-wrap` centres its 44px trigger in the capsule, `align-items: center`); the panel never scrolls, its list does; both menus are one `NavDropdown` - add rows, never fork it.
- **Count braces after deleting any rule near the end of `header.css`** (`{` minus `}` must be 0): an earlier `1040px` block lost its closing brace and silently swallowed the blocks after it.
- **Measured with no page overflow, no console errors, no capsule children overlapping and no header or strip target under 44px** (hit-tested with `elementFromPoint`) on eleven routes at 320x568, 360x640, 390x844, 430x932, 768x1024, 1024x768, 1179x800, 1180x800, 1240x800, 1280x800, 1320x800, 1440x900 and 1920x1080, with the numbers sheet starting on `innerHeight`, `--hd-height` equal to the header box, and the tone `film` on `/` and `paper` everywhere else. Under `prefers-reduced-motion` the tone still switches and nothing in the header animates.

### Hospitals menu

The hospitals menu is `NavDropdown` fed by `branches.json`; the phone menu renders the same list as its picker (`.hd__pick`).

- It is a **single 340px column**, not the 660px two-column mega panel it replaced. Six short city names read faster in one column, and a narrow panel sits under its own trigger instead of spanning half the header - the wide version was a white slab dropped over the hero with no visible relationship to the link that opened it.
- The panel is offset left by `--hd-mega-inset` (22px, its own padding plus the row padding) so the branch names line up under the nav label, and a small caret points back at the trigger.
- The six identical `MapPin` tiles are gone. They repeated the same glyph six times and told the reader nothing; the row now carries name, `Head Office` tag and locality, with a check on the branch the reader is on (or has selected) and a chevron that appears on hover only. Six permanent arrows would be the same mistake as six pins.
- The check marks `activeSlug ?? selectedSlug`, and `aria-current="page"` is set only when the URL is actually showing that branch. It is deliberately **not** the italic accent the phone menu gives the page the reader is on - that means "the page you are on", and "your hospital" is a different claim.
- A 10px gap sits between the trigger and the panel, so the panel bridges it with a transparent `::before` strip and closes on a 160ms timer. Without both, the menu died halfway through the diagonal travel from the link to the first row.
- Keyboard: `ArrowDown` on the trigger opens the menu and focuses the first city, `ArrowDown` / `ArrowUp` / `Home` / `End` move between rows, `Escape` closes and returns focus to the trigger. Hover-open is still gated on `(hover: hover) and (pointer: fine)`.
- The panel caps its height against the viewport and scrolls, so a short laptop window still reaches Juhapura and `View all hospitals`.
- Below 1180px there is no dropdown at all - the phone menu's picker carries the same six hospitals, and on a phone it is the only selector. The route to a hospital's own page is `Our Hospitals` and each row of `/branches`.
- Nothing compacts on scroll any more: the strip scrolls away by itself and the capsule keeps its height, so `isScrolled` (past 4px) only adds the paper band's hairline and shadow. See the Header section.
- The header slides away at every width (`hd--hidden`, a `translateY(-100%)` transform) while the reader scrolls down, and returns on the first scroll up. It reclaims 72px on a phone and 88px on desktop. There is no bottom bar to fall back on, so the reveal has to be instant at every width - never gate it behind a delay, and never lengthen the scroll-up distance needed to bring it back.
- **Direction is read from travel, never from one frame's delta.** Movement is summed while it keeps its sign and reset the moment it reverses, and the header changes state once the sum passes `TRAVEL` (12px). The per-frame `DELTA` of 6px it replaced failed on real devices: a slow reading scroll of 3-4px a frame never crossed it in either direction (the header neither hid nor came back), and a 120Hz phone halves every frame's delta, so ordinary flicks read as jitter there too. The reset on reversal is what still filters trackpad jitter and an iOS rubber-band bounce.
- **There is no settle window and no timer, and there must not be one again.** Two guards were carried for the build that compacted by shrinking a row: a `SETTLE_MS` window that ignored any frame moving less than `SETTLE_NOISE`, to filter the scroll-anchoring adjustment the height change caused. The header no longer changes height at all, so both were removed. An earlier version of that window also deferred _everything_ to a `resolveSettle()` timer, and that had a hole a real phone found every time: a scroll event under `HIDE_AFTER` disarmed the timer, so a flick from the top of the page that stopped inside the window never applied its direction and the header simply did not hide until the reader flicked again. **Re-check that the document never moves before trusting any change here** - its `scrollHeight` and `main`'s offset must be identical across a tone switch and a hide/show, on desktop and on a phone. Verified with real touch flicks (`Input.dispatchTouchEvent`) and wheel events: one flick from y=0 hides on every phone and tablet size, one wheel burst hides on every desktop size, a 3px-a-step drag hides on the way down and returns on the way up, and the drawer-open, under-160px and at-bottom guards are unchanged.
- The hide is guarded so it can never trap the user: it does not engage above 160px of scroll, while any dropdown or the drawer is open (`menusOpenRef`), at the very bottom of the page, or while something inside the header carries a **visible** focus (`.hd:has(:focus-visible)`). A route change resets it. **That guard was `.hd:focus-within`, and it was the second half of "the header does not hide on my phone but does after a reload"**: closing the drawer returns focus to the menu button (correct for keyboard readers), a scroll gesture is not a tap so nothing on a phone ever blurred it again, and the header sat pinned at `transform: none` with `hd--hidden` on it through every scroll until a reload dropped the focus. Programmatic focus after a tap does not match `:focus-visible`; Escape from a keyboard does, so a keyboard reader who closes the drawer still keeps the header. Verified: after a touch open-and-close the header hides on the next flick with focus still on the button; after an Escape close it stays until the button blurs.
- The header is `position: sticky` and hides with a transform, so nothing reflows and the page never shifts when it moves. Keep it that way - animating height or toggling `display` here would cost layout on every scroll frame.
- **`--hd-slide` (`300ms cubic-bezier(0.32, 0.72, 0, 1)`, on `:root` in `header.css`) is the slide, and anything pinned under the header moves its `top` on it** - the doctors filter rail and the hospitals map do. A pinned bar on its own timing either jumps (no transition: 72px in one frame, with the page showing under the header as it slid back) or runs ahead of the header (160ms: a 27px gap). Measure a new one with a per-frame log of the header's bottom edge against the bar's top edge; they should agree to 0.1px on every frame of the hide and the reveal.
- **Measured for the deployment pass** with real input - finger drags on phones and tablets, the mouse wheel from 1180px - and a per-frame log: the header hides only past 160px and fully leaves the screen, returns one frame (17ms) after 12px of upward travel and is 95% in 167ms later, survives a 3px-a-step crawl and scroll jitter in both states, returns at the very bottom, and restores the film tone at the top of `/`; `main`'s offset never moves. At 4x CPU throttle on a 390x664 the frames stay at 17ms. Checked at 320x568, 360x640, 375x553, 390x664, 390x844, 393x659, 430x746, 844x390, 768x1024, 1024x768, 1179x800, 1180x800, 1366x768, 1440x900, 1920x1080 and 2560x1440 across the home, doctors, hospitals, service, hospital, journey, contact and booking pages, and the header's statics at those sizes and more on fourteen routes. Checked in Safari as well: the same states and numbers. **A test rig that runs several headless Chromes at once drops synthetic input** - whole runs where no gesture scrolls, an 8px "drag" that lands as a tap - and long frames that do not reproduce alone; run the timing checks one browser at a time.
- `html` carries `scroll-padding-top` in `global.css` so anchor targets land below the sticky header rather than under it.

### Mobile menu

The phone menu is the `.hd__glyph` / `.hd__drawer` / `.hd__bloom` / `.hd__sheet*` / `.hd__place*` / `.hd__picker*` / `.hd__pick*` rules in `header.css`. **It is a full-screen sheet built to a reference the hospital supplied** (Titan's phone menu): the places a patient goes to set large, the rest small under them, and a dock at the foot. It replaced a `min(400px, 92%)` side panel over a dimmed page - a tinted two-control hospital card, a `MENU` label, eight bold rows with a tinted active plate and a magenta inset bar, and a grey foot of three bordered icon tiles. Every object was boxed and it read as a form.

**Three passes, three complaints from a real phone, all fixed structurally - keep them.** The second pass answered "the dock is confusing" and "the animation stops and then comes back". The third answered "the look is still inconsistent": the menu had been tuned on a 390x844 capture, but **an iPhone in Safari has about 390x664 of visible viewport** with its toolbars showing, so the user was seeing the short-screen fallback - a card with its `Your hospital` label dropped (a bare `Visnagar` with nothing saying what it was), and on an SE (375x553) the small links scrolled under the fade altogether. Measure this menu at Safari's visible heights - 390x664, 393x659, 430x746, 375x553 - and at 360x560 for a small Android, not at device heights.

**The motion, and why it is built this way.**

- **The sheet is always mounted and hidden** (`visibility: hidden`, `pointer-events: none`, `inert`), so opening is one attribute change on `data-state`. The first build mounted it on open and revealed it by animating `clip-path`; both run on the main thread, so the mount stalled the first frames and the circle jumped, and a route loading under a closing menu froze it mid-shut. **Do not go back to `clip-path` or a mount-on-open.** Measured: 16-17ms frames through the whole opening at 4x CPU throttle, and a tap reaches the first frame in 28ms at 4x (49ms at 6x) on the production build - the dev server's unminified React takes 70ms at 4x, which is the number a phone on the LAN sees.
- **The menu glyph is three CSS bars (`MenuGlyph`), and the close button is the same glyph in the same place turning into a cross** - the bars close onto the middle one, then the pair turns (`hd-bar-top` / `hd-bar-mid` / `hd-bar-bottom`, 380ms), and the reverse on the way out. A single rotate-and-translate passed through a `>` for a few frames. It replaced a lucide `Menu` glyph that vanished under the circle while a separate grey X plate rotated in beside it; the close button is now bare, like the button it replaces. On the film the cross fades to white as the circle closes on it, so it hands over to the capsule's white glyph without a colour change on the last frame.
- **Everything that moves is a transform or an opacity.** `.hd__bloom` is a circle of paper whose radius is the distance from the menu button to the farthest corner of the screen (`measureBloom()`, plus 2px), scaled from `0.001` to `1` over 520ms on the header's ease-out (`cubic-bezier(0.32, 0.72, 0, 1)`). **The ease-out is the point**: the ease-in-out it replaced barely moved for its first tenth of a second, which read as the tap not landing. It casts a soft navy shadow onto the page (`0 0 44px`), so paper reads as sliding over paper; the magenta rim it replaced showed as a dotted ring at the button on its first frame and cut a magenta arc through the page as it grew.
- **Content arrives with the circle, not after it.** `stageReveal()` in `Header.jsx` measures how far each `[data-reveal]` element's farthest corner is from the button and writes the moment the bloom's edge reaches it as `--hd-at`, inverting the same curve - `BLOOM_MS` / `BLOOM_EASE` there must match `.hd__drawer[data-state="open"] .hd__bloom`. Each element fades up 10px over 440ms from its own moment, so the whole menu has settled by about 0.7s (it was 1.1s). The head's objects and the two hairlines fade without rising - something in the header's own row that slides reads as the header moving.
- Once the bloom has landed the sheet carries its own paper (`hd-ground`, a 1ms step at 520ms), so a phone turned with the menu open is always covered; it drops the paper the moment the menu starts to shut.
- **Shutting**: the content fades in 120ms and the circle starts closing at 90ms (420ms, `cubic-bezier(0.3, 0.6, 0.2, 1)`), leaving at speed and slowing into the button, and fading over its last 30% so it does not linger as a white dot on the film. A slow-starting shut held an all-white frame between the fade and the page. The bloom's `animationend` for `hd-bloom-shut` sets `closed`; a 900ms timer is the fallback. `hd--drawer-open` holds on the header through `closing` and carries `transition: none`, because the sheet is `position: fixed` inside `.hd` and any transform on `.hd` would become its containing block.
- **On paper the sheet's logo never animates**, in or out: it lies exactly on the header's own logo. The sheet's head carries `padding-bottom: 1px` because the paper band's 1px bottom border moves its row half a pixel up; with it the logo, the pill and the glyph land on the header's to 0.0px at every phone size. Over the home film the logo fades (the film capsule's logo is white and 10px further in).
- **A link tap waits for its page, then closes over it.** `leaveDrawerFor()` marks the tapped link (`data-pending`: it holds its step to the right in the accent and draws a line under itself), and the location sync closes the menu once the new page has committed; a link to the page already on screen closes at once.
- **Hover plates are for a real pointer only** (`(hover: hover) and (pointer: fine)`), on the menu button, the cross, the picker's close, both booking pills and the small links. A touch leaves `:hover` on whatever it tapped, so the grey plate showed behind the cross while the menu closed and stayed on the capsule's menu button afterwards. A finger gets `:active` instead.
- The emergency dot's beacon is a transform/opacity ring on `::after`, and only runs while the menu is open.

**The layout: three bands on one paper.**

- **Head, links, dock, split by two matching hairlines** (`.hd__sheet-rule` under the head and its twin over the dock). There is no card inside the sheet - the grey 24px-radius card with white discs it replaced was the one surface in the menu, and the fourth way the site drew Call / WhatsApp / Directions.
- **The head is the header's own row, object for object**: the logo where the logo was, the emergency line in the booking pill's slot at the booking pill's geometry (38px drawn, 44px through an `::after` halo, a borderless `--hd-emergency-soft` pill with a red dot), and the cross where the menu glyph was. Opening the menu turns one pill into another rather than dropping a bigger plate into the row.
- **The links are the display serif** (`--e-serif`, 400) at `clamp(1.75rem, min(8.6vw, 5.4svh), 2.4rem)` - the width sets the size, and on the shortest screens the visible height caps it (`svh`, because Safari's `vh` is the height with its toolbars hidden). `clamp(2.5rem, 5.4vw, 3.4rem)` from 700px. **The page the reader is on is set in the italic accent.** `Home`, `Services`, `Doctors`, `Our Hospitals` and `Contact` are large; `Our journey` and `Vision and mission` are small (0.98rem, `--e-ink-2`) **in one row on every phone**, 320px included - they were stacked rows on a tall screen and one row on a short one, so two phones showed two different menus. The split is data - `"drawerGroup": "more"` in `navigation.json` - and a parent with `children` is replaced by its children.
- **The list fades at its foot only when there is more** (`data-more`, from `markMore()` on open, on scroll and on resize, the doctors rail's device). It reads offsets rather than `scrollHeight` because the rows are still rising when it first runs and a transformed row counts toward the scrollable overflow - which is also why `.hd__sheet-body` is `position: relative`.
- **The dock is a field, three pills and the booking.** The hospital is a labelled field (`.hd__place`): the site's eyebrow (`Your hospital`, the `.e-label` values) over a soft pill (`.hd__place-field`, `--e-paper-2` with a `--hd-rule-soft` inset edge, 62px, 50px under 620px of height) holding a navy `MapPin`, the hospital's name in the serif and **whether its OPD is open right now** (`describeOpd()`, the hospital's own `hours` through `getOpenState`, green when open), and `Change` with lucide's `ChevronsUpDown` - the mark a select carries - at its end. The whole pill is the one control that opens the picker; it presses to 0.985 on `--e-paper-3`, and the button is named by the label plus its own content (`aria-labelledby`), so a screen reader hears `Your hospital, Visnagar, OPD open until 8:00 PM, Change`. **It replaced a bare line with an outlined `Change` pill at its end, and the hospital sent that back as the one part of the menu they were not confident in**: the pill was the same white, `--hd-edge`-bordered, navy-worded object as the three actions under it, so the dock read as four actions; it was 36px beside their 48px, centred on a three-line block so it aligned to nothing; and its down chevron promised a dropdown where a sheet rises from below. Five shapes were rendered side by side before choosing - an outlined select box, the booking pill's light twin with a navy disc, an accent text link, a navy pin disc, and this one; the navy discs competed with the booking button and the link read as a web form's. The fill is the emergency pill's borderless soft-plate device in the site's paper, so it is a choice rather than an action, and the word and the mark identify it - the fill is not a boundary that needs 3:1. Below 360px the pin goes, so Himmatnagar and its hours keep one line each in the 288px field. The name and the hours rise into the field together when the picker drops (`.hd__place-text` is keyed on the hospital). **The label shows at every size** - it is what says `Visnagar` is the reader's hospital.
- **Call OPD, WhatsApp and Directions are three pills** with the hospital page's tile vocabulary - the 3:1 `--hd-edge`, a navy glyph, one word each - glyph beside word, 48px (46px under 620px of height). Pills, because every other control in the sheet (the emergency line, the hospital field, the booking) is a pill and 14px boxes among them were the one shape out of place. Below 360px wide the word goes under the glyph (56px), as on the hospital page, because a 90px pill cannot hold both on one line. Measured with `scrollWidth`: no label clips at 320, 360, 375, 390, 393, 412 or 430px.
- **The booking** is the hero's button turned over: a 54px navy pill with cream type and the hero's cream arrow disc (48px under 620px of height). Not magenta.
- **Under 620px of visible height** (an SE, a small Android) the dock and the list take the air out of their padding, the hospital field drops to 50px with 4px under its label, and the name steps to 1.2rem; nothing is dropped and no text a patient reads shrinks. Measured with nothing to scroll at 390x664, 393x659, 430x746, 412x830, 390x844, 375x553, 360x560, 844x390 and 768x1024, and - since the gallery link left the small row and it stopped wrapping - at 320x568 too (it scrolled 35px before). Re-measured for the hospital field at all of those plus 393x659 and 360x640: nothing to scroll; the label and field cost 12px more than the old line, and the short tier's tighter gaps are what pay for it - without them 375x553 scrolled 13px.
- **The hospitals rise from the foot as a sheet of their own** (`.hd__picker`, 20px top corners, 480ms on the header's ease) over a dimmed menu (`.hd__picker-scrim`, which closes it on a tap). The menu under it never moves. The picker is a title with its own close (`.hd__picker-close`, the soft round plate every bottom sheet carries - the menu's own close is the glyph because it is the menu button), the hint, six hairline rows (the name in the serif, the street under it, a `Head office` tag, and a ring that fills navy and draws a check on the chosen one) and `View all hospitals`. Choosing sets the hospital in place and drops the sheet; the new name rises into the line. **The picker and scrim set their own `visibility`, so their selectors carry `:not([data-state="closed"])`**, and the flag is reset on every navigation and in `closeAll()`. Under 620px of height the picker covers everything below the head, so the scrim's tap target there is the head band.
- **From 700px wide, and on a phone held sideways**, the links take the left and the dock sits at the foot of a right column (`minmax(300px, 380px)`, `minmax(280px, 340px)` sideways), its hairline ending on the column's padding.
- **Focus and keys.** While the picker is up the menu is `inert`, and while it is down the picker is; the Tab trap filters `[inert]`. Tab runs Emergency, the cross, the eight links, the hospital field, the three pills and the booking, and wraps both ways. Focus goes into the chosen hospital when the picker rises and back onto the hospital field when it drops, in a `requestAnimationFrame` because the layer is still inert until the state commits. Escape backs out one layer at a time: the picker, then the menu, then focus returns to the menu button.
- **Reduced motion**: the menu, the picker and the close are instant, with 0 running animations and every part - the cross included - in its finished state. The header's reduced-motion block zeroes delays as well as durations, and **it has to name `.hd *::before` and `.hd *::after`** - `.hd *` does not select pseudo-elements.
- Contrast on white: links 18.2:1, the active accent 6.4:1, small links and the eyebrow 7.8:1, the three pills and the cross 14.3:1, the hospital's name and `Change` 13.0:1 on the field (12.0:1 pressed), open status (`--color-success`) 5.9:1 on the field and closed status 7.1:1, the emergency word 6.5:1 on its plate, the booking 13.4:1, the pills' edge 3.48:1.
- **Tap-tested with real `Input.dispatchTouchEvent` taps** at 390x664, 375x553, 320x568 and 844x390: every control hit-tests to itself; Emergency and Call OPD dial, WhatsApp and Directions open their links, the field opens the picker, a row, the scrim and the picker's close drop it, a chosen hospital moves the field, the Call number and the stored key, every link and Book navigate and the menu closes over the new page, `View all hospitals` navigates with the picker reset, and a drag over the menu leaves the page's `scrollY` where it was. With the menu closed nothing in it is visible, hit-testable or tabbable, and the menu button wears no plate. Checked in Safari as well as Chrome at 390x664: the cross, the pills and the type render the same.
- All copy is in `site.header`: `drawer` (the dialog and close labels, `pickerCloseLabel`, the three action labels, `headOfficeLabel`, and the three OPD status templates) and `branchPicker` (the line's label, `changeLabel`, and the picker's `menuTitle`, `menuHint` and `allLabel`).

## Footer

`src/components/Footer.jsx` follows the Function Health footer and is the only consumer of `src/styles/footer.css`. It renders on every route through `RootLayout`.

- The shape is the reference's: **one rounded card inset on a warmer ground**, with the fine print left outside the card on the ground itself. The card is `--ft-card` `#f8f7f3` and the ground `--ft-ground` `#e6e3db`; both are local to `.ft` rather than global tokens, because nothing else on the site uses this pair.
- It is **light, not the navy it used to be**. Three reasons, in order: `PatientStories` ends the home page on `--e-navy`, and two adjacent dark bands in slightly different navies read as a mistake rather than as a footer; the logo is a magenta-on-transparent wordmark that needed a white pill to survive on navy, which looked like a patch stuck to the page; and this is the densest block of small text on the site, where dark-on-light is the easier read for the older patients the site is built for.
- The inset above the card is `padding-top` on `.ft`, never `margin-top` on `.ft__card`. As a margin it collapsed straight out of the footer's box and the page background showed through as a white strip between the navy section above and the footer ground - visible on every page.
- **Every control in the footer is the header's 4px rectangle (`--ft-radius`), not the reference's pill.** The site's button is the small-radius rectangle - `.e-btn` is 3px, the cookie sheet 3px (the header's one pill is a documented departure, built to its reference) - and the footer's pills (primary, ghost, the two utility buttons and the social circles) were the one surface disagreeing with the button directly above them on every page: `Book Appointment` at the top of the page and `Book an appointment` at the bottom were two different shapes. The social marks are 42px squares on the shape of the header's menu button rather than circles for the same reason. The card keeps its 18-26px radius - it is a container, not a control. The landing hero's phone pill is the deliberate exception and is documented under Hero.
- The reference's app QR and store badges are dropped: there is no app. Its newsletter block is dropped too - there is no list to subscribe anyone to, and a form that posts nowhere is worse than no form. The action block that replaces it keeps that slot's position and typography (serif headline, italic accent from `--e-accent`, one line of support, then the buttons).
- The masthead is the wordmark plus a `Since 1993` tag, nothing else. A 25-word summary paragraph used to sit beside the logo and was removed; the tag keeps that row from reading as an empty band, and everything the paragraph said is on the page above it.
- The `Since 1993` tag is set in two parts, not one label: `SINCE` stays the tracked 0.72rem micro word the footer uses for contact labels, and the year is the serif italic accent (`--e-serif`, `--e-accent`), baseline-aligned beside it behind the hairline divider. As one uppercase micro string it read as a caption stuck to the logo; the serif year makes the pair a lockup and repeats the serif/italic pairing of the action headline and the hero accent. `splitEstablished()` reads the year off the end of `site.header.establishedLabel`, so the JSON stays one plain string and a label with no trailing year still renders whole. Measured with no overflow and one line at 320, 390 and 1440px.

**Bands, in DOM order, which is also the reading order at every width.** No `order` tricks - the visual order and the tab order are the same everywhere.

1. `.ft__masthead` - the logo and the `Since 1993` tag.
2. `.ft__reach` - the helpline, the email, and the reader's hospital's OPD number and hours (the one the header stored, followed through `BRANCH_CHANGE_EVENT` and named in each label: `Bharuch OPD`, `OPD hours · Bharuch`), with the emergency note under them behind an accent rule. **The phone rows are the header strip's rule**: where the chosen hospital answers its OPD on the emergency line (Visnagar) it is one row under `emergency.combinedLabel`; any other hospital gets `emergency.label` over the helpline and its own `<name> OPD` row. Before the deployment pass the footer always printed `OPD and emergency helpline` over Visnagar's number, beside `OPD hours · Bharuch` and under a header offering Bharuch's OPD - a number under two different names on one screen. The order is network-wide first (helpline, email), then the hospital (OPD, hours), so in two columns the second row is all one hospital and in one column its number and hours sit together. The strip is `auto-fit` with a `260px` minimum rather than a fixed three columns, because the email is 26 characters at 1.12rem: at 768px a hard three-column grid ran it straight into the `OPD HOURS` label in the next cell. With three rows it drops to two columns at about 830px of card width and to one on a phone; **with four (`data-count="4"`) a 40% floor caps it at two columns**, so the rows pair up 2x2 from about 667px rather than setting a fourth cell alone under three. The email may break after its `@` and nowhere else (`breakAfterAt()`, a `<wbr>`): on a 320px phone it is wider than its cell and ran past the card. These were first written into the action column and made it 420px taller than the link columns beside it, which left the bottom-left third of the card empty; as their own full-width band they fill the width, and on a phone the phone number lands second in the footer instead of below eighteen links.
3. `.ft__body` - three link columns plus the action block, which is explicitly placed in column 4 on desktop and falls to a full-width two-column band at <= 1080px.
4. `.ft__utility` - social pills on the left, `Privacy and cookie preferences` and `Back to top` on the right.
5. `.ft__fine` - the medical fine print and the copyright, outside the card.

**Content sources.** Nothing in the footer is hard-coded.

- `site.footer` holds `hospitalsTitle`, the `cta` block, `contactLabels` (`email`, `hours`), `emergencyNote`, `disclaimers`, `legalNote` and `copyright`. The phone rows' labels are the header's own (`site.header.emergency.label` / `combinedLabel`, `opdLabel`), so the top and the foot of the page name a number the same way.
- `navigation.footer` holds exactly the two link columns that are pages - `Treatments` and `Hospital`. The third column is the six branches, derived from `branches.json` and titled from `site.footer.hospitalsTitle`, so the branch list can never drift from the rest of the site. Every branch link goes through `buildBranchHref()` - `/branches/<slug>` for all six - and the header adopts the hospital from that path, so a footer branch link also sets the OPD number at the top of the next page and in the footer's own reach band. **`Book appointment` is not a column link**: it sat in `Hospital` directly under the action block's `Book an appointment` button, the same route twice, and left that column a row longer than the other two; without it all three are six rows and end level. **`All hospitals` (`/branches`) took the gallery's row** when that page was removed: the index was the one header destination the footer did not link, and it keeps `Hospital` at six rows, level with the other two (measured at 768-1440px, and side by side at 390).
- `Ask on WhatsApp` opens the reader's hospital's thread (`buildWhatsApp(chosenBranch)`), as the header's menu does; it used to open Visnagar's whichever hospital was chosen.
- **The social row shows only confirmed profiles** (`confirmedProfiles()` in `lib/contact.js`; an entry flagged `"unconfirmed": true` is a lead, shown nowhere and kept out of the JSON-LD): the network's own (`site.socialLinks`) once confirmed, else the reader's hospital's (`socialLinks` in `branches.json`), else the head office's - each square's `aria-label` names whose account it is. The marks are `components/SocialMark.jsx` (lucide-react 1.x removed its brand icons), keyed by `icon`; a link whose `icon` matches no mark renders nothing.

**Typography.** The column headings are sentence case at 0.98rem in `--e-tight`, not the 0.72rem tracked uppercase micro-label the rest of the site uses for section labels. Uppercase made them the smallest type in the footer, sitting directly above the links they title, so each column read as one undifferentiated block; at 0.98rem a reader can land on a heading. The micro uppercase style stays on the contact labels, where it labels a value rather than titling a list. Link rows are 0.98rem, contact values 1.12rem, and the fine print 0.84rem capped at `108ch` - it ran the full 1240px shell before, at about 150 characters a line.

- **Two things in the footer never break inside themselves**, both through `.ft__nobr`: the hours value (`9:00 AM - 6:00 PM`), which stranded `PM` alone on a line at 320-390 and 768px and split the range at 1024px, so the line now breaks after `Monday to Saturday,`; and any hyphenated word in the action headline, which `keepCompounds()` wraps so `check-up` never sets as `check-` / `up`. The headline also carries `text-wrap: balance`. Measured line by line with a per-character `Range` walk at 320-1920px. Each open day set is its own `.ft__hours` line (`display: block`), so a Saturday half day reads as two lines. **The label rule is `.ft__contact div > span`, not `.ft__contact span`** - the bare descendant selector caught the `.ft__nobr` span inside the value too and set the time range as a 0.71rem uppercase micro label on its own line, which is what the footer shipped with.
- `.ft__fine p` carries that `108ch` cap, and `.ft__copy` is a `p` inside `.ft__fine`, so the copyright rule needs `.ft__fine .ft__copy` to beat it. Written as a bare `.ft__copy` the cap won on specificity and the rule above the copyright stopped two thirds of the way across the page.
- Footer link labels are written to fit the two-column phone layout: `LASIK and laser`, `Eye check-up`, `Children's eyes`. The widest, `Cataract surgery`, is 125px, which two columns hold from 345px. Measured with `Range.getClientRects()`, no footer link wraps at any width from 320 to 2560px. If a label is lengthened, re-measure - a wrapped label knocks the two columns out of alignment row for row, which is what the shortening fixed.

**Breakpoints.** 1080px moves the action block out of column 4 into a full-width two-up band; 900px hides the `Head office` label; 860px stacks that band, because below it the copy column is under ~250px and the headline broke to four lines beside a half-empty button column; 720px drops the links to two columns; 344px drops them to one, below the 125px the widest label needs. It was 380px, from when the labels were longer, and a 360 or 375 phone gave up about 310px of screen to a single column it did not need (2453px of footer at 360x640, now 2140).

**Contrast and targets are measured, not assumed.** On the card: body copy and link text `--e-ink-2` 7.3:1, headings and contact values `--e-ink` 17:1, the italic accent 6.0:1, the fine print 5.5:1 on the ground. Two things were fixed after measuring: the `Head office` label was `--e-ink-3` at 2.97:1 and moved to `--e-ink-2`, and control outlines needed their own token - `--ft-line` is the decorative hairline at 1.3:1 and is fine between bands, but WCAG 1.4.11 wants 3:1 on the boundary of a control, so pills and the ghost button use `--ft-edge` `#948f83` at 3.01:1. **Do not put `--ft-line` on a button, and do not lighten `--ft-edge` to match the rules.** Re-measured for the deployment pass (computed on the solid grounds): labels, links, lede, note and utility buttons 7.28:1, headings and values 16.98:1, the accent and the year 5.95:1, both action buttons 14.27:1, the fine print 5.52:1.

- **Every footer control is a 44px target at every width**: link rows `min-height: 44px` with 11px padding, the social squares and the utility buttons 44px, the logo link 44px, and the phone numbers and email keep their 26px line and gain a `::after` halo (`inset: -9px 0`). They were 44px only on phones and touch screens until the deployment pass; a desktop reader got 40px rows, 42px squares and 28px number lines, and the rest of the site holds 44px at every width.
- **Hover is for a real pointer** (`(hover: hover) and (pointer: fine)`), and a finger gets `:active` (a navy tint on the outlined controls, the accent on links). The footer outlives the route, so a tapped link stayed in the accent - or a social square filled navy - on the page it opened, for the rest of the visit.
- **The action pair and the utility pair are each one width**: `flex: 1 1 0` with `min-width: max-content`, so side by side they share the row equally where they fit and stack full width where they do not (the SE held sideways is the one place a label sets a floor: 247 / 223px). In the 861-1080px two-up band the actions are two equal grid tracks (236 / 236), because as flex items in a content-sized column they hugged their labels. The old `flex: 1 1 210px` squeezed `Book an appointment` onto two lines at 568x320.
- **Below 721px the two utility buttons fill the row** (`.ft__legal` full width): one above the other where both labels do not fit (every phone), side by side where they do (600px).
- **The column headings are `h2`**, beside the action block's `h2`: as `h3` they sat under `Need an eye check-up or a second opinion?` in the heading outline, which they are not part of.
- **`Back to top` from the keyboard also takes focus to the top** (the skip link, when the press has `detail` 0 - Enter, Space or a screen reader), so the next Tab starts at the top of the page rather than past the footer. A mouse or a tap only scrolls, so the skip link never flashes for a pointer.

- `Head office` is hidden at <= 900px, where either two branches share a row or the column is about 210px, and the label wrapped the Visnagar row two lines taller than the other five. The branches page carries the same fact.
- `Back to top` honours `prefers-reduced-motion`: an instant jump rather than a smooth scroll across a 6000px page.
- **Measured for the deployment pass** with Bharuch (four rows) and Visnagar (three) chosen, at 320x568, 340x640, 345x640, 360x640, 375x553, 390x664, 393x659, 412x915, 430x746, 568x320, 667x375, 740x360, 844x390, 600x960, 721x900, 768x1024, 820x1180, 861x900, 900x1000, 1024x768, 1080x800, 1081x800, 1180x800, 1280x800, 1366x657, 1440x900, 1920x1080 and 2560x1440, on the dev server and the production build: no overflow, no clipped text, no link or button label over one line, both link columns level row for row, the hours never stranding `PM`, and every control hit-testing to itself over a 44px square with `elementFromPoint`. Real touch at 320x568, 390x664 and 844x390 (27 controls each): both numbers dial the right hospital, the email opens mail, WhatsApp opens the chosen hospital, every link and square opens its own href, the privacy button opens the cookie dialog, `Back to top` returns to 0, and a link tapped then scrolled on the page it opened is back to `--e-ink-2`. Choosing Bharuch on the contact switchboard turns the footer to four rows and repoints WhatsApp at once. Checked in Safari 27 at 320, 390, 768, 1024 and 1440px through a self-reporting page: the 2x2 grid, the email's break, the equal buttons and the 44px rows match Chrome.

## Cookie consent

`src/components/CookieConsent.jsx` renders on every route through `RootLayout` and is the only consumer of `src/styles/cookie.css`. **It is not in the prerendered page**: the choice lives in the reader's cookie, so the banner and the dialog mount as React adopts the page (`useHydrated()`), which is when a page built fresh mounted them. It is two surfaces and one state machine: a **banner** on a first visit, and a **dialog** opened from the banner's `Choose cookies` or from the footer's `Privacy and cookie preferences` link, which dispatches `aakash:open-cookie-preferences`. The cookie itself is read and written by `src/lib/consent.js` (`readConsent`, `writeConsent`, `allowsCategory`), so anything that stores something optional can ask first without importing the component.

**The choice is honoured, not just recorded.** Remembering the reader's hospital between visits is the `experience` category: `storeBranch()` in `src/lib/contact.js` keeps it in `localStorage` only when `allowsCategory("experience")` is true and in `sessionStorage` otherwise, so until the reader says yes it lasts the visit and is gone when the tab closes. `readStoredBranchSlug()` reads either, so the header, the service page, the contact page and the booking flow never need to know which. `applyBranchConsent()` moves the stored value to the storage the choice allows - on every load (a hospital remembered before this existed moves to the session) and on every save. `aakash_intro_seen` is session-only interface state and is listed under Necessary. No analytics script exists, so that switch gates nothing yet; the copy says so.

- **Every optional category starts off.** `noOptional` is both the initial state of the dialog and exactly what `Reject optional` stores, and it is written out key by key rather than as a bare `{ necessary: true }` - `normalize()` fills missing keys from that same object, so a short payload would have quietly re-enabled a category the reader had just refused. A pre-ticked switch is consent nobody gave; do not default one to on.
- **`Accept all` and `Reject optional` carry the same weight** - both the filled navy button, GOV.UK's pattern. Refusing is never the quieter or harder choice. `Choose cookies` is the text link after them.
- The choice is stored in the `aakash_cookie_preferences` cookie for 180 days (`SameSite=Lax`, `Secure` on https) and read once through a lazy `useState` initialiser, not in an effect - `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` rejects the effect version.
- On a first visit the banner waits for the opening curtain's `INTRO_DONE_EVENT` and then `INTRO_EXIT_MS + 500ms`, so it is never slid in behind the curtain; a visit with no curtain (the intro already seen this session) gets it after `BANNER_DELAY_MS` (600ms).
- **The banner is first in the tab order**: `RootLayout` renders `CookieConsent` before the skip link (GOV.UK's order), so a keyboard reader meets the choice before the page rather than after the footer. It is `position: fixed`, so nothing moves visually.
- Its `z-index` is **190, deliberately under the header (200) and the mobile drawer (210)**, which carries Book Appointment and the call actions in its thumb zone. It sits above the sticky service and hospital action bars (4). The dialog takes the screen at 260.

**Where it sits.**

- **Desktop and tablet: a 480px card in the bottom-right corner** (456px below 1024px). Never bottom left: the home hero sets its headline and `Book appointment` in the lower left at every desktop width, and the card that used to sit there covered `Advanced eye care.` the moment the curtain lifted. Measured against the hero's text (per line, not its box): headline and button clear at every width from 744 (iPad mini) to 2560px; it covers the decorative `06` / `09` figures and, from 1180px, the film's pause control, until the reader chooses. 721-743px - no real device - clips the button's edge.
- **Phone (<= 720px): a bottom sheet** with the heading beside the glyph, the short sentence (`bodyShort`) across the full width, the two decisions sharing a row and `Choose cookies` under them. 261px at 390x664 (Safari's visible height), 259px at 320x568, 238px at 430x746. Below 360px the glyph and title step down so the heading keeps one line.
- **A phone held sideways**: from 654px wide the words take the left and the decisions stack on the right (143px at 667x375, 126px at 844x390, a bar across the width from 721px); below that - the SE sideways - the sentence runs the width and all three actions share one row (178px at 568x320).
- The dialog is a centred 620px card, a bottom sheet at <= 720px. Its `max-height` is `svh` with a `vh` fallback: Safari's `vh` is the height with its toolbars hidden, so a `92vh` sheet ran under the address bar on an iPhone. Its list scrolls inside and **fades at its foot only while there is more** (`data-more` from a scroll listener and a `ResizeObserver`, the phone menu's device). Its foot is Save alone then the two blanket choices below 560px, three in a row above.

**How it moves.** Everything is `transform` and `opacity`, and every exit finishes inside `LEAVE_MS` (320ms) before the surface goes.

- The banner arrives as a sheet sliding up from the screen's edge (phone, 520ms on `--e-ease`) or a card rising 20px (wider), and its heading, sentence and actions come up 70ms apart behind it.
- **The dialog rises over the banner rather than replacing it.** The banner stays mounted under it (`inert` while the dialog traps focus), so opening never shows an empty frame between the two, and closing without a choice uncovers the banner that was there all along; on a wide screen the corner card fades out under the scrim (`data-covered`) and returns when the dialog closes. A choice sends everything on screen away together (`leaving === "all"`): the phone's two sheets drop at their own heights, so the taller dialog never exposes the banner beneath it.
- **The dialog is always mounted and hidden** (`data-state="closed"`, `visibility: hidden`, `inert`), like the phone menu: building it on the tap cost 34ms at 4x CPU before the sheet could start to rise. Its entrances are keyed on `[data-state="open"]`, never set on the elements, so each opening replays them. Measured on the production build at 390x664, tap to the sheet moving: 47ms at 4x (63ms when it mounted on the tap) with no frame over 17ms, 55-64ms at 6x.
- The categories arrive 60ms apart; the switch knob slides; `What this stores` is a native `<details>` that glides open through `::details-content` where supported (Chrome) and snaps where not (Safari 27), the FAQ's device.
- Under `prefers-reduced-motion` every surface is simply there and simply goes - `leave()` skips the exit, and the entrances carry an explicit `animation: none` because `global.css`'s rule zeroes durations but not delays. 0 running animations, measured.

**The rest.**

- The dialog is the only surface that locks the page (`body.ck-locked`, a class, the gallery viewer's rule), traps `Tab` (the `FOCUSABLE` list includes `summary`), answers `Escape` and returns focus to whatever opened it - the footer link, or `Choose cookies` once the banner stops being inert. The listeners read the latest handlers through `useEffectEvent`, so they subscribe once per opening and a toggle never pulls focus back to Close.
- A polite `role="status"` line announces `site.cookies.savedMessage` after any choice, because the surface that confirmed it has just gone.
- Category switches are `role="switch"` buttons labelled by the category title; 54x32 with a `::after` stretching the pressable area to 44px. `Necessary` carries **`Always on` as a status, not a pill**: navy micro caps led by the success-green dot, no border or fill for a finger to mistake for a button - the journey page's tag lesson. The categories carry **no icon**: three glyphs in wells were one device repeated per item, and they pushed `Website experience` onto two lines at 320px.
- **Hover plates are for a real pointer** (`(hover: hover) and (pointer: fine)`), a finger gets `:active`, and a button presses to 0.98. A tap left the disclosure accent-coloured for the rest of the dialog.
- Contrast: title 18.2:1, text 7.1-7.8:1, eyebrow 6.4:1, both banner buttons 14.3:1, `Always on` 13.0:1 and its dot 5.9:1 on the Necessary card, `What this stores` 7.1-7.8:1. Control boundaries need `--ck-edge` (`#7f8b96`, the header's edge value): 3.48:1 on the card and 3.16:1 on the footer band. `--ck-rule` is the 1.2:1 hairline and belongs between bands only - **do not put it on a button, and do not lighten `--ck-edge`**; `#949aa1` measured 2.58:1 on that band.
- All copy lives in `site.cookies` (`banner`, `dialog`, `categories`, `savedMessage`). The category `items` name what is really stored and where - `aakash_cookie_preferences`, `aakash_selected_branch` (for the visit under Necessary, between visits under Website experience), `aakash_intro_seen` - so a new stored value means a new line there.
- **Measured for the deployment pass** at 320x568, 360x640, 375x553, 390x664, 393x659, 430x746, 568x320, 653x360, 667x375, 740x360, 844x390, 721x900, 744x1133, 768x1024, 820x1180, 834x1194, 900x1000, 1023x768, 1024x768, 1180x800, 1280x800, 1366x657, 1440x900, 1920x1080 and 2560x1440: no overflow, no target under 44px (hit-tested over a 44px square), no button label or heading over one line, the dialog on screen at every size. Real touch at 320x568, 390x664 and 844x390 (27 checks each): every control lands on itself and does what it says, including the hospital moving between storages with the choice, a drag over the list scrolling the list and leaving the page, the scrim closing the dialog, and focus returning to the footer link. Keyboard: the banner first, then the skip link; Enter opens the dialog on Close; Tab wraps; Space and Enter toggle the disclosures and switches; Escape stores nothing and returns to `Choose cookies`. Frame by frame at 4x throttle: no empty frame at the screen's foot through the handoff, 17ms frames through arrival and exits. Checked in Safari 27 at 390 and 1024px through a self-reporting page: the arrival, the handoff, the exits, the fade, `inert` and the lock match Chrome.

## UI/UX Standards

- Mobile first: design and test from small screens upward.
- Keep navigation short, obvious, and thumb-friendly.
- Primary actions should be visible and direct: Book Appointment, Call, WhatsApp, Directions.
- Use large tap targets, readable type, strong contrast, and enough spacing for older patients.
- Keep sections purposeful. If a section does not help a patient decide, contact, book, or understand care, simplify or remove it.
- Avoid visual clutter, nested cards, decorative overload, and heavy animations.
- Use real hospital, doctor, branch, and treatment imagery where possible. Avoid generic stock-like visuals when real assets exist.
- Make branch selection easy on mobile; users should quickly choose Visnagar, Ahmedabad, or Bharuch.
- Use icons from Lucide React for actions and navigation cues.
- Preserve accessibility: semantic landmarks, labels, focus states, keyboard access, reduced-motion support, and alt text.
- Ensure every UI works at small mobile widths, tablet widths, laptop widths, and large desktop widths.

## Visual Direction

- The site should feel premium, clinical, clean, and trustworthy.
- Prefer calm medical colors from the existing theme over random new palettes.
- Keep typography readable and polished. Avoid text that is too small, too dense, or squeezed into cards.
- Use motion sparingly for reveal and orientation, not as decoration.
- Keep mobile pages direct and vertically efficient.
- Avoid unnecessary hero complexity. The homepage should get users to care options and contact routes quickly.

## Content And Data Rules

- `src/data/site.json`: brand (`logo` is the SVG the site renders, `logoLight` its white-wordmark twin for the header on the home film, `logoRaster` the PNG structured data and social cards need), the header block (labels, `bookLabel` / `bookShortLabel`, `menuLabel`, `opdLabel`, the emergency line with the `combinedLabel` the rail prints when a hospital answers its OPD on that number, the branch picker's strings, and the phone menu's `drawer` labels and OPD status templates), global CTAs, defaults, `openingHours` (the time zone every hospital's clock runs on, and the default `schedule` / `closedNote` / `note` a hospital without its own `hours` block inherits - there are no display strings; the printed lines are derived from the schedule), social links, and the whole `footer` block (action-block copy, contact labels, emergency note, medical fine print, copyright). It also holds `defaultSeo` (the fallback head; `url` is the canonical host every absolute URL reads), `verification` (`google` / `bing` ownership tokens, emitted as meta tags only when filled), and in `brand` the raster logo's pixel size (`logoRasterWidth` / `logoRasterHeight`), `languages` (the consultation languages the JSON-LD states) and `alternateNames` ("Akash Eye Hospital", the common misspelling). `socialLinks` entries flagged `"unconfirmed": true` are leads, shown nowhere.
- `src/data/navigation.json`: header navigation (an item with `"drawerGroup": "more"` is set small in the phone menu, under the large links), and the two page-link columns the footer renders. The footer's third column is the branches and is derived from `branches.json`.
- `src/data/home.json`: homepage hero (the chip's `eyebrow`, the one-line `title` / `titleAccent`, the two-line `subtitle`, `ctaLabel`, the stats and the film), impact (the cards, and the phone card's `photoTag` / `photoTagNoYear` / `nearestLabel`), conditions (the picker's labels, the phone index's `findPrompt` / `findLabel`, and its eleven service-keyed `items`, each a slug and one `headline`), trust, doctor-highlight and patient-voices copy. `cta` used to sit here for `CTASection`; both went with the doctors page rebuild, which was that section's last consumer. `timeline` and `missionVision` used to live here and are now `about.json`'s `journey.milestones` and `vision.statements`.
- `src/data/about.json`: the two About pages. `nextLabel` is the eyebrow on the closing plate both pages share. `journey` holds the founding date (`establishedCaption` is the stamp's label, `establishedLabel` the date under it), the milestone list (each with its own `image` and `alt`), the title-card and year-rail labels, the scroll cue, the closing `now` block and the page-level `ctaLabel` / `ctaLabelShort` pair the plate uses; `vision` holds the mission and vision statements and the core values, plus its own `ctaLabel` / `ctaLabelShort`.
- `src/data/services.json`: the services index page's own copy under `page` (opening, category list, the finder's concern list - each concern with its `label` and the `shortLabel` the phone's options show, each mode's `question` and each group's `shortLabel` for the phone's route heading and group tabs, and the phone results header's strings in `finder.status` (`allTitle`, `matchTitle`, `because`, `countOf`, `showAll`, `showAllShort`, `kindCount`, `kindCountOne`) - index head, the six pathway steps and the emergency band), the service detail page's labels under `page.detail` (the three tab names and the short forms a phone's segmented control shows in `tabsShort`, the overview caption's `areaLabel`, the visit with its pointer / touch `hint` pair, questions, related, and the emergency page's own band heading in `urgent`), the finder's `sceneHint` / `sceneHintTouch` pair, the eye model's `modelHint` / `modelHintTouch` pair (a service page's eye) and `modelHintChoose` / `modelHintChooseTouch` pair (the index's, which also says a part can be tapped), plus the eleven services under `items`, each with its `category`, `longDescription`, `causes`, `symptoms` (patient-language signs, each naming one of the chart effects `SymptomScene` draws), `featureBullets`, `faq`, its two pictures (`image` for the detail page and the pane, `thumb` for the tablet row and the phone's answer cards), and the `shortTitle` the phone's card grid shows; `page.index` also carries the grid's `cardLabel` / `cardLabelShort` / `urgentLabel`. There is no `icon` field: nothing reads one, and `src/lib/icons.js` registers only the glyphs the trust tiles and the two statements name. Every word a service page shows is either one of that service's own fields in `items` or a label in `page` - nothing on it is written twice. Each item also carries `schemaType` (its JSON-LD type: `SurgicalProcedure`, `MedicalProcedure`, `DiagnosticProcedure` or `MedicalTherapy`) and, once a doctor has actually reviewed the page, `review: { by, on }` (emitted as `reviewedBy` / `lastReviewed`; never add it otherwise). `page.detail.visit.placesLabel` names the visit section's hospital list.
- `src/data/doctors.json`: the doctors page's own copy under `page` (head, the filter's labels, the three runs with the visiting run's `note`, the card's place templates under `card`, the readout templates and the closing line), then the team: each record's `name`, `qualifications`, `specialty`, optional `interests`, optional `photo`, `branches` and optional `visiting` (a hospital and, where known, a `schedule` in the OPD `hours` shape). A hospital is named by its display name, not its slug. See the Doctors section for how each field is shown. The nineteen doctors also carry `slug` (their page's URL, stored so it never moves) and `services` (the treatments their page lists, from their own interests and specialty), and `page.profile` holds the doctor page's labels and head templates.
- `src/data/branches.json`: branch names, slugs, addresses, phone groups, email, maps, WhatsApp numbers, each hospital's own `hours` block (a `schedule` of day-set slots with `opens` / `closes`, the `closedNote` for the days in no slot, and the `note` under the hours on its page - see `src/data/README.md`), and each hospital's `map` point on the index's drawn map (a share of the 660x492 sketch plus the side its label sits on). The top-level `index` block holds the index page's own copy (head, row labels, the note under the list). The top-level `page` block holds the labels every hospital page shares - including the locate card's three tab names, the gallery's `openLabel` / `showLabel` / `closeLabel` / `viewerLabel`, and the short button labels the phone swaps in - and a branch item's own `page` block (all six carry one) holds that hospital's page copy, photographs, landmarks and services, and is what gives it a page at all - including its own gallery sentence (`insideLede`) once it has its own photographs, and a `portrait` flag on any gallery entry that is taller than it is wide. Anywhere a phone shows shorter copy, the short version is authored here (`ledeShort`, `mapsShortLabel`, `copyShortLabel`, `bookShortLabel`); nothing on this page is truncated in CSS. Each hospital also carries `postalAddress` (street and locality for the JSON-LD; the PIN stays `page.postcode` - keep both in step with `address`), optional `socialLinks` (its own accounts; `"unconfirmed": true` hides one), and a phone group flagged `"unconfirmed": true` (a placeholder number) is kept out of the JSON-LD. `page.locate.followLabel` heads the social row.
- `src/data/contact.json`: the contact page - its `seo`, the head, the switchboard's labels and WhatsApp message (`desk`), and the directory's labels (`directory`). Every number, address and email on the page comes from `branches.json` and `site.header.emergency`; nothing here is a phone number.
- `src/data/appointment.json`: the appointment page - its `seo`, the head, the five `steps` (label, title, lede), every field's labels, hints and error strings (`fields`, including the three `daypart` options with their hour ranges and the `unsure` reason), the foot buttons (`nav`), the slip's labels and stamps (`slip`), the receipt shown after Send (`after`), and the WhatsApp message templates (`message` - a heading, one line template per field, and the closing line). Hospitals and treatments come from `branches.json` and `services.json`; nothing here names one.
- `src/data/testimonials.json`: patient-voice themes, rendered by the `PatientStories` wall on the home page - each a `branch` slug, a `theme` and a `quote`, in reading order (one theme per hospital in each half); its `notes` record which themes are sourced and which are drafted.
- `src/data/site.json` also holds `notFound`: the not-found page's `seo`, its head (`label`, `title`, `titleAccent`, `lede`, `triedLabel`), the two buttons (`bookLabel`, `homeLabel` / `homeShortLabel`), the guesses' headings, kind labels and `doctorDetail` template (`matches`), the ways' two headings (`ways`), and `pages` - the site pages a lost reader can be sent to, each with the `keywords` an address is matched against, `way: true` for the four always offered, and a `description` with `{services}` / `{specialists}` / `{hospitals}` / `{names}` filled from the data. A page's title is the header's label for it (`navigation.json`); only `/appointment` carries its own. The emergency row reuses `contact.json`'s `directory.emergencyLabel` / `emergencyNote`.
- `src/data/site.json` also holds `cookies`: the consent banner copy, the preferences dialog copy, the three cookie categories with what each one stores and where (no `icon` - the categories carry none), and the `savedMessage` announced after a choice.
- `src/data/redirects.json`: the permanent (301) redirect map - every legacy URL of the 2018 PHP site and every live URL that has moved - read by `scripts/prerender.mjs` into each host's rules. Path only (query strings are dropped). Add a line whenever a page moves; never delete one.
- Do not hard-code business content into components when it belongs in JSON.
- Keep branch slugs stable because URLs, query links, and future branch pages depend on them.

## SEO, Trust, And Medical Site Requirements

- **Read `docs/SEO.md` before touching anything here.** It is the SEO handbook: the prerender, the head, the JSON-LD graph, the redirect map, the keyword ownership, the per-hospital local SEO and the open items.
- Every route renders `SEO` with its data's `seo` block, and one `*JsonLd` from `components/JsonLd.jsx` (a single `@graph` per page built by `lib/schema.js`). Never add a second `<script type="application/ld+json">`, and never mark up anything the page does not show - no ratings, prices, awards or unconfirmed phones or profiles.
- Keep copy medically responsible. Do not make unrealistic guarantees or exaggerated health claims.
- Make phone numbers, WhatsApp links, addresses, maps, and appointment CTAs easy to find.
- `sitemap.xml` and `robots.txt` are generated on every build from `getPages()` in `src/entry-server.jsx` - there are no hand-written copies. A moved or removed page gets a line in `src/data/redirects.json`; never delete a line from it.
- Verify branch contact details, business hours, maps, and doctor bios before launch.

## Performance Rules

- Keep route-level lazy loading through `src/router.jsx`.
- Use `SmartImage` and public optimized images when possible.
- Use `LazyMapFrame` for embedded maps.
- Avoid unnecessary large libraries.
- Avoid adding complex client-side state when simple JSON-driven rendering is enough.
- After major UI changes, run a production build and check bundle output.

## Quality Checklist Before Handoff

Run:

```bash
npm run lint
npm run build
```

Manual checks:

- Homepage looks clean and direct on mobile.
- Header menu works on desktop and mobile.
- `Our Hospitals` dropdown works and branch choices open the expected branch state.
- The hero's `Book appointment` is above the fold on every phone, and its headline is one line from 360px up.
- The booking flow validates each step as it is left, and `Send on WhatsApp` opens the chosen hospital's line with the message the slip shows.
- Phone, WhatsApp, maps, and email links work.
- All pages fit mobile width without horizontal scrolling.
- Reduced-motion mode does not depend on animation to understand content.
- Lighthouse mobile should be checked for Performance, Accessibility, Best Practices, and SEO before final deployment.

## Known Launch Notes
