- **The hosting platform is not chosen, and the launch depends on two host-level jobs** (`docs/hosting.md`): every host and protocol must 301 to `https://www.aakasheyehospital.com`, and unknown paths must get `404.html` with a 404 status. The build already writes the rules for Cloudflare, Netlify and Apache/LiteSpeed. Verify the domain in Search Console by DNS, submit the sitemap, and follow the 30-day checklist there.
- **Two other hospital websites compete with the new one**: `aehbharuch.com` (an unfinished Bharuch template with "Doctor Name" placeholders and the same NAP) and `blog.aakasheyehospital.com` (four 2021 posts). Redirect both at the host level once the hospital confirms it owns them; the per-post targets are in `docs/hosting.md`.
- **The home film is AI-generated** (its frames carried a generator watermark that had to be cropped out), but its accessible description (`home.hero.video.description`) calls it "the hospital building, the main atrium reception and an examination room". Replace the film with real footage, or reword the description so it claims nothing the film cannot back. This is a trust problem on a medical site, not a ranking one.
- **React now adopts the prerendered page, and what is left of PageSpeed's slow LCP is the app's script, not the paint** (Search and prerendering). Lighthouse's default mobile run on a gzipping local host, old build against new, two runs each: Chrome's observed LCP went from 2.1-2.3s (React's copy) to 67-107ms (the first paint) on contact, doctors, services, cataract, Visnagar, booking and journey, and home stays the hero poster arriving (93-202ms in both); CLS 0 everywhere; total blocking time lower on six of eight pages (Visnagar 42 to 7ms, cataract 22 to 5ms; booking 4 to 16ms). The observed LCP is what real visitors' Chrome reports to Google. **PageSpeed's simulated LCP moved less** - Visnagar 6.5 to 5.5s, cataract 7.0 to 6.0s, doctors 5.5 to 5.0s, the rest level (home 6.1-6.3s, contact 4.4s, services 5.0s, booking 4.6s, journey 4.5s) - because Lighthouse's simulation counts every request that _starts_ before the observed LCP as blocking it, and the app's module script starts with the HTML. **Starting the app's script after the first paint was built and measured, and rejected - do not redo it.** An inline loader in the prerender that added the module script once the document was parsed and the next frame had painted made the first paint 0.09-0.36s earlier on a slow line (4x CPU, 1.6Mbps, median of three, eight page and visit combinations), but React took the page over 0.04-0.37s later on every one, so the reload veil and the curtain lifted 0.07-0.34s later (doctors' veil 3.71 to 4.03s, contact's curtain 3.40 to 3.71s). A reader waiting longer to use the page is the wrong trade for a lab figure; the field LCP, which Google uses, is already the first paint.
- **Bharuch's PIN is not on the site.** A public map listing gives `392001`; confirm it with the hospital and add `page.postcode`. Its landline is stored as `(026)-42242359` (STD code `02642`) - confirm the display form.

- Ahmedabad OPD mobile number must be verified before launch.
- **Gota, Himmatnagar and Juhapura still carry placeholder numbers** (`+91-900-000-0001` to `0006`, and the matching `whatsappNumber`s) in `branches.json`. A reader who chooses one of them in the header gets Call OPD and WhatsApp actions - in the header, the menu and the footer - pointing at numbers that may belong to someone else. **This is a patient-privacy blocker, not only a broken link**: the booking flow addresses the patient's name, mobile number and reason for the visit to that hospital's `whatsappNumber`, so a request for Gota, Himmatnagar or Juhapura would go to a stranger's WhatsApp. Himmatnagar's OPD number is now real, but its `whatsappNumber` is still the placeholder `919000000003`. Replace all of them with the real desk numbers before launch; nothing in the code changes.
- Map coordinates are approximate and should be verified.
- Business hours are placeholders and should be confirmed by the hospital team.
- **The team is the hospital's own list (October 2026) and was applied as supplied, with these calls made on the way - confirm them:** Dr. Asesh B. Patel (Visnagar) is taken to be the PDF's Dr. Ashesh Patel (Bharuch), and keeps the old portrait (`ashesh-patel.jpg`); Kashyap M. Patel, Bhavika R. Patel and A. K. Patel (Visnagar optometrists) and Dr. Mahendra M. Patel (Bharuch) are not on the list and were removed with their portraits (in git history if they belong back) - **Visnagar's list names no optometrists at all**, so its page has no optometrist line; Dr. Pooja Yadav Patel is listed at Bharuch and as visiting Visnagar, so she is treated as based at Bharuch; Dr. Ronak Patel's registration number (G-35206) is not shown, since no other doctor's is; Jaimin Modi's "PhD scholar, Shri C H Nagri Eye Hospital" is shortened to `PhD Scholar`; and the specialty of a doctor the list gave no title is `Ophthalmic Surgeon` where their areas of interest are surgical and `Ophthalmologist` otherwise. Only Dr. Dhaivat Vasavada's visiting days (Visnagar, Tuesday and Saturday, 10 AM - 5 PM) were given; add a `schedule` to any other `visiting` entry when the hospital confirms the days.
- **The squint service's photograph is wrong.** `public/assets/media/services/squint-services.jpg` (and its thumbnail) is a composite medical infographic of a cataract (`Cloudy lens`) and glaucoma - nothing about squint - and it shows wherever squint does: the home page's picker on every device, the services index and `/services/squint-services`. No suitable photograph is in the project. Replace it with the hospital's own photograph of a squint or orthoptic examination (or a child at the synoptophore) and re-cut the thumbnail with the recipe in the ServiceExplorer section.
- The phone index's way on (`home.conditions.findPrompt` / `findLabel`, `Not sure which one?` / `Find it by what you notice`) is drafted and needs sign-off with the rest of the home copy.
- **Eight of the twelve patient-voice themes are drafted, not sourced**: Ahmedabad `Clear costs`, Bharuch `Follow-up care`, and both themes for Gota, Himmatnagar and Juhapura. No public review text for Himmatnagar or Juhapura could be found, and Gota shows a Justdial rating but no readable reviews. Replace them with themes from each hospital's own Google reviews (the card needs only a `branch`, a `theme` and a quote of 16-21 words), and have the hospital sign off all twelve - `Glasses same visit` also claims Juhapura's optical counter makes up a prescription on the day.
- The journey timeline's six photographs are the site's existing curated stock, cropped into `public/assets/media/journey`; none of them shows the hospital it describes. Replace them with the hospital's own photography before launch - it is the single biggest upgrade available to that page.
- The journey timeline carries the five milestones the hospital has confirmed (1993, 1995, 2009, 2011, 2014). The opening years for Bharuch, Gota, Himmatnagar and Juhapura, and the year bladeless laser surgery started, are not in any data file - get them from the hospital and add them to `about.json` rather than estimating.
- The mission and vision on `/about/vision` are the hospital's own approved wording, quoted verbatim. The six core values are derived from those two sentences but the value wording itself is drafted and needs hospital sign-off.
- The footer's medical fine print (`site.footer.disclaimers`, `emergencyNote`, `legalNote`) is drafted, not approved. It needs the hospital's sign-off, and `legalNote` in particular should be checked against the hospital's registration details before launch.
- **Social profiles are confirmed only where research could verify them** (October 2026): Visnagar's Instagram `@aeh.visnagar` and Facebook `eyehospitalvisnagar`, and Ahmedabad's Facebook `AakashEyeHospitalOdhav`, in `branches.json`. The network-level leads in `site.json` (`facebook.com/AakashEyeCareHospital`, `instagram.com/aakash_eyehospital`, `x.com/aakashehospital`) are flagged `unconfirmed` and appear nowhere. The owner says every hospital has its own Instagram - get the other five, confirm the three above, and remove a flag to publish.
- **Only 5 of the 25 people have a portrait** (Vishnu S. Patel, Sanjay B. Patel, Saurabh Kapoor, Dhaivat Vasavada, Ashesh Patel); the rest show a monogram. Those five are 270x260 and inconsistently framed. Photograph the whole team at ~1200x1500 on a consistent background before launch, save each as `public/assets/media/doctors/<first-last>.jpg` and add `photo` to its record - nothing else changes.
- **The site now renders the hospital's own vector logo** (`public/assets/logo.svg`; see Opening curtain). It is a two-line lockup and carries no `YOUR EYES IN SAFE HAND` tagline, which the raster did - **confirm with the hospital that the vector lockup is the approved mark to use without that line**, since the tagline now appears only as set type on the curtain and in the footer. `logo.png` stays for structured data and social cards, and the browser icons are the emblem alone: `public/assets/icon-192.png`, `icon-512.png` and `apple-touch-icon.png` (180px) are the blue square cut from the raster master's left 871x854 (padded to square with its own blue, `#0c519c`), quantised to 64 colours at 4-21KB. Re-cut from the master, never from `logo.png`. **The vector's `EYE HOSPITAL` is 2.6:1 on navy**, so if a dark surface ever needs the logo, ask the hospital for a reversed (white) lockup rather than recolouring this one.
- The cookie consent is wired: the remembered hospital persists between visits only once `Website experience` is allowed, and lasts the visit otherwise. **Have the hospital check the wording in `site.cookies` against its privacy policy** - including the Necessary line about security and page-delivery cookies, which assumes what the host sets. No analytics script exists, so the Analytics switch gates nothing yet; if one is added, load it only when `allowsCategory("analytics")` is true and add its cookies to that category's `items`.
- **Every `faq` on every service was rewritten and none of it is approved.** Six or seven questions each, 69 in total, in patient language. They deliberately state no price, no duration in days, no success rate and no guarantee - anything that depends on the eye in front of the doctor says so. **This is clinical copy and needs a doctor's sign-off, not a marketing review.** Two content bugs were fixed on the way: six answers shipped the words _"legacy content"_ / _"the legacy site"_ - migration notes from whoever moved the old site, showing to patients - and one told readers the appointment form offers _"Visnagar, Ahmedabad or Bharuch"_ when there are six hospitals. Check for both if any of this copy is re-imported from the old site.
- **The `causes` and `symptoms` on every one of the eleven services are newly written and none of them is approved.** They are patient-language descriptions drafted from general ophthalmic practice - why a condition happens, and what someone might notice - and they are what the service detail page's `Why it happens` and `Signs to notice` tabs show. **This is clinical copy and needs a doctor's sign-off, not a marketing review.** The page says on its own face that the signs are a starting point and not a diagnosis; that note must stay.
- **Six of the eleven services are newly written and none of their copy is approved**: Oculoplasty, Pediatric Eye Care, Myopia Clinic, Squint Services, Optical Services and Emergency Eye Care. Their `shortDescription`, `longDescription`, `featureBullets` and `faq` are drafted from the design brief and general ophthalmic practice, **not** from anything the hospital has confirmed it offers. Confirm each service is actually provided, and at which branches, before launch.
- The five carried-over services kept their approved copy but were renamed and re-slugged. Check the new titles read the way the hospital describes them.
- `page.emergency` is clinical safety copy - the nine warning signs and the chemical-exposure first aid. **This is the highest-risk text on the site and needs a doctor's sign-off, not a marketing review.** The primary action dials `site.header.emergency.phone`; confirm that line is answered at the hours patients will call it.
- **The eye model on the services page and every service page is an anatomical illustration and has not been reviewed by a doctor.** It is drawn to the eye's proportions and as a left eye (disc nasal to the fovea, recti on the spiral of Tillaux, a brown iris), with the retina, choroid and sclera exaggerated so a section reads. Have an ophthalmologist look over each of the seven poses before launch - it is the one picture on the site a visiting eye doctor will study. Front window (now a profile, with the pin on the clear dome) and the optic nerve (now from the nose side) were re-angled in the deployment pass, and the part names now sit on the model beside the pin; include both in that review.
- **The eye model's device timing has been exercised on this Mac's GPU, in Safari 27 and on simulated slow GPUs, not on a real budget phone.** Before launch, open a service page on one inexpensive Android phone (a Redmi or a Galaxy A-series) and one older iPhone and scroll past the eye: it should turn smoothly and go still within about ten seconds, or show the drawing instead.
- The services page's `page.finder.concerns` are patient-language symptoms used to route a reader to a department. They are not clinical triage and need the hospital's sign-off - and so does each one's `shortLabel`, the form the pager shows below 901px (`Blurred or hazy vision`, `Less dependence on glasses`, `Child's number keeps rising`), which must say the same thing as its sentence.
- **The service detail page's new labels are drafted**: the short tab names (`Overview` / `Causes` / `Signs`), the visit hints (`Choose a step...` / `Swipe or tap a step...`), `Choose a sign...`, and the emergency page's band heading (`When to call` / `Signs that cannot wait.`), which sits over the doctor-pending emergency copy and needs the same doctor's sign-off.
- The six `page.pathway` steps describe how a visit works. Confirm the sequence with the hospital, and confirm that walk-in OPD is genuinely available at every branch if that claim is added back.
- Service photographs are used large on `/services/:slug` and in the index's sticky pane, and as 240px thumbnails in the index rows on tablets and on the phone's answer cards (`public/assets/media/services`). The six new services borrow existing stock images (LASIK, Squint and Emergency carry theirs as `public/assets/media/services/<slug>.jpg`, beside their thumbnails - they came from the removed gallery); replace them with the hospital's own photography of the relevant clinic when it exists, and re-cut the thumbnails with the recipe in the ServiceExplorer section. The eleven `shortTitle`s the phone's card grid shows are drafted and need the same sign-off as the titles.
- **All six hospital pages are live and none of their copy is approved.** Every `lede`, `ledeShort`, the four `facts` and the SEO text on all six are drafted from the address and the network's own data. **Every page lists all eleven services**, because no hospital has confirmed which treatments it actually runs - add a `page.services` array of slugs to a branch to narrow it. Confirm the copy hospital by hospital.
- **The hospital pages now carry the hospital's own photography, and the patients in it have not consented to being on a website.** Visnagar's waiting-hall, atrium and reception frames show identifiable patients and families at close range. **Confirm with the hospital that these may be published**, and if not, ask for the same rooms photographed empty - the crops and the code do not change. Visnagar's `reception.jpg` is already cropped to keep the nearest faces out; the others are not. The Odhav set was chosen to avoid the problem - the reception frame shows two patients from behind and the waiting area one person at a distance - and the Odhav frames that show faces close up (the waiting hall, a patient at the autorefractor) were left out. The Himmatnagar set was photographed before the hospital opened and shows no one.
- **Visnagar, Ahmedabad (Odhav), Juhapura (Sarkhej) and Himmatnagar have been photographed; Bharuch and Gota have not.** Those two pages borrow the Visnagar shoot - their hero is the waiting hall rather than the Visnagar facade so no page claims a building it cannot show, they carry no street photograph at all, and every alt line says `an Aakash Eye Hospital` rather than a city. This is still the single biggest upgrade available to those two pages and it needs nothing but the files. The Odhav and Sarkhej files are WhatsApp forwards at 1280-1600px, and Himmatnagar's facade is an AI upscale of a 720px WhatsApp frame, so none of those heroes can be encoded at 2000px until the camera originals arrive.
- **The Himmatnagar photographs carry a fact the site does not have yet.** The road signboard gives `+91 95120 41164` for appointments; the page still shows the placeholder number, on the user's instruction, until the hospital confirms which desk it is and whether it takes WhatsApp. (The facade's signboard names Dr. Ronak Patel, which the hospital's team list has since confirmed.) The gallery's `Recovery room` caption is also a reading of the photograph (a row of beds near the theatre); confirm what the room is called.
- **Juhapura's reception photograph shows the consultation-fee card on the wall** (a small Gujarati notice, `100` for a new case and `50` for a return visit, left of the emblem). It is unreadable at the stage size but legible in the full-screen viewer, and a price is not something this site states anywhere else. Confirm with the hospital that the fees may be shown, or ask for the reception photographed without the card - the crop and the code do not change.
- Four of the nine photographs supplied were not used: two are near-duplicates of the first-floor view, one frames a third-party medical store behind a row of close faces, and one is the optical counter under a printed `320/-` price banner - a price is not something this site states anywhere else. If the optical shop should be shown, ask for it photographed without the banner.
- Every hospital now has a team: Visnagar 6 consultants and 3 visiting specialists; Ahmedabad 4 consultants, 3 visiting specialists and a visiting optometrist; Bharuch 3 consultants and an optometrist; Gota 7 consultants and 2 optometrists; Himmatnagar 1 consultant and 2 optometrists; Juhapura 1 consultant.
- **`establishedYear` is set only where a year is confirmed**: Visnagar 1993 and Ahmedabad 2014, both from `about.json`'s milestones. The other four show no `Since` tag rather than an estimated one.
- **Bharuch's and Gota's addresses name no road**, so their drawn maps carry an unlabelled road line; Bharuch's carries no PIN either, because its address has none. Ask the hospital for both.
- The appointment flow's three dayparts (`Morning 9 AM - 12 PM`, `Afternoon 12 - 3 PM`, `Evening 3 - 6 PM` in `appointment.json`) are placeholders drawn from the displayed hours and need the hospital's confirmation, and they are the same three at every hospital even though each hospital's `hours` block can now differ - a hospital that opens at 10 still offers `Morning 9 AM - 12 PM`. Confirm the hours first, then either write the dayparts to fit all six or move them into the branch. (The old `20:00` / `6:00 PM` disagreement is gone: the structured block is the only copy and it is settled on `18:00`.)
- **The pages tell a reader `OPD open now` or `OPD closed now` from each hospital's `hours` block, and the six blocks now differ** (Mon-Sat everywhere; Visnagar 08:00-20:00, Ahmedabad 09:00-20:00, Bharuch 09:00-19:00, Himmatnagar 10:00-20:00, Gota and Juhapura 09:00-18:00) - confirm these are the hospital's real hours, because they are a claim a patient may act on, so those hours need the hospital's confirmation before launch more urgently than they did when they were only a printed line, and the closed-day note (`Emergency and prior appointments only`) needs the same. The structure is ready for six different answers - a Saturday half day is a second slot in that hospital's `schedule` - so get the real hours hospital by hospital and write each block; nothing in the code changes when they land.

## Coding Style

- Keep changes minimal, focused, and consistent with existing patterns.
- Do not introduce new abstractions unless they remove real duplication or complexity.
- Prefer clear component names and readable JSX.
- Avoid one-letter variables.
- Avoid comments unless the code would otherwise be hard to understand.
- Use ASCII text unless the file already requires non-ASCII.
- Do not commit changes unless explicitly requested.
